// () is the empty construction (spec 11.1.1, 11.1.5.1): the constructor without parameters of the type known from the context
namespace emptynew
import Root
class Aclass {
  public i32 n = 7
}
class Box<T> {
  public T v
}
struct Vec3 { r32 x, r32 y, r32 z = -1.0 }
fun show(Aclass a) { Console.log(a.n) }
fun ->Aclass make() { return () }
class Holder {
  public Aclass inner = ()
}
Aclass a = ()
Console.log(a.n)
a.n = 9
a = ()
Console.log(a.n)
show(())
Console.log(make().n)
Vec3 v = ()
Console.log(v)
Box<i32> b = ()
Console.log(b.v)
Console.log(Holder().inner.n)
// enforce, step 3 (spec 7.2.3, 7.2.5): the catch and atEnd clauses read from top to bottom; a pending exception skips the atEnd clauses
// until a catch handles it; the last atEnd always runs, like a finally; the temporary usage of a weak reference ends after it
import Root

public class E1(string text) : Exception { }
public class E2(string text) : Exception { }
public class E3(string text) : Exception { }

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

fun raise(i32 which) {
  if which == 1 { throw E1("e1") }
  if which == 2 { throw E2("e2") }
  if which == 3 { throw E3("e3") }
}

// block raises in the block, a1 in atEnd 1, a3 in atEnd 3
fun sequence(i32? value, i32 block, i32 a1, i32 a3) {
  enforce value {
    Console.log("  block")
    raise(block)
  }
  atEnd {
    Console.log("  atEnd 1")
    raise(a1)
  }
  catch E1 { Console.log("  catch E1") }
  atEnd {
    Console.log("  atEnd 3")
    raise(a3)
  }
  catch E2 { Console.log("  catch E2") }
  atEnd { Console.log("  atEnd 5") }
  catch E3 { Console.log("  catch E3") }
  atEnd { Console.log("  atEnd 7") }
}

fun bubbles(i32? value) {
  enforce value {
    raise(3)
  }
  atEnd { Console.log("  success atEnd") }
  catch E1 { Console.log("  catch E1") }
  atEnd { Console.log("  finally") }
}

fun i32 early(i32? value) {
  enforce value {
    return value * 2
  }
  atEnd { Console.log("  finally after return") }
  return 0
}

fun weakUsage() {
  @Dog first = Dog("rex")
  %Dog w = %first
  enforce w {
    first is done.                       // the enforce still holds a usage
    Console.log("  in block: " + w.name)
  }
  atEnd { Console.log("  atEnd: " + w.name) }
  Console.log("  after the enforce")
}

Console.log("nothing fails")
sequence(1, 0, 0, 0)
Console.log("atEnd 1 raises E2")
sequence(1, 0, 2, 0)
Console.log("atEnd 3 raises E2")
sequence(1, 0, 0, 2)
Console.log("block raises E1")
sequence(1, 1, 0, 0)
Console.log("null: nothing runs")
sequence(null, 1, 1, 1)
Console.log("no catch matches")
bubbles(1)
catch E3 { Console.log("  caught outside") }
Console.log("return")
Console.log($"  {early(4)}")
Console.log("weak usage")
weakUsage()
Console.log("done")
// enforce, step 5 (spec 7.2.1, 7.2.6): default, the catch-all of an enforce; the expression form, T r = enforce E clauses: the variables of E
// are enforced, each clause gives the value (a single expression, or return in a longer block), nothing handled gives zero or null
import Root

public class Trouble(string text) : Exception { }

public class Box(i32 size) {
  public i32 size
  unrestricted fun i32 count() { size }
  unrestricted fun i32 test(bool fail) {
    if fail {
      throw Trouble("box trouble")
    }
    return size * 10
  }
  unrestricted fun ->string label() { $"box {size}" }
}

// statement form with default
fun statement(i32? a, Box b, bool fail) {
  enforce a, b {
    Console.log($"  value {a + b.test(fail)}")
  }
  null on a { Console.log("  no a") }
  default { Console.log("  default") }      // b null, or an exception no catch takes
}

fun i32 plain(Box b) {
  i32 size = enforce b.count()
  return size
}

fun i32 withDefault(Box b) {
  i32 size = enforce b.count() default { -1 }
  return size
}

fun i32 clauses(Box f, bool fail) {
  i32 count = 100
  i32 res = enforce f.test(fail)
    catch NullPointerException { return -1234 }
    catch Trouble {
      count++
      return count
    }
    default { -1 }
  return res
}

fun i32 nullClause(i32? a, Box b) {
  i32 total = enforce a + b.count() null on a { 1 } default { 42 }
  return total
}

fun i32 returned(Box b) {
  return enforce b.count() default { -7 }
}

fun i32 lastExpression(Box b) {
  enforce b.count() default { -8 }
}

fun ->string text(Box b) {
  string s = enforce b.label() default { "no box" }
  return s
}

Box box = Box(3)
Console.log("statement form")
statement(1, box, false)
statement(null, box, false)
statement(1, null, false)
statement(1, box, true)
Console.log("expression form")
Console.log($"  plain {plain(box)} {plain(null)}")
Console.log($"  default {withDefault(box)} {withDefault(null)}")
Console.log($"  clauses {clauses(box, false)} {clauses(null, false)} {clauses(box, true)}")
Console.log($"  null clause {nullClause(2, box)} {nullClause(null, box)} {nullClause(2, null)}")
Console.log($"  returned {returned(box)} {returned(null)}")
Console.log($"  last expression {lastExpression(box)} {lastExpression(null)}")
Console.log("  text " + text(box) + ", " + text(null))
Console.log("done")
// A declaration with its own atEnd (spec 14.2): Some a = Some() atEnd { } is atEnd of a, not run when a is null; the last atEnd of an
// enforce raising with nothing pending bubbles up (spec 7.2.3); enforce expressions in assignments, arguments, var and if conditions
// (spec 7.2.6)
import Root

public class Trouble(string text) : Exception { }

public class Res(string name) {
  public string name
  public fun release() { Console.log("  release " + name) }
  unrestricted fun i32 size() { (i32)name.len }
}

fun declared() {
  Res a = Res("a") atEnd { a.release() }
  Res none = null atEnd { Console.log("  wrong: none is null") }
  i32 n, Res b = 3, Res("bb") atEnd { Console.log($"  multiple {n} {b.name}") }
  Console.log("  body")
}

fun finallyRaises(i32? v) {
  enforce v {
    Console.log("  block")
  }
  atEnd {
    Console.log("  last atEnd raises")
    throw Trouble("from the last atEnd")
  }
}

fun i32 twice(i32 x) { x * 2 }

fun expressions(Res f) {
  i32 x = 1
  x = enforce f.size() default { -1 }
  Console.log($"  assigned {x}")
  Console.log($"  argument {twice(enforce f.size() default { -5 })}")
  var v = enforce f.size() default { 0 }
  Console.log($"  var {v}")
}

fun sized(Res f) {
  if enforce f.size() default { 0 } > 2 {
    Console.log("  big")
  } else {
    Console.log("  small or none")
  }
}

Console.log("declaration atEnd")
declared()
Console.log("last atEnd raising")
finallyRaises(1)
catch Trouble ex { Console.log("  caught outside: " + ex.message) }
Console.log("expressions")
expressions(Res("four"))
expressions(null)
Console.log("if condition")
sized(Res("four"))
sized(null)
Console.log("done")
// enforce, step 2 (spec 7.2.1, 7.2.2): the checks of T?, objects, counted objects and weak references in the order written; when one fails
// the first null clause that names it (or names none) runs, the else branch in the short form, or nothing when no clause matches
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

fun check(i32? a, Dog b, i32? c) {
  enforce a, b, c {
    Console.log($"  all there: {a + c} {b.name}")
  }
  null on a { Console.log("  no a") }
  null on b, c { Console.log("  no b or c") }
}

fun firstClause(i32? a, i32? b) {
  enforce a, b {
    Console.log("  both")
  }
  null on b { Console.log("  b missing (named)") }
  null { Console.log("  something missing (any)") }
}

fun quiet(i32? a) {
  enforce a {
    Console.log("  a is there")
  }
  null on a { }
  Console.log("  goes on")
}

fun noClause(i32? a, i32? b) {
  enforce a, b {
    Console.log("  both there")
  }
  null on a { Console.log("  no a") }
  Console.log("  after")                 // b missing: no clause for it, the enforce is skipped quietly
}

fun shortForm(i32? a) {
  enforce a {
    Console.log($"  value {a}")
  } else {
    Console.log("  else")
  }
}

fun weak() {
  @Dog first = Dog("rex")
  %Dog w = %first
  enforce w {
    Console.log("  alive: " + w.name)
  }
  null on w { Console.log("  gone") }
  first is done.
  enforce w {
    Console.log("  alive: " + w.name)
  }
  null on w { Console.log("  gone") }
}

fun counted() {
  @Dog d = Dog("max")
  enforce d {
    Console.log("  counted: " + d.name)
  }
  d is done.
  enforce d {
    Console.log("  counted: " + d.name)
  }
  null { Console.log("  counted released") }
}

Console.log("all three")
check(1, Dog("a"), 2)
Console.log("a null")
check(null, Dog("b"), 2)
Console.log("b null")
check(1, null, 2)
Console.log("c null")
check(1, Dog("c"), null)
Console.log("first clause")
firstClause(1, null)
firstClause(null, 1)
Console.log("quiet")
quiet(null)
Console.log("no clause")
noClause(1, null)
noClause(null, 1)
Console.log("short form")
shortForm(3)
shortForm(null)
Console.log("weak")
weak()
Console.log("counted")
counted()
Console.log("done")
// enforce, step 4 (spec 7.2.3): catch E on x takes only the exceptions generated by x (a call whose receiver is x); a catch without on takes
// the others. And a return inside the block runs every atEnd clause, the ones before the catches too (spec 7.2.4)
import Root

public class Trouble(string text) : Exception { }

public class Part(string name) {
  public string name
  unrestricted fun work(bool fail) {
    if fail {
      throw Trouble(name + " failed")
    }
    Console.log("    " + name + " works")
  }
  unrestricted fun Part self() { this }
  unrestricted fun i32 value(i32 v) { v }
}

fun run(Part a, Part b, bool failA, bool failB) {
  enforce a, b {
    a.work(failA)
    b.work(failB)
  }
  catch Trouble ex on a { Console.log("  from a: " + ex.message) }
  catch Trouble ex on b { Console.log("  from b: " + ex.message) }
}

fun nested(Part a, Part b) {
  enforce a, b {
    a.value(b.value(1))                  // b's call ends before a's: a raising would be a's
    b.work(true)
  }
  catch Trouble on a { Console.log("  wrong: a") }
  catch Trouble on b { Console.log("  right: b") }
}

fun throughMember(Part a, Part b) {
  enforce a, b {
    a.self().work(true)                  // the receiver chain starts at a
  }
  catch Trouble on b { Console.log("  wrong: b") }
  catch Trouble on a { Console.log("  right: a") }
}

fun others(Part a) {
  Part loose = Part("loose")
  enforce a {
    loose.work(true)                     // not a variable of the enforce
  }
  catch Trouble on a { Console.log("  wrong: a") }
  catch Trouble ex { Console.log("  not on a: " + ex.message) }
}

fun i32 early(i32? value) {
  enforce value {
    return value * 3
  }
  atEnd { Console.log("  success atEnd 1") }
  catch Trouble { }
  atEnd { Console.log("  success atEnd 2") }
  catch all { }
  atEnd { Console.log("  finally") }
  return 0
}

fun i32 earlyRaise(i32? value) {
  enforce value {
    Part("p").work(true)
    return value
  }
  atEnd { Console.log("  wrong: success atEnd") }
  catch Trouble { Console.log("  caught") }
  atEnd { Console.log("  finally") }
  return -1
}

Part a = Part("a")
Part b = Part("b")
Console.log("a fails")
run(a, b, true, false)
Console.log("b fails")
run(a, b, false, true)
Console.log("nested calls")
nested(a, b)
Console.log("through a member")
throughMember(a, b)
Console.log("other objects")
others(a)
Console.log("return")
Console.log($"  {early(5)}")
Console.log("raise before the return")
Console.log($"  {earlyRaise(5)}")
Console.log("done")
// enforce timeout <ms> <expression> (spec 7.2.6): one wait for every lock of the expression form; a busy lock gives the lockfail value
import Root
public class Player(string name) {
  public i32 score
  public fun i32 bump(i32 n) { score += n; score }
}
fun i32 inside(&@Player p) {
  enforce p {
    // p is already locked here: the expression waits 20 ms and fails
    return enforce timeout 20 p.bump(1) lockfail { -1 }
  }
  return 0
}
fun run() {
  &@Player player = @Player("ann")
  i32 first = enforce timeout 500 player.bump(5) lockfail { -1 }
  Console.log(first)
  i32 wait = 10
  i32 second = enforce timeout wait player.bump(2) lockfail { -1 }
  Console.log(second)
  Console.log(inside(&@player))
}
run()
namespace enums
import Root

enum Status { Active, Inactive, Pending: 10, Waiting, OnHold: Pending }

enum u8 Level {
  Low: 1, Mid: 5, High: 9
  , fun Level Next(Level current) {
    return current ? { Low: Mid, Mid: High, High: High }
  }
  , fun bool IsTop(Level value) { value == High }
}

enum i64 Big { Min: -9223372036854775808, Max: 9223372036854775807 }

fun string describe(Status value) {
  return value ? { Active: "running", Inactive: "stopped", default: "waiting" }
}

Status current = Status.Pending
Status fresh
Console.log($"current = {current}, value {(i32)current}")
Console.log($"fresh = {fresh}")
Console.log($"Waiting = {(i32)Status.Waiting}")
Console.log($"OnHold prints as {Status.OnHold}, equals Pending: {Status.OnHold == Status.Pending}")
Console.log($"Active < Pending: {Status.Active < Status.Pending}")
Console.log("describe(Inactive) = " + describe(Status.Inactive))
Console.log("describe(OnHold) = " + describe(Status.OnHold))

Level level = Level.Low
level = Level.Next(level)
Console.log($"next of Low = {level}, top = {Level.IsTop(level)}")
level = Level.Next(level)
Console.log($"next of Mid = {level}, top = {Level.IsTop(level)}")

Status fromNumber = (Status)11
Status unknown = (Status)3
Console.log($"(Status)11 = {fromNumber}, (Status)3 = {unknown}")
Console.log($"Big: {Big.Min} {(i64)Big.Min} {Big.Max}")
if current then Console.log("enum to bool: true")
// Classes, phase 5: equals(T other), the operator ==, identity
import Root

public class Plain(i32 id) {
  public i32 id
}

// equals with several types of the right side
public class Number {
  public i32 value = 0
  replace fun bool equals(T other) {
    if T is int then return value == other
    if T is string then return value == (i32)other.len
    if T is Number {
      return value == other.value
    }
    return false
  }
}

// a subclass inherits equals; another one replaces it
public class Child : Number {
}
public class Odd : Number {
  replace fun bool equals(T other) {
    if T is Number then return true
    return false
  }
}

// the operator
public class Point(i32 x, i32 y) {
  public i32 x
  public i32 y
  fun bool ==(Point other) { x == other.x && y == other.y }
}

Plain a = Plain(1)
Plain b = Plain(1)
Plain sameA = a
Console.log(a == b)
Console.log(a == sameA)
Console.log(a != b)

fun ->Number num(i32 v) {
  Number made = Number()
  made.value = v
  return made
}
Number n = num(5)
Number m = num(5)
Number k = num(6)
Console.log(n == m)
Console.log(n == k)
Console.log(n != k)
Console.log(n == 5)
Console.log(n == 6)
Console.log(n == "hello")
Console.log(n == "hi")
Console.log(n == 5.5)
Console.log(n == a)

// null is handled before equals
Number none = null
Console.log(n == none)
Console.log(none == n)
Console.log(none == none)
Console.log(n == null)
Console.log(null == n)

// subclasses: the class of the left operand decides, also through a parent variable
Child c = Child()
Number asNumber = Odd()
Console.log(c == 0)
Console.log(asNumber == k)
Console.log(asNumber == 99)
Number plainOne = Number()
Console.log(plainOne == c)

Point p = Point(1, 2)
Point q = Point(1, 2)
Point z = Point(3, 4)
Console.log(p == q)
Console.log(p == z)
Console.log(p != z)
Point nothing = null
Console.log(p == nothing)
Console.log(nothing == p)
Console.log(nothing == nothing)
namespace errors
import Root

fun i32 divide(i32 total, i32 parts) { total / parts }

fun i32 safeDivide(i32 total, i32 parts) {
  i32 result = divide(total, parts)
  catch DivisionByZeroException ex {
    Console.log($"caught: {ex.message} | id {ex.id} | in {ex.function} | line {ex.line}")
    return -1
  }
  return result
}

Console.log($"safeDivide(10, 2) = {safeDivide(10, 2)}")
Console.log($"safeDivide(10, 0) = {safeDivide(10, 0)}")

// catches in order: the first one that handles the type runs
fun check(i32 mode) {
  if mode == 1 then throw Exception("plain")
  if mode == 2 then throw DivisionByZeroException("by zero")
  i32 zero = 0
  i32 boom = 1 / zero
  catch DivisionByZeroException {
    Console.log($"  mode {mode}: division by zero catch")
  }
  catch Exception ex {
    Console.log($"  mode {mode}: generic catch, message '{ex.message}'")
  }
  Console.log($"  mode {mode}: code after the catches runs")
}
check(1)
check(2)
check(3)

// throw in a function, caught by the caller; rethrow to the next level
fun fail(string message) {
  throw Exception(message)
}
fun relay() {
  fail("from fail()")
  catch Exception ex {
    Console.log("relay saw: " + ex.message)
    throw ex
  }
}
relay()
catch all ex {
  Console.log($"top level got it again: {ex.message}, thrown in {ex.function}")
}

// a catch only covers the code above it
{
  Console.log("before")
  catch all {
    Console.log("never runs")
  }
  fail("below the catch")
  catch Exception ex {
    Console.log("second catch: " + ex.message)
  }
}

// atEnd: whole scope, every exit, last to first
fun i32 cleanup(i32 mode) {
  atEnd { Console.log($"  first atEnd, mode {mode}") }
  atEnd { Console.log($"  second atEnd, mode {mode}") }
  if mode == 1 then return 10
  if mode == 2 then fail("failing in cleanup")
  return 20
}
Console.log($"cleanup(1) = {cleanup(1)}")
Console.log($"cleanup(3) = {cleanup(3)}")
i32 failed = cleanup(2)
catch Exception ex {
  Console.log("cleanup(2) raised: " + ex.message)
}

// the return value is computed first; a return inside atEnd overrides it
fun i32 counter() {
  i32 count = 1
  atEnd {
    Console.log($"  atEnd sees count = {count}")
    return count * 100
  }
  return count++
}
Console.log($"counter() = {counter()}")

// variables declared after atEnd have their default value when the scope ends before them
fun early(bool leave) {
  atEnd { Console.log($"  early({leave}): total = {total}") }
  if leave then return
  i32 total = 5
}
early(true)
early(false)

// loop body atEnd: once, after the loop ends, only if the body ran
for i32 i = 0..3 {
  atEnd { Console.log($"loop atEnd after the loop, i = {i}") }
  Console.log($"iteration {i}")
}
i32 none = 0
while none > 0 {
  atEnd { Console.log("never: the body never ran") }
  none--
}
i32 turns = 0
while true {
  atEnd { Console.log($"break: atEnd once, turns = {turns}") }
  turns++
  if turns == 2 then continue
  if turns == 4 then break
}

// leaving nested scopes with jump and break
for i32 outer = 0..3 {
  {
    atEnd { Console.log($"inner block atEnd, outer = {outer}") }
    if outer == 1 then jump #done#
  }
}
#done#
Console.log("after #done#")

// a catch that leaves the scope runs the atEnd
fun leaving() {
  atEnd { Console.log("  leaving(): atEnd") }
  fail("x")
  catch {
    Console.log("  leaving(): catch returns")
    return
  }
}
leaving()
Console.log("done")
// Left-to-right order (spec 2.5): the operands of an operator and the arguments of a call run from left to right, so a part that changes
// a variable (a & argument, an assignment, ++) is seen by the parts after it
import Root
fun i32 bump(&i32 n) {
  n += 10
  n
}
fun ->string twice(&string s) {
  s = s + s
  s
}
fun i32 pair(i32 a, i32 b) {
  a * 100 + b
}
public class Calc {
  public fun i32 pair(i32 a, i32 b) { a * 100 + b }
}
fun main() {
  string t = "ab"
  Console.log("1 " + twice(&t) + " " + t)
  i32 k = 1
  Console.log($"2 {bump(&k) + k}")
  i32 j = 1
  Console.log($"3 {pair(bump(&j), j)}")
  i32 m = 1
  Console.log($"4 {pair(m, bump(&m))}")
  i32 c = 1
  Console.log($"5 {Calc().pair(bump(&c), c)}")
  i32 q = 1
  i32(i32, i32) f = (i32 a, i32 b) { a * 100 + b }
  Console.log($"6 {f(bump(&q), q)}")
  i32 w = 1
  Console.log($"7 {bump(&w)} {w}")
}
// Every exception record is released: caught, ignored (silent), replaced by another, left through return / break / continue.
// Run with the environment variable DF_EXC_COUNT=1 to print how many records are alive at the end (it must be 0).
import Root

public class Notice(string text) : Exception, SilentException { }
public class Trouble(string text) : Exception { }

fun i32 divide(i32 a, i32 b) { a / b }
fun note(i32 x) {
  if x > 0 then throw Notice("note")
}
fun trouble(i32 x) {
  if x > 0 then throw Trouble("trouble")
}

fun i32 caught(i32 a) {
  return divide(a, 0)
  catch DivisionByZeroException { return -1 }              // left through return
}
fun ->string withVariable(i32 a) {
  return $"{divide(a, 0)}"
  catch DivisionByZeroException ex { return ex.message }   // the message is copied before the record is released
}
fun i32 replaced(i32 a) {
  return divide(a, 0)
  catch DivisionByZeroException { throw Trouble("replaced") }   // the original is released, the new one goes up
}
fun i32 rethrown(i32 a) {
  return divide(a, 0)
  catch DivisionByZeroException ex { throw ex }            // the same record goes up
}
fun ->string outerCatch(i32 a) {
  return $"{replaced(a)}"
  catch Trouble t { return t.message }
}
fun ->string rethrownCatch(i32 a) {
  return $"{rethrown(a)}"
  catch Exception e { return e.message }
}
fun i32 ignoredSilent(i32 a) {
  note(a)                                                  // the notice is ignored here and released
  return a
}

i32 total = 0
i32 i = 0
while i < 1000 {
  total += caught(i)
  note(i)
  total += ignoredSilent(1)
  trouble(0)
  i += 1
}
Console.log(total)
Console.log(withVariable(1))
Console.log(outerCatch(1))
Console.log(rethrownCatch(1))

// break and continue out of a catch body
i32 count = 0
i32 k = 0
while k < 10 {
  k += 1
  total += divide(k, 0)
  catch DivisionByZeroException {
    count += 1
    if k == 6 then break
    continue
  }
}
Console.log(count)
// Classes, phase 6: exception classes, catch by class and by trait, silent exceptions
import Root

public class AppException : Exception { }

public class NetworkException(i32 code) : Exception {
  public i32 code
}

public class TimeoutException : AppException {
  public string host = "unknown"
}

public class AlertException(string what) : Exception, SilentException {
  public string what
}

fun Fail(i32 kind) {
  if kind == 1 then throw AppException("app failed")
  if kind == 2 then throw NetworkException("net down", 503)
  if kind == 3 then throw TimeoutException("too slow")
  if kind == 4 then throw AppException()
  if kind == 5 then throw NetworkException(code: 7)
}

fun ->string Try(i32 kind) {
  Fail(kind)
  return "ok"
  catch TimeoutException ex {
    return "timeout " + ex.host + ": " + ex.message
  }
  catch NetworkException ex {
    return "network " + ex.code + ": " + ex.message
  }
  catch AppException ex {
    return "app [" + ex.message + "]"
  }
}
Console.log(Try(0))
Console.log(Try(1))
Console.log(Try(2))
Console.log(Try(3))
Console.log(Try(4))
Console.log(Try(5))

// the base class catches the children; the properties of every exception
fun ->string Describe(i32 kind) {
  Fail(kind)
  return "ok"
  catch Exception ex {
    return "any: " + ex.message + " id>=256: " + (ex.id >= 256)
  }
}
Console.log(Describe(2))
Console.log(Describe(3))

// is, and the exception as the base type
fun ->string Which(i32 kind) {
  Fail(kind)
  return "ok"
  catch Exception ex {
    if ex is TimeoutException t then return "timeout/" + t.host
    if ex is NetworkException n then return "network/" + n.code
    return "other"
  }
}
Console.log(Which(3))
Console.log(Which(2))
Console.log(Which(1))

// standard exceptions are caught by the same mechanism
fun ->string Divide(i32 d) {
  i32 q = 10 / d
  return "q=" + q
  catch DivisionByZeroException {
    return "division"
  }
  catch Exception {
    return "never"
  }
}
Console.log(Divide(2))
Console.log(Divide(0))

// rethrow of a caught exception object, and a throw inside a catch
fun Wrap(i32 kind) {
  Fail(kind)
  catch NetworkException ex {
    throw ex
  }
}
fun ->string Outer(i32 kind) {
  Wrap(kind)
  return "wrapped ok"
  catch TimeoutException ex {
    return "outer timeout " + ex.host
  }
  catch NetworkException ex {
    return "outer network " + ex.code
  }
}
Console.log(Outer(0))
Console.log(Outer(2))
Console.log(Outer(3))

// silent exceptions: they leave a function only when a catch of its caller handles them (catch Exception included)
fun Alarm(string text) {
  throw AlertException("alert", text)
}

fun string NoCatch() {
  Alarm("a")
  return "continued"
}
fun string CatchPlain() {
  Alarm("b")
  return "continued"
  catch Exception ex {
    return "caught by plain catch (wrong)"
  }
}
Console.log(NoCatch())
Console.log(CatchPlain())

fun ->string CatchOwn() {
  throw AlertException("alert", "c")
  return "continued"
  catch AlertException ex {
    return "own type: " + ex.what
  }
}
fun ->string CatchTrait() {
  throw AlertException("alert", "d")
  return "continued"
  catch SilentException ex {
    return "by trait: " + ex.message
  }
}
fun string CatchAll() {
  throw AlertException("alert", "e")
  return "continued"
  catch all {
    return "catch all"
  }
}
Console.log(CatchOwn())
Console.log(CatchTrait())
Console.log(CatchAll())

// a silent standard exception is caught by the trait too
fun string Standard() {
  time t = time(99999999)
  return "no catch"
  catch SilentException {
    return "silent standard"
  }
}
Console.log(Standard())

// an unhandled exception object ends the program with its message
throw NetworkException("unhandled network", 1)
namespace features

// Root is global: no alias needed
Root.Console.print("Print adds no newline, ")
Root.Console.log("Log does")

// overloading by number, order and types of parameters
fun ->string describe(i32 value) { "i32 " + value }
fun ->string describe(i64 value) { "i64 " + value }
fun ->string describe(i32 value, i32 width) { $"i32 {value} width {width}" }
fun ->string describe(string text, i32 count) { $"text {text} x{count}" }
fun ->string describe(i32 count, string text) { $"count {count} then {text}" }
i8 small = 5
i32 medium = 6
Root.Console.log(describe(7))              // literal: the native-size i64 wins
Root.Console.log(describe(small))          // i8: smallest widening is i32
Root.Console.log(describe(medium))
Root.Console.log(describe(1, 2))
Root.Console.log(describe("a", 3))
Root.Console.log(describe(4, "b"))

// named arguments in any position, defaults for the rest
fun ->string connect(string host = "localhost", u16 port = 80, bool secure = false) {
  $"{host}:{port} secure={secure}"
}
Root.Console.log(connect())
Root.Console.log(connect(port: 443, secure: true))
Root.Console.log(connect(secure: true, "example.org"))

// ranges that count down
Root.Console.print("10..=7:")
for u8 down = 10..=7 {
  Root.Console.print($" {down}")
}
Root.Console.print(" | 3..=0 unsigned:")
for u8 toZero = 3..=0 {
  Root.Console.print($" {toZero}")
}
Root.Console.print(" | 5..2:")
for i32 exclusive = 5..2 {
  Root.Console.print($" {exclusive}")
}
Root.Console.print(" | 0..=6 step 2:")
for i32 stepped = 0..=6 step stepped += 2 {
  Root.Console.print($" {stepped}")
}
Root.Console.log("")

// switch case ranges, multi-line bodies, jump into a range
fun ->string httpClass(i32 code) {
  string result = ""
  switch code {
    100, 201:
      result = "success"
    400..=499:
      result = "client error"
    0:
      result = "zero, then "
      jump 1
    1..10:
      result += "small"
    default: result = "unknown"
  }
  return result
}
Root.Console.log($"201 {httpClass(201)}, 404 {httpClass(404)}, 0 {httpClass(0)}, 5 {httpClass(5)}, 999 {httpClass(999)}")

// switch expressions with ranges: complete without default
for i32 value = -2..=2 {
  i32 sign = value ? { ..=-1: -1, 0: 0, 1..: 1 }
  Root.Console.print($"{value}->{sign} ")
}
Root.Console.log("")
char letter = 'q'
bool isAlphaNum = letter ? { 'a'..='z': true, 'A'..='Z': true, '0'..='9': true, default: false }
Root.Console.log($"'q' alphanumeric: {isAlphaNum}")

// unsigned widens to a larger signed type
u16 port = 8080
i32 widened = port
i64 total = widened + port
Root.Console.log($"widened = {widened}, total = {total}")

// [bool] transmute
string empty = ""
r64 notANumber = 0.0 / 0.0
Root.Console.log($"[bool]0 = {[bool]0}, [bool]7 = {[bool]7}, [bool]\"\" = {[bool]empty}, [bool]NaN = {[bool]notANumber}, [bool]'x' = {[bool]letter}")

// definite assignment: no warning when every path assigns
i32 assigned
if total > 0 {
  assigned = 1
} else {
  assigned = 2
}
Root.Console.log($"assigned = {assigned}")

// Console input
Root.Console.print("ReadLine: ")
string line = Root.Console.readLine()
Root.Console.print($"[{line}]")
char? first = Root.Console.read()
char? second = Root.Console.read()
Root.Console.log($" Read: '{first ?? '-'}' '{second ?? '-'}'")
string rest = Root.Console.readLine()
string afterEnd = Root.Console.readLine()
char? noMore = Root.Console.read()
Root.Console.log($"rest [{rest}], at end [{afterEnd}], Read at end is null: {noMore == null}")
// Field groups (spec 11.1.4.1) and readonly fields (spec 11.1.4)
namespace fieldgroups
import Root

class Sprite {
  public pos { r32 x, r32 y }
  public scale { r32 x = 1.0, r32 y = 2.0 }
  public readonly i32 hits
  public fun hit() { hits++; pos.x += 1.0 }
  public fun r32 sum() { pos.x + pos.y }
  public fun reset() { pos = (0.0, 0.0) }
}

var s = Sprite()
s.pos.x++
s.pos.y = 5.0
s.pos.x += 2.5
Console.log(s.pos.x)
Console.log(s.scale.y)
s.hit()
Console.log(s.hits)
Console.log(s.sum())
s.pos = (10.0, 20.0)
Console.log(s.pos.y)
(r32 x, r32 y) p = s.pos
Console.log(p.x)
p.y = 7.5
s.pos = p
Console.log(s.pos.y)
var other = Sprite()
other.pos = s.pos
Console.log(other.pos.x)
s.reset()
Console.log(s.pos.x)
Console.log(s)

// errors
fun ->string readMissing() {
  File missing = File(File.combinePaths(File.getTempDir(), "dflat-no-such-file.txt"))
  string text = missing.readAllText()
  return "no error"
  catch FileNotFoundException ex {
    return "FileNotFoundException id " + ex.id
  }
}
Console.log(readMissing())

fun ->string badPath() {
  File bad = File("/d:/bad|name")
  return "no error"
  catch InvalidPathException ex {
    return "InvalidPathException id " + ex.id
  }
}
Console.log(badPath())

fun ->string createOnly(File target) {
  target.writeAllText("again", String.StringEncoding.UTF8, fs.FileWriteMode.CreateOnly)
  return "no error"
  catch FileAlreadyExistsException ex {
    return "FileAlreadyExistsException id " + ex.id
  }
}
Console.log(createOnly(report))

fun ->string noParent(string where) {
  File.createDirectory(File.combinePaths(where, "a/b/c"))
  return "no error"
  catch InvalidPathException ex {
    return "InvalidPathException id " + ex.id
  }
}
Console.log(noParent(base))

File.delete(base, fs.DeleteOptions.Recursive)
Console.log($"cleaned: {!File.exists(base)}")
// filter of objects and arrays (spec 6.6): the new collection owns clones of the items it keeps (vec, list, arr); an exception in the
// function ends the clones made so far; a new array from filter or from return ->a is owned by the local that receives it
namespace filterobjects
import Root
public class Dog(string name, i32 age) {
  public string name = name
  public i32 age = age
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun run() {
  vec<Dog> dogs = [Dog("rex", 3), Dog("max", 9), Dog("bo", 5)]
  vec<Dog> old = dogs.filter((Dog d) { d.age > 4 })
  Console.log(old.len)
  old[0].name = "MAX"
  Console.log($"{dogs[1].name} {old[0].name}")
  list<Dog> ld = [Dog("l1", 1), Dog("l2", 7)]
  list<Dog> lo = ld.filter((Dog d) { d.age > 4 })
  Console.log(lo.len)
  arr<Dog> ad = [Dog("a1", 6)]
  arr<Dog> ao = ad.filter((Dog d) { d.age > 4 })
  Console.log(ao[0].name)
  Console.log(dogs.filter((Dog d) { d.age > 100 }).len)
  vec<arr<i32>> grid = [[1, 2], [3], [4, 5, 6]]
  vec<arr<i32>> wide = grid.filter((arr<i32> r) { r.len > 1 })
  wide[0][0] = 99
  Console.log($"{grid[0]} {wide}")
  Console.log("end of run")
}
run()
fun stops() {
  vec<Dog> dogs = [Dog("a", 1), Dog("b", 1), Dog("c", 1)]
  vec<Dog> kept = dogs.filter((Dog d) {
    if d.name == "c" { throw InvalidCastException("stop") }
    return true
  })
  Console.log("not here")
  catch InvalidCastException ex {
    Console.log("caught " + ex.message)
  }
}
stops()

  arr<Dog> r = [Dog("m1", 1)]
  return ->r
}
fun given() {
  arr<Dog> ao = mk()
  Console.log(ao.len)
}

// sort with a lambda and the functional members filter, map<U>, reduce (spec 6.6, 13.3)
namespace functional
import Root

public class Dog(string name, i32 age) {
  public string name
  public i32 age
} atEnd {
  Console.log("end " + name)
}

arr<i32> numbers = [5, 3, 9, 1, 7]
numbers.sort((i32 a, i32 b) { b - a })
Console.log(numbers)
vec<string> names = ["pear", "fig", "banana", "kiwi"]
names.sort((string x, string y) { (int)x.len - (int)y.len })
Console.log(names)
list<i32> chain = [4, 2, 8]
chain.sort((i32 a, i32 b) { a - b })
Console.log(chain)
vec<Dog> dogs = [Dog("rex", 5), Dog("max", 2), Dog("ace", 9)]
dogs.sort((Dog a, Dog b) { a.age - b.age })
Console.log(dogs.map<string>((Dog d) { d.name }))
i32 limit = 4
Console.log(numbers.filter((i32 x) { x > limit }))
Console.log(names.filter((string s) { s.len > 3 }))
Console.log(chain.map<string>((i32 x) { "#" + x.toStr() }))
Console.log(numbers.reduce(0, (i32 total, i32 x) { total + x }))
Console.log(names.reduce("", (string text, string s) { text + s[0].toStr() }))
arr<i32> four = [1, 2, 3, 4]
Console.log(four.filter((i32 x) { x % 2 == 0 }))

Console.log(olds.len)

chain.sort(desc)
Console.log(chain)
// Generic methods of classes and traits (spec 11.3.1): fun addSize<T>(T t) and fun neg(Ints x) inside a class make one copy per type list,
// inferred or written (s.addSize<i32>(5)), also called without a receiver inside the class; a replacing method is generic too and
// replaces every copy; a generic method of a trait is replaced by the classes. A written type argument is not inferred again (same<u64>(2))
import Root
Generic Ints { i32, i16, i64 }
class SizeChecker {
  public uns total = 0

  public fun addSize<T>(T t) {
    total += t is ? {
      i32: 4, u64: 8,
      default: t.sizeof
    }
  }

  public fun neg(Ints integer) {
    Console.log($"neg {-integer}")
  }

  public fun T same<T>(T x) { x }

  public fun inside() {
    addSize(1)
    addSize<i32>(1)
    this.addSize<u64>(2)
  }
}
class Loud : SizeChecker {
  replace fun addSize<T>(T t) {
    Console.log($"loud {t}")
  }
}
class Pair<A> {
  public A first
  public fun B mapped<B>(B other) { other }
}
trait Shower {
  public fun show<T>(T x)
}
class Left : Shower {
  replace fun show<T>(T x) { Console.log($"left {x}") }
}
class Right : Shower {
  replace fun show<T>(T x) { Console.log($"right {x}") }
}
fun T same<T>(T x) { x }
fun f() {
  var s = SizeChecker()
  s.addSize(5)
  u64 seven = 7
  s.addSize(seven)
  s.addSize<i16>(5)
  s.addSize(true)
  s.inside()
  Console.log($"total {s.total}")
  s.neg(3)
  i64 four = 4
  s.neg(four)
  Console.log($"{s.same("x")} {s.same(2.5)}")
  SizeChecker l = Loud()
  l.addSize(9)
  l.addSize("str")
  var p = Pair<string>()
  Console.log($"{p.mapped(3.5)} {p.mapped<string>("q")}")
  Shower a = Left()
  a.show(1)
  a.show("t")
  Shower b = Right()
  b.show(2.5)
  Console.log($"{same<u64>(2)}")
}
f()
// Generics, phase 7a: generic functions and classes, sets, constraints, inference
import Root

Generic Texts { string, char }
Generic Whole { Integers, bool }

trait Named {
  unrestricted fun string Name()
}

public class Dog(string label) : Named {
  replace fun string Name() { label }
}

fun Item smaller<Item : Numeric>(Item first, Item second) {
  if first < second {
    return first
  }
  return second
}

fun r64 average(Numeric first, Numeric second) {
  ((r64)first + (r64)second) / 2.0
}

fun ->string describe<Item : Texts>(Item value) {
  $"text:{value}"
}

fun Item identity<Item>(Item value) { value }

fun Item firstOf<Item, Other>(Item first, Other second) where Other : Numeric { first }

fun string nameOf<Item : Named>(Item thing) { thing.Name() }

fun i32 total<Item : Integers>(Item first, Item rest...) {
  i32 sum = (i32)first
  for n in rest {
    sum += (i32)n
  }
  return sum
}

public class Box<Item>(->Item value) {
  public Item value
  public static i32 created = 0

  public fun Item Get() { value }
  public fun Set(->Item next) { value = ->next }
  public static fun Bump() { created += 1 }
}

public class Pair<First, Second>(First left, Second right) {
  public First left
  public Second right
  public fun ->string Show() { $"({left}, {right})" }
}

public class Registry<Item : Named>(->Item first) {
  public Item first
  public fun string Describe() { first.Name() }
}

Console.log(smaller(3, 5))
Console.log(smaller<u8>(9, 4))
Console.log(smaller(2.5, 1.5))
Console.log(average(3, 4.5))
Console.log(describe("hi"))
Console.log(describe('c'))
Console.log(identity("same"))
Console.log(firstOf("kept", 7))
Console.log(nameOf(Dog("rex")))
Console.log(total(1, 2, 3, 4))

Box<i32> a = Box<i32>(10)
a.Set(a.Get() + 5)
Console.log(a.Get())
Box<string> b = Box<string>("hello")
Console.log(b.Get())
Box<i32>.Bump()
Box<i32>.Bump()
Console.log(Box<i32>.created)
Console.log(Box<string>.created)

Pair<i32, string> p = Pair<i32, string>(1, "one")
Console.log(p.Show())

Box<Box<i32>> nested = Box<Box<i32>>(Box<i32>(7))
Console.log(nested.Get().Get())

Registry<Dog> registry = Registry<Dog>(Dog("fido"))
Console.log(registry.Describe())
var inferred = Box<r64>(2.5)
Console.log(inferred.Get())
Console.log(a)

// Errors (each one alone): smaller("a", "b")  -> string does not satisfy Numeric
//   Registry<i32>(5) -> i32 does not satisfy Named
//   identity()       -> cannot infer the type argument for 'Item'
//   Box x            -> 'Box' is generic: write its type arguments
// Generic traits (11.3.2): one trait made per list of type arguments
import Root

trait Container<Item> {
  unrestricted fun Item Get(u64 index)
  unrestricted fun u64 Size()
}

public class Names : Container<string> {
  replace fun string Get(u64 index) { "name" }
  replace fun u64 Size() { 1 }
}

public class Numbers : Container<i32> {
  replace fun i32 Get(u64 index) { (i32)index * 10 }
  replace fun u64 Size() { 3 }
}

public class Wrapper<Item>(Item held) : Container<Item> {
  public Item held
  replace fun Item Get(u64 index) { held }
  replace fun u64 Size() { 1 }
}

fun string first(Container<string> source) { source.Get(0) }

fun u64 sizeOf<Source : Container<i32>>(Source source) { source.Size() }

Names n = Names()
Numbers m = Numbers()
Container<string> viaTrait = n
Console.log(viaTrait.Get(0))
Console.log(first(n))
Console.log(m.Get(2))
Console.log(sizeOf(m))
Wrapper<i32> w = Wrapper<i32>(7)
Console.log(sizeOf(w))
Console.log(w.Get(0))
Container<i32> asNumbers = w
Console.log(asNumbers.Get(0))
// 'where' on the header of a generic class (11.3.1): constraints on a plain parameter, mixed with inline ones
import Root

trait Named {
  public fun string Name()
}

public class Dog(string label) : Named {
  replace fun string Name() { label }
}

public class Holder<Item, Count>(->Item first) where Item : Named, Count : Integers {
  public Item first
  public fun string Describe() { first.Name() }
}

public class Both<Item : Named>(->Item first) where Item : Named {
  public Item first
}

Holder<Dog, i32> h = Holder<Dog, i32>(Dog("rex"))
Console.log(h.Describe())

// of a global read and written through its accessors
import Root

  public i32 x = px
  public fun move(i32 d) { x += d }
  unrestricted fun i32 get() { x }

  public vec<i32> items = vec<i32>()
  public list<Point> points = list<Point>()
  public Point origin = Point(5)
  i32 hidden = 1
  public i32 level { R: { field } W: { field = value * 2 } }
}

// Top-level const (spec 1.5): set at program start, seen by every function and method;
// a list literal in a for loop takes the type of the loop variable
namespace globalconsts

class Board {
  public static i32 cells = AREA + 1        // a static reads a const: the consts are set first
  public fun i32 doubled() { WIDTH * 2 }
}

fun i32 total() { WIDTH + AREA + KICKS[1] }

// Globals (spec 18.3): fields read and written from outside under the lock (g.f = v, g.f += v, g.f++), a lent result copied
// (g.first() gives a copy of the object), a field that holds an object or a string copied when it is read
import Root

class Point(i32 px) {
  public i32 x = px
}

class Store {
  public i32 count = 0
  public string name = "store"
  public Point origin = Point(5)
  public vec<i32> items = vec<i32>()
  public fun Point first() { origin }
  unrestricted fun string label() { name }
  public fun add(i32 v) { items.add(v); count += 1 }
}

class Reader {
  public fun i32 read() { st.count + st.origin.x }
}

global Store st = Store()

st.count = 3
st.count += 2
st.count++

// a lent result of a method called on a global is copied while its lock is held (spec 18.3)
class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
}
class Shelf {
  Dog d = Dog("rex")
  unrestricted fun Dog first() { d }
  public fun Dog second() { d }
}
global Shelf shelf = Shelf()
Dog x = shelf.first()
Dog y = shelf.second()!
Root.Console.log(x.name + " " + y.name)
// Globals (spec 18.4, 18.5): the rank order checked at run time (CallingGlobalDefinedAfterException), an object owned by a global
// that calls it back (an internal call), an unrestricted method that calls back to write its global (it takes the write lock), field writes
// and reads refused by the rank at run time (low) but not for a global of the same class declared later (low2); the locks are released after an exception
import Root

class D {
  i32 n = 0
  public fun i32 bump() { n += 1; n }
}

class C {
  public fun i32 useD() {
    return d.bump()
    catch CallingGlobalDefinedAfterException ex {
      Console.log($"refused: {ex.message}")
      return -1
    }
  }
}

class B(i32 id) {
  public fun update() { a.note(id) }
}

class A {
  list<B> bs = list<B>()
  i32 notes = 0
  public fun addObj(->B b) { bs.add(->b) }
  public fun update() { for &B b in bs { b.update() } }
  public fun note(i32 id) { notes += id }
  unrestricted fun i32 total() { notes }
  unrestricted fun i32 sneaky() { poke(); notes }
}

fun poke() { a.note(100) }

global C c1 = C()
global D d = D()
global C c2 = C()
global A a = A()

fun tryPoke() {
  Console.log(a.sneaky())
  catch ReadLockBecameWriteLockException ex {
    Console.log($"refused: {ex.message}")
  }
}

Console.log(c2.useD())
Console.log(c1.useD())
a.addObj(B(1))
a.addObj(B(2))
a.update()
Console.log(a.total())
tryPoke()
Console.log(a.total())
Console.log(c2.useD())

// field writes and reads under the rank rule
class Low {
  public fun poke() { high.count = 1 }
  public fun i32 peek() { high.count }
}
class High {
  public i32 count = 0
}
global Low low = Low()
global High high = High()
global Low low2 = Low()   // after high: it can call it (low cannot)
fun tryWrite() {
  low.poke()
  catch CallingGlobalDefinedAfterException ex {
    Console.log("write refused")
  }
}
fun tryRead() {
  Console.log(low.peek())
  catch CallingGlobalDefinedAfterException ex {
    Console.log("read refused")
  }
}
tryWrite()
tryRead()
high.count = 5
Console.log(high.count)
low2.poke()
Console.log(low2.peek())
// Globals (spec 18), part 1: declaration, creation in declaration order, destruction in reverse order, use from functions and
// methods, field reads and writes, copies with !, names that hide a global
import Root

class Logger(string prefix) {
  vec<string> lines = vec<string>()
  public fun write(string text) { lines.add(prefix + text) }
  unrestricted fun u64 count() { lines.len }
} atEnd { Console.log($"logger end {lines.len}") }

class Cache(u64 start) {
  u64 hits = start
  public string name = "cache"
  public fun u64 getOrAdd(u64 k) { hits += k; log.write($"add {k}"); hits }
  unrestricted fun string label() { name }
} atEnd { Console.log($"cache end {hits}") }

global Logger log = Logger("> ")
global Cache cache1 = Cache(10)
global Cache cache2 = Cache(log.count() + 100)

fun bump() { cache1.getOrAdd(5) }

bump()
Console.log(cache1.getOrAdd(1))
Console.log(cache2.getOrAdd(2))
cache1.name = "first"
Console.log(cache1.name)
string copied = cache1.label()!
Console.log(copied)
Cache c = cache2!
Console.log(c.getOrAdd(0))
Console.log(log.count())

// a local, a parameter or a field with the same name hides the global
class Item(i32 v) {
  public i32 value = v
}
fun show(Item it) { Console.log(it.value) }
class Holder {
  Item item = Item(7)
  public fun print() { show(item) }
}
fun local() {
  Item item = Item(3)
  show(item)
}
fun param(Item item) { show(item) }
global Item item = Item(1)
local()
param(Item(4))
Holder().print()
Console.log(item.value)
// Globals (spec 18.5): the arguments of the calls in a chain and the value of g.f = v are evaluated before the lock of the global, so
// they can call a global declared after it (box is declared before src)
import Root

class Box {
  public i32 value = 0
  public vec<i32> items = vec<i32>()
  public list<Item> things = list<Item>()
}
class Item(i32 n) {
  public i32 n = n
}
class Source(i32 start) {
  i32 v = start
  public fun i32 next() { v += 1; v }
}

global Box box = Box()
global Source src = Source(10)

box.value = src.next()
Console.log(box.value)
box.value += src.next()
Console.log(box.value)
box.items.add(src.next())
box.items.add(5)
Console.log(box.items[0])
box.items[1] = src.next()
Console.log(box.items[1])
box.items[0] += src.next()
Console.log(box.items[0])
box.things.add(Item(src.next()))
Console.log(box.things[0].n)
Console.log(box.items.has(src.next()))
import Root

enum Color { Red, Green: 5 }

class Point {
  public i32 x = 1
  public i32 y = 2
}

class Named(string name, ->Point where) {
  public vec<i32> items = []
}

class Tagged {
  public i32 id = 5
  replace fun uns get_hash() {
    return 1000 + (uns)id
  }
}

class SubTagged : Tagged {
  public i32 extra = 9
}

class Point3 : Point {
  public i32 z = 3
}

// strings: rapidhash V3, seed 0
Console.log($"null constant: {"null".hash}")
Console.log($"empty: {"".hash}")
string nothing = ""
Console.log($"empty variable: {nothing.hash}")
Console.log($"a: {"a".hash}")
Console.log($"abcd: {"abcd".hash}")
Console.log($"16 bytes: {"0123456789abcdef".hash}")
Console.log($"17 bytes: {"0123456789abcdefg".hash}")
string long = ""
for i32 i in 0..30 {
  long += "abcdefgh"
}
Console.log($"240 bytes: {long.hash}")
string built = "ab"
built += "cd"
Console.log($"same text, same hash: {built.hash == "abcd".hash}")

// natives: the value widened with zeros
i32 minus = -1
i8 small = -1
u64 big = 18446744073709551615
Console.log($"i32 -1: {minus.hash}, i8 -1: {small.hash}, u64 max: {big.hash}")
bool yes = true
char letter = 'A'
Console.log($"true: {yes.hash}, false: {false.hash}, 'A': {letter.hash}")
r64 zero = 0.0
r64 negativeZero = -0.0
r64 half = 1.5
r32 halfShort = 1.5
Console.log($"-0.0 like 0.0: {negativeZero.hash == zero.hash}, 1.5: {half.hash}, r32 1.5: {halfShort.hash}")
r64 nan = zero / zero
Console.log($"NaN: {nan.hash}")
Color c = Color.Green
Console.log($"enum: {c.hash}")

// null: the constant, for every type
i32? missing = null
i64? wide = null
i32? present = 7
Point noPoint = null
Console.log($"null i32?: {missing.hash == "null".hash}, null i64?: {wide.hash == "null".hash}, 7 as i32?: {present.hash}, null object: {noPoint.hash == "null".hash}")

// tuples: $ over the components
(i32, i32) pair = (1, 2)
Console.log($"(1, 2): {pair.hash}")
(string, bool) mixed = ("a", true)
Console.log($"(\"a\", true): {mixed.hash}")

// classes: the default
Point p = Point()
Point q = Point()
Console.log($"Point 1, 2: {p.hash}, same content: {p.hash == q.hash}, get_hash(): {p.get_hash() == p.hash}")
Point3 p3 = Point3()
Point asPoint = Point3()
Console.log($"Point3 1, 2, 3: {p3.hash}, through a Point: {asPoint.hash == p3.hash}")
Named n = Named("ada", Point())
Named other = Named("ada", Point())
other.items.add(5)
Console.log($"Named: {n.hash}, the vec is not hashed: {n.hash == other.hash}")
Named lonely = Named("ada", null)
Console.log($"Named with a null field: {lonely.hash}")

// get_hash() overridden, and inherited as it is
Tagged t = Tagged()
SubTagged s = SubTagged()
Tagged asTagged = SubTagged()
asTagged.id = 6
Console.log($"Tagged id 5: {t.hash}, SubTagged id 5: {s.hash}, through a Tagged: {asTagged.hash}")

// collections: the type name only
vec<i32> v = [1, 2, 3]
vec<i32> w = []
arr<i32> a = [1, 2]
Console.log($"vec<i32>: {v.hash}, same for any content: {v.hash == w.hash}, arr<i32>: {a.hash}")

// the hash of a type: the hash of its fully qualified name (Root. for a program without a namespace)
Console.log($"i32.hash: {i32.hash == "i32".hash}, string.hash: {string.hash == "string".hash}, byte.hash: {byte.hash == u8.hash}")
Console.log($"Point.hash: {Point.hash == "Root.Point".hash}, Color.hash: {Color.hash == "Root.Color".hash}, not a value: {Point.hash != Point().hash}")
namespace hello
import Root

Console.log("Hello world!")

// hmap step 1 (spec 6.8.5, 6.8.5.1): native keys and values
hmap<i32, i32> ages = []
Console.log(ages.len)                          // 0
Console.log(ages.add(1, 10))                   // true: a new key
Console.log(ages.add(1, 11))                   // false: the value was replaced
ages[2] = 20
ages[-3] = 30
ages[2] += 5
Console.log($"{ages[1]} {ages[2]} {ages[-3]} {ages[99]}")   // 11 25 30 0
Console.log($"{ages.hasKey(2)} {ages.hasKey(99)} {ages.has(30)} {ages.has(31)}")
bool hit, i32 found = ages.tryGet(2)
Console.log($"{hit} {found}")
hit, found = ages.tryGet(7, -1)
Console.log($"{hit} {found}")
Console.log(ages.keyOf(30))                    // -3
Console.log(ages.len)                          // 3
Console.log(ages)
i32 taken = ->ages[2]
Console.log($"taken {taken}, left {ages.len}, has 2: {ages.hasKey(2)}")
ages.removeAt(-3)
Console.log(ages)
Console.log(ages.remove(11))                   // true
Console.log(ages.len)                          // 0

Console.log(flags.len)

// null is a key; a full-width nullable keeps it in a second word

Console.log($"{small[null]} {small[0]} {small[-1]} {small.len}")
hmap<i64?, i32> big = [null: 7, 0: 8]
Console.log($"{big[null]} {big[0]} {big.hasKey(5)}")
big.removeAt(null)
Console.log($"{big.hasKey(null)} {big.len}")

// reals: -0.0 is 0.0, every NaN is one key
hmap<r64, i32> reals = []

reals[0.0] = 1
reals[-0.0] = 2
reals[zero / zero] = 3
reals[-(zero / zero)] = 4
Console.log($"{reals.len} {reals[0.0]} {reals[zero / zero]}")

// other native keys
hmap<Color, char> colors = [Color.Red: 'r', Color.Blue: 'b']
Console.log($"{colors[Color.Blue]} {colors.hasKey(Color.Green)}")
hmap<bool, i32> truth = [true: 1, false: 0]

// many keys: splits, doublings, merges and shrinks, checked against an array
hmap<u32, u32> table = []
arr<u32> mirror = arr<u32>[3000]
arr<bool> present = arr<bool>[3000]
u64 seed = 12345
i32 round = 0
while round < 20000 {
  seed = seed * 6364136223846793005ul + 1442695040888963407ul
  u32 k = (u32)((seed >> 33) % 3000ul)
  if (seed >> 20) % 3ul == 0ul {
    table.removeAt(k)
    present[k] = false
  } else {
    table[k] = (u32)round
    mirror[k] = (u32)round
    present[k] = true
  }
  round += 1
}
i32 wrong = 0
u64 count = 0
u32 key = 0
while key < 3000 {
  if present[key] {
    if !table.hasKey(key) || table[key] != mirror[key] {
      wrong += 1
    }
    count += 1
  } else if table.hasKey(key) {
    wrong += 1
  }
  key += 1
}
Console.log(wrong)                             // 0
Console.log(table.len == count)                // true
u64 walked = 0
u64 sum = 0
for (u32 k, u32 v) in table {
  walked += 1
  if v != mirror[k] {
    wrong += 1
  }
}
Console.log($"{walked == count} {wrong}")      // true 0
Console.log(table.sizeof > 0u)
key = 0
while key < 3000 {
  table.removeAt(key)
  key += 1
}
Console.log($"{table.len} {table.sizeof}")

// a new key while the map is walked
hmap<i32, i32> locked = [1: 1, 2: 2]
fun tryAdd(&hmap<i32, i32> m) {
  for (i32 k, i32 v) in m {
    m[k + 100] = v
  }
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException {
    Console.log("cannot add while walking")
  }
}
tryAdd(&locked)
Console.log(locked.len)

// hmap step 5 (spec 6.8.5.1): .capacity, reserve, shrinkToFit and hmap<K, V>[n]
hmap<i32, i32> empty = []
Console.log($"{empty.capacity} {empty.sizeof}")          // nothing is allocated before the first add
empty[1] = 1
Console.log(empty.capacity)                             // 11: one bucket

hmap<i32, i32>[1000] sized
Console.log($"{sized.len} {sized.capacity >= 1000u}")
i32 i = 0
while i < 1000 {
  sized[i] = i * 2
  i += 1
}
Console.log($"{sized.len} {sized[999]} {sized.capacity >= 1000u}")
var made = hmap<u8, bool>[50]
Console.log($"{made.len} {made.capacity >= 50u}")

// reserve(n): room for n entries in all, made now; a smaller n changes nothing
hmap<i32, string> names = [1: "one"]
names.reserve(500)
uns reservedCapacity = names.capacity
Console.log($"{names.len} {names.capacity >= 500u} {names[1]}")
names.reserve(10)
Console.log(names.capacity == reservedCapacity)          // true: the room stays

// shrinkToFit: every merge that fits, the smallest directory; false when nothing changed
i = 2
while i < 500 {
  names[i] = $"n{i}"
  i += 1
}
uns full = names.sizeof
i = 2
while i < 500 {
  names.removeAt(i)
  i += 1
}
Console.log(names.shrinkToFit())                        // false: the removals already merged the buckets and shrank the directory
Console.log($"{names.len} {names[1]} {names.sizeof < full} {names.capacity}")

// a reserved hmap that holds few entries: shrinkToFit merges the empty buckets
hmap<i32, i32>[5000] roomy
roomy[1] = 10
roomy[2] = 20
uns reserved = roomy.sizeof
Console.log(roomy.shrinkToFit())                        // true
Console.log($"{roomy.capacity} {roomy.sizeof < reserved} {roomy[1]} {roomy[2]} {roomy.len}")
Console.log(roomy.shrinkToFit())                        // false: nothing to do
names.removeAt(1)
Console.log($"{names.shrinkToFit()} {names.capacity} {names.len}")   // an empty hmap frees everything
names[7] = "seven"
Console.log(names)

// not while the map is walked
fun tryReserve(&hmap<i32, i32> m) {
  for (i32 k, i32 v) in m {
    m.reserve(10)
  }
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException {
    Console.log("cannot reserve while walking")
  }
}
tryReserve(&sized)

fun i32 sum(hmap<string, i32> m) {
  i32 total = 0
  for (string k, i32 v) in m {
    total += v
  }
  return total
}
Console.log(sum(["a": 1, "b": 2, "c": 3]))         // 6

// many string keys, checked against arrays
hmap<string, u32> table = []
arr<u32> mirror = arr<u32>[2000]
arr<bool> present = arr<bool>[2000]
u64 seed = 4242
i32 round = 0
while round < 12000 {
  seed = seed * 6364136223846793005ul + 1442695040888963407ul
  u32 k = (u32)((seed >> 33) % 2000ul)
  string key = $"key number {k}"
  if (seed >> 20) % 3ul == 0ul {
    table.removeAt(key)
    present[k] = false
  } else {
    table[key] = (u32)round
    mirror[k] = (u32)round
    present[k] = true
  }
  round += 1
}
i32 wrong = 0
u64 count = 0
u32 n = 0
while n < 2000 {
  string key = $"key number {n}"
  if present[n] != table.hasKey(key) || (present[n] && table[key] != mirror[n]) {
    wrong += 1
  }
  if present[n] {
    count += 1
  }
  n += 1
}
Console.log($"{wrong} {table.len == count}")

// .keys and .values of an hmap (spec 6.5, 6.6), step 4 of the hmap plan: the same program as mapviews, in the order of the table
import Root

hmap<string, i32> ages = []
ages["bob"] = 30
ages["alice"] = 25
ages["carol"] = 40

for string name in ages.keys {
  Console.log(name)                                // alice, bob, carol
}
i32 total = 0
for i32 age in ages.values {
  total += age
}
Console.log(total)                                 // 95
for var age in ages.values {
  Console.log(age * 2)                             // 50, 60, 80
}
Console.log(ages.keys.len)                         // 3
Console.log(ages.values.len)                       // 3
Console.log(ages.keys.has("bob"))                  // true
Console.log(ages.keys.has("dave"))                 // false
Console.log(ages.values.has(40))                   // true
Console.log(ages.keys)                             // [alice, bob, carol]
Console.log(ages.values)                           // [25, 30, 40]
ages["dave"] = 50                                    // the views above are gone: the map can grow
Console.log(ages.len)                              // 4

// m.keys! and m.values! are vectors with a copy
vec<string> names = ages.keys!
vec<i32> years = ages.values!
names.add("eve")
Console.log(names)                                 // [alice, bob, carol, dave, eve]
Console.log(years)                                 // [25, 30, 40, 50]
ages.removeAt("alice")
Console.log(names.len)                             // 5: the copy does not change

// a view held in a variable counts as open: the map cannot change its size while it exists
fun string grow(&hmap<string, i32> m) {
  var held = m.keys
  m["zed"] = 1
  return "grew"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(grow(&ages))                            // locked
Console.log(ages.len)                              // 3: the view ended with the function
fun ->string walk(hmap<string, i32> m) {
  var held = m.values
  string text = ""
  for i32 v in held {
    text += $"{v} "
  }
  return text + $"{held.len} {held.has(40)} {held}"
}
Console.log(walk(ages))                            // 30 40 50 3 true [30, 40, 50]
ages["zed"] = 1
Console.log(ages.keys)                             // [bob, carol, dave, zed]

// the loop over a view holds the counter like the loop over the map
fun string addInLoop(&hmap<string, i32> m) {
  for string k in m.keys {
    m["new"] = 0
  }
  return "added"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(addInLoop(&ages))                       // locked
for i32 v in ages.values {
  Console.log(v)                                   // 30, 40, 50, 1
}

// nullable keys: null comes first in .keys too
hmap<i32?, bool> maybe = []
maybe[2] = true
maybe[null] = false
Console.log(maybe.keys)                            // [null, 2]
Console.log(maybe.values)                          // [false, true]

// string values: up to 59 bytes in the slot, longer ones point to the string; a kept string is a copy
hmap<i32, string> words = []
words[5] = "five"
words[6] = "a string of exactly fifty-nine bytes, the most a slot holds"
words[7] = "a string of sixty bytes is longer than the slot and is pointed"
string kept = words[5]
string held
held = words[6]
i32 i = 0
while i < 5 {
  words[i] = $"small {i}"                            // the leaf moves its entries
  i += 1
}
Console.log(kept)                                  // five
Console.log(held.len)                              // 59
Console.log(words[7].len)                          // 62
Console.log(words[6] == held)                      // true
words[5] += "!"
Console.log(words[5])                              // five!
string out = ->words[7]
Console.log(out.len)                               // 62
Console.log(words.removeAll("small 3"))            // 1
Console.log(words.values)                          // [small 0, small 1, small 2, small 4, five!, a string of exactly fifty-nine bytes, the most a slot holds]
for (i32 k, string w) in words {
  if k == 6 {
    words[k] = "replaced in the loop"
  }
}
Console.log(words[6])                              // replaced in the loop
vec<string> texts = words.values!
words.clear()
Console.log(texts.len)                             // 6
Console.log(texts[0])                              // small 0
// hset<T> (spec 6.8.7), step 2 of the sets plan: the table of hmap with an empty value. Native and string items; add, has, remove, ->s[item], clear,

import Root

}

fun i32 count(hset<string> s, string wanted) {
  i32 found = 0
  for string item in s {
    if item == wanted {
      found += 1
    }
  }
  return found
}
Console.log(count(tags, "red"))                    // 1
class Visited {
  public hset<i32> ids = []
}
Visited v = Visited()
v.ids.add(5)
v.ids.add(5)
Console.log(v.ids)                                 // [5]

// not while it is walked (spec 6.4)
fun string grow(&hset<i32> s) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s.add(n + 100)
  }
  return "grew"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(grow(&numbers))                         // locked
fun string again(&hset<i32> s) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s.add(n)                                         // already there: the size does not change
  }
  return "same size"
}
Console.log(again(&numbers))                        // same size
// Classes, phase 2: single inheritance, replace, parent., virtual calls, is, upcasts, inherited statics
import Root

public class Shape {
  string label = "shape"
  public static i32 count = 0
  public static fun i32 Total() { count }
  unrestricted fun ->string Describe() { "I am a " + Kind() + " " + label }
  unrestricted fun string Kind() { "shape" }       // only reads: unrestricted (spec 1.6.2)
  unrestricted fun r64 Area() { 0.0 }
  public fun Touch() { count += 1 }
}

public class Circle : Shape {
  public r64 radius = 2.0
  replace fun string Kind() { "circle" }
  replace fun r64 Area() { 3.0 * radius * radius }
  public fun r64 Radius() { radius }
}

public class Ring(r64 hole) : Circle {
  i32 depth = 2
  replace fun ->string Kind() { "ring/" + parent.Kind() }
  public fun i32 Depth() { depth }
}

public class Square : Shape {
  public r64 side = 2.0
  replace fun r64 Area() { side * side }
}

// virtual calls through the parent type
fun ->string show(Shape s) { s.Describe() + " area " + s.Area() }

Circle c = Circle()
Console.log(c.Kind())
Console.log(c.Area())
Console.log(c.Describe())

Shape s = c
Console.log(s.Kind())
Console.log(s.Area())
Console.log(show(c))
Console.log(show(Square()))
Console.log(show(Shape()))
Console.log(show(Ring(0.5)))

Ring ring = Ring(0.5)
ring.radius = 1.0
Console.log(ring.Kind())
Console.log(ring.Depth())
Console.log(ring.Radius())
Console.log(ring.Area())

// array of mixed shapes
arr<Shape> shapes = [Circle(), Square(), Shape(), Ring(1.0)]
r64 total = 0.0
for Shape item in shapes { total += item.Area() }
Console.log(total)
for var item in shapes { Console.print(item.Kind() + ";") }
Console.log("")

// is
Console.log(shapes[0] is Circle)
Console.log(shapes[1] is Circle)
Console.log(shapes[3] is Circle)
Console.log(shapes[3] is Ring)
Console.log(shapes[2] is Ring)
Shape nothing = null
Console.log(nothing is Shape)
for Shape item in shapes {
  if item is Ring big {
    Console.log("ring with depth " + big.Depth())
  } else if item is Circle round {
    Console.log("circle " + round.Radius())
  } else {
    Console.log("other " + item.Kind())
  }
}

// statics are inherited
Shape.count = 0
c.Touch()
ring.Touch()
Console.log(Shape.Total())
Console.log(Circle.Total())
Console.log(Ring.count)
Circle.count = 10
Console.log(Shape.count)

// a parent field set through the child, and the printing of a child
Square sq = Square()
sq.side = 3.0
Console.log(sq.Area())
Console.log(sq)
Console.log(ring)

// comparing parent and child references
Shape same = ring
Console.log(same == ring)
Console.log(same == c)
// writing one part of a collection item (spec 3.2, 11.1.5): v[0].1 = 5, l[1].name = "x"; the part is written in place, and an object
// or an array that is replaced ends
namespace itemparts
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name = name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
struct Pet { Dog dog, string tag, i32 age }
fun plain() {
  vec<(i32, i32)> v = [(1, 2), (3, 4)]
  v[0].1 = 5
  v[1].0 += 10
  v[1].1++
  Console.log(v)
  arr<(string, i32)> a = [("a", 1)]
  a[0].0 = "b"
  a[0].1 *= 7
  Console.log(a)
  list<(i32, r64)> l = [(1, 1.5), (2, 2.5)]
  l[1].0 = 9
  l[1].1 += 1.0
  Console.log(l)
}
plain()
fun owned() {
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("d1"), 1), (Dog("d2"), 2)]
  v[0].0 = Dog("d3")
  v[0].1 = 7
  v[1].0.name = "D2"
  Console.log($"{v[0].0.name} {v[0].1} {v[1].0.name}")
  list<(Dog, i32)> l = [(Dog("e1"), 1)]
  l[0].0 = Dog("e2")
  Console.log(l[0].0.name)
  vec<(arr<i32>, i32)> g = [([1, 2], 1)]
  g[0].0 = [7, 8, 9]
  g[0].0[1] = 80
  Console.log(g[0].0)
  Console.log("end of owned")
}
owned()
fun structs() {
  list<Pet> l = [Pet(Dog("s1"), "a", 1)]
  l[0].age = 4
  l[0].tag = "b"
  l[0].dog = Dog("s2")

// Iterators and views as children of their collection (spec 6.4, 6.5, 6.7, 12.5): while one exists the collection keeps its size; its atEnd gives the
// counter back; one that outlives its collection raises UsageOfDestroyedObjectException
import Root

fun string tryAdd(&vec<i32> values) {
  values.add(0)
  return "added"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
fun string tryAddList(&list<i32> values) {
  values.add(0)
  return "added"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}

// an iterator made by hand: the vec is locked while the iterator exists (changing it through the variable that the iterator borrows
// from, while the iterator is still used, is a compile error, spec 12)
vec<i32> numbers = [1, 2, 3]
Console.log(tryAdd(&numbers))
Console.log($"{tryAdd(&numbers)} {numbers.len}")

// the iterator of a list follows the nodes
list<string> words = ["a", "b", "c"]
var wordIt = words.getIterator()
var w = wordIt.next()
while w.isValid {
  Console.print(w.value + ";")
  w = wordIt.next()
}
Console.log("")
wordIt is atEnd.
words.add("d")
Console.log(words)

// an iterator that outlives its collection
fun ->IndexIterator<i32> orphan() {
  vec<i32> local = [7, 8]
  var iterator = local.getIterator()
  return ->iterator
}
IndexIterator<i32> lost = orphan()
fun ->string tryNext(&IndexIterator<i32> iterator) {
  var got = iterator.next()
  return $"{got.value}"
  catch UsageOfDestroyedObjectException { return "the vec is gone" }
}
Console.log(tryNext(&lost))

// views: a read-only part of the collection, no copy
vec<i32> scores = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50]
// (in top-level code, a block: a function that changes a lent collection while views of it exist is a compile error, spec 12)
{
  var middle = scores[1..3]
  View<vec<i32>> head = scores[..2]
  var tail = scores[3..]
  var closed = scores[1..=3]
  Console.log($"{middle} {head} {tail} {closed} {middle.len} {middle[1]} {closed.has(40)} {closed.indexOf(30)}")
  i32 total = 0
  for i32 s in tail {
    total += s
  }
  Console.log($"{total}")
  scores[1] = 21                       // an item can still be replaced: the view sees it
  Console.log(middle)
}
Console.log(tryAdd(&scores))
fun string badView(vec<i32> source) {
  var outside = source[2..9]
  return "made"
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException { return "outside" }
}
Console.log(badView(scores))

list<i32> chain = [1, 2, 3, 4]
{
  var part = chain[1..3]
  i32 sum = 0
  for i32 n in part {
    sum += n
  }
  Console.log($"{part} {sum} {part[0]} {part.indexOf(3)}")
}
Console.log(tryAddList(&chain))

arr<string> letters = ["x", "y", "z"]
var some = letters[1..]
Console.log($"{some} {some.len} {some.has("z")}")
// objects as keys (spec 6.2, 6.6, 11.2.1): the class implements Keyable; the collection owns its keys (a new object or ->x),
// a lookup borrows the key; the default order is the hash, so two objects with the same content are the same key
import Root

class Person(string name, i32 age) : Keyable {
  public string name
  public i32 age
}

map<Person, string> roles = []
roles[Person("ann", 30)] = "admin"
Person bob = Person("bob", 40)
roles.add(->bob, "user")
Console.log(roles.len)
Person lookup = Person("ann", 30)
Console.log(roles[lookup])                              // admin: same content, same hash
Console.log(roles.hasKey(Person("bob", 40)))
roles[Person("ann", 30)] = "owner"                        // the key is already there: the new object ends
Console.log($"{roles.len} {roles[lookup]}")
for (Person p, string role) in roles {
  Console.log($"{p.name} {p.age}: {role}")
}
roles.removeAt(lookup)
Console.log(roles.len)

hmap<Person, i32> scores = []
scores[Person("cid", 20)] = 7
scores[Person("cid", 20)] += 1
Console.log(scores[Person("cid", 20)])

set<Person> seen = []
seen.add(Person("dan", 50))
seen.add(Person("dan", 50))
Console.log(seen.len)
hset<Person> hs = []
hs.add(Person("eve", 60))
Console.log(hs.has(Person("eve", 60)))

map<Person, i32> copy = []
copy.add(roles2())
Console.log(copy.len)

fun ->map<Person, i32> roles2() {
  map<Person, i32> m = []
  m[Person("fay", 70)] = 1
  return ->m
}
// hmap keys of a class that replaces equals (spec 6.8.5.1, 6.2): the same hash is not enough, equals decides; .hash of a Keyable object
// runs the check of 11.2.1; a Keyable class with a collection field gets a level 1 warning
import Root

class Tag(string name) : Keyable {
  public string name
  replace fun uns get_hash() { 7u }                 // every tag has the same hash
  replace fun bool equals(T other) {
    if T is Tag {
      return name == other.name
    }
    return false
  }
}

class Bag(string label) : Keyable {
  public string label
  vec<i32> items = []                              // warning L1: a collection field in a Keyable class
}

hmap<Tag, i32> counts = []
counts[Tag("a")] = 1
counts[Tag("b")] = 2                               // same hash, equals says different: two keys
counts[Tag("a")] += 10
Console.log($"{counts.len} {counts[Tag("a")]} {counts[Tag("b")]}")
Console.log(counts.hasKey(Tag("c")))
Tag t = Tag("x")
Console.log(t.hash)
Bag b = Bag("one")
Console.log(b.label)
// Counted and weak captures (spec 13.3.1): @a keeps a usage, %a a weak reference, &@a a read/write usage (writes inside enforce), ->a of a counted object moves its usage, f! adds a usage
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
  public i32 score
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun ->fun() keeper() {
  @Dog d = Dog("kept")
  fun() f = (; @d) { Console.log($"  in lambda {d.name} users {d.usersCount}") }
  Console.log($"  users {d.usersCount}")
  return f
}
fun weakOne() {
  fun() g
  if (true) {
    @Dog w = Dog("weak")
    g = (; %w) {
      enforce w {
        Console.log($"  alive {w.name}")
      } else {
        Console.log("  gone")
      }
    }
    g()
  }
  g()
}
fun writable() {
  &@Player p = Player("ann")
  fun() add = (; &@p) {
    enforce p {
      p.score += 5
    }
  }
  add()
  add()
  enforce p { Console.log($"  score {p.score}") }
}
fun moved() {
  @Dog m = Dog("moved")
  fun() h = (; ->m) { Console.log($"  moved {m.name} {m.usersCount}") }
  h()
  fun() h2 = h!
  h2()
  Console.log("  end of moved")
}
public class Player(string name) {
  public string name
  public i32 score
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun main() {
  Console.log("keeper")
  fun() k = keeper()
  k()
  Console.log("weak"); weakOne()
  Console.log("writable"); writable()
  Console.log("moved"); moved()
  Console.log("end of main")
}

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

fun ->fun i32(i32) adder(i32 n) { (i32 x; n!) { x + n } }

fun fun(), i32 pair(string who) {
  Dog d = Dog(who)
  return (; ->d) { Console.log(d.name + " here") }, 7
}

Console.log("1 vec")
var handlers = vec<fun(i32)>()
handlers.add((i32 x) { Console.log($"first {x}") })
handlers.add((i32 x) { Console.log($"second {x * 10}") })
for fun(i32) h in handlers {
  h(2)
}
handlers[1](5)
Console.log(handlers.len)

Console.log("2 arr and list")
arr<i32(i32)> ops = [adder(1), adder(10), (i32 x) { x * x }]
for i32(i32) op in ops {
  Console.log(op(3))
}

steps.add(adder(100))

Console.log("3 owned captures end with the collection")
{
  var barks = vec<fun()>()
  Dog a = Dog("a")
  Dog b = Dog("b")
  barks.add((; ->a) { Console.log(a.name + " barks") })
  barks.add((; ->b) { Console.log(b.name + " barks") })

  Console.log("removed")
  barks[0]()
}

fun() call, i32 n = pair("t")

Console.log(n)

// exceptions leaving a lambda (spec 13.3.6): through a direct call, a parameter, sort, filter, map, reduce; a silent one is ignored by the caller
namespace lambdaexceptions
import Root

public class Bad(string what) : Exception { }
public class Quiet(string what) : Exception, SilentException { }

fun i32 apply(i32(i32) f, i32 v) {
  f(v)
}

fun ->string direct() {
  i32(i32) f = (i32 x) { 100 / x }
  return "got " + f(0).toStr()
  catch DivisionByZeroException { return "division by zero from the lambda" }
}

fun ->string throughParam() {
  return "got " + apply((i32 x) {
    if x > 2 {
      throw Bad("too big")
    }
    x
  }, 5).toStr()
  catch Bad ex { return "Bad: " + ex.message }
}

fun ->string inSort() {
  vec<i32> v = [3, 1, 2]
  v.sort((i32 a, i32 b) {
    if a == 2 || b == 2 {
      throw Bad("no twos")
    }
    a - b
  })
  return "sorted"
  catch Bad ex { return "sort: " + ex.message }
}

fun ->string inFilter() {
  vec<string> words = ["a".toUpper(), "b".toUpper(), "c".toUpper()]
  vec<string> kept = words.filter((string s) {
    if s == "C" {
      throw Bad("C")
    }
    true
  })
  return "kept " + kept.len.toStr()
  catch Bad ex { return "filter: " + ex.message }
}

fun ->string inMap() {
  list<i32> n = [1, 2, 3]
  list<string> m = n.map<string>((i32 x) {
    if x == 3 {
      throw Bad("three")
    }
    "#" + x.toStr()
  })
  return "mapped"
  catch Bad ex { return "map: " + ex.message }
}

fun ->string inReduce() {
  arr<i32> n = [1, 2, 3]
  string s = n.reduce("", (string t, i32 x) {
    if x == 3 {
      throw Bad("three")
    }
    t + x.toStr()
  })
  return s
  catch Bad ex { return "reduce: " + ex.message }
}

fun ->string silentOne() {
  i32(i32) f = (i32 x) {
    throw Quiet("ignored")
    x
  }
  return "silent gives " + f(5).toStr()
}

fun ->string uncaught() {
  i32(i32) f = (i32 x) { 10 / x }
  return "got " + apply(f, 0).toStr()
}

fun ->string outer() {
  return uncaught()
  catch DivisionByZeroException { return "passed up to the caller of the caller" }
}

Console.log(direct())
Console.log(throughParam())
Console.log(inSort())
Console.log(inFilter())
Console.log(inMap())
Console.log(inReduce())
Console.log(silentOne())
Console.log(outer())
// lambdas in methods (fields by their bare names) and captures that own objects and arrays: a!, ->a (spec 13.3.1)
namespace lambdafields
import Root

public class Dog(string name, i32 age) {
  public string name
  public i32 age
  unrestricted fun ->string bark() { name + "!" }
} atEnd {
  Console.log("end " + name)
}

public class Kennel {
  public i32 visits = 0
  public string label = "k"
  public vec<i32> scores = [3, 1, 2]
  public Dog boss = Dog("boss", 7)

  public fun ->string describe() {
    fun() count = (; &visits) { visits++ }
    count()
    count()
    string(string) tag = (string s) { label + "/" + s + "/" + visits.toStr() }
    return tag("x") + " " + scores.filter((i32 v) { v > visits - 1 }).toStr()
  }

  public fun ->fun string() keeper() {
    return (; boss!) { boss.bark() }
  }
}

fun ->fun string() withDog() {
  Dog d = Dog("rex", 3)
  string() f = (; d!) { d.bark() + d.age.toStr() }
  return f
}

fun ->fun i32() withArray() {
  arr<i32> a = [4, 5, 6]
  return (; ->a) { a[0] + a[2] }
}

fun ->fun u64() withArrayCopy() {
  arr<string> a = ["x", "yy"]
  return (; a!) { a.len + a[1].len }
}

Kennel k = Kennel()
Console.log(k.describe())
string() kept = k.keeper()
Console.log(kept())
Console.log(withDog()())
Console.log(withArray()())
Console.log(withArrayCopy()())
Console.log("done")
// Function values as values of map and hmap (spec 13.3.3): each entry holds a usage; a replaced or removed value ends; clone copies them with !; printed as their type
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun ->fun() barker(string n) {
  Dog d = Dog(n)
  return (; ->d) { Console.log("  bark " + d.name) }
}
fun run(bool hashed) {
  map<string, fun(i32)> m = ["twice": (i32 x) { Console.log($"  {x * 2}") }]
  m["plus"] = (i32 x) { Console.log($"  {x + 1}") }
  m["twice"](4)
  m["plus"](4)
  fun(i32) f = m["plus"]
  f(10)
  Console.log($"  len {m.len} has {m.hasKey("plus")}")
  hmap<string, fun()> h = []
  h["a"] = barker("ha")
  h["b"] = barker("hb")
  h["a"]()
  h["a"] = barker("ha2")
  h.removeAt("b")
  h["a"]()
  hmap<string, fun()> copy = h.clone()
  copy["a"]()
  for (k, v) in h {
    v()
  }
  Console.log("  end of run")
}
fun main() {
  run(true)
  Console.log("end of main")
}
// Parameter modes in function types (spec 13.3.2): fun(&Dog), fun(->Dog), fun(@Dog), fun(%Dog), fun(&@Dog), fun(&i32); the lambda declares the same modes and the call repeats the markers
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun run(fun(&Dog) rename, Dog d) {
}
fun main() {
  fun(&Dog) rename = (&Dog d) { d = Dog(d.name + "2") }
  Dog rex = Dog("rex")
  rename(&rex)
  Console.log("  now " + rex.name)
  fun(->Dog) keep = (->Dog d) { Console.log("  kept " + d.name) }
  keep(Dog("tmp"))
  Dog max = Dog("max")
  keep(->max)
  fun(@Dog) share = (@Dog d) { Console.log($"  shared {d.name} {d.usersCount}") }
  @Dog c = Dog("counted")
  share(@c)
  fun(%Dog) peek = (%Dog d) {
    enforce d { Console.log("  weak " + d.name) }
  }
  peek(%c)
  fun(&@Dog) grow = (&@Dog d) {
    enforce d { d.name = d.name + "+" }
  }
  &@Dog w = Dog("rw")
  grow(&@w)
  enforce w { Console.log("  " + w.name) }
  fun(&i32) inc = (&i32 n) { n += 1 }
  i32 k = 1
  inc(&k)
  inc(&k)
  Console.log($"  k {k}")
  Console.log("end of main")
}
// Properties and the parent in a lambda (spec 13.3.1): read through the object (unlisted: read-only), &prop writes through W:, prop! copies the value read at creation
import Root
public class Library(string name) {
  public string name
  unrestricted fun ->string label() { "library " + name }
}
public class Reader(Library library as parent, string who) {
  public string who
  public i32 level { R: { field + 1 } W: { field = value * 2 } } = 3
  public string tag { R: { "tag-" + who } }
  public fun show() {
    fun() f = () { Console.log($"  {who} level {level} tag {tag} in {library.label()}") }
    f()
    fun() up = (; &level) { level = 10 }
    up()
    f()
    fun() snap = (; level!, tag!) { Console.log($"  snap {level} {tag}") }
    level = 20
    snap()
    f()
  }
  public fun ->fun() keep() {
    return (; level!) { Console.log($"  kept {level}") }
  }
}
fun main() {
  Library lib = Library("city")
  Reader rd = Reader(lib, "ann")
  rd.show()
  fun() k = rd.keep()
  k()
}
// Lambdas and function types (spec 13.3): captures, function values as variables, parameters and results, calls of null ones
namespace lambdas
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
  unrestricted fun ->string bark() { name + "!" }
} atEnd {
  Console.log("end " + name)
}

fun i32 apply(i32(i32) f, i32 v) {
  f(v)
}

fun ->fun i32(i32) adder(i32 n) {
  return (i32 x; n!) { x + n }
}

fun ->fun string() barker(string who) {
  Dog d = Dog(who)
  return (; ->d) { d.bark() }
}

fun ->fun string(string) prefixer(string p) {
  string own = p + ":"
  return (string s; ->own) { own + s }
}

fun ->string callNull() {
  fun(i32) f
  f(1)
  return "called"
  catch UnassignedFunctionCallException ex { return "unassigned " + ex.id }
}

fun ->string callNullAsNullPointer() {
  i32(i32) f
  return "got " + f(1)
  catch NullPointerException { return "a NullPointerException" }
}

fun owners() {
  string() b = barker("rex")
  Console.log(b())
  string() b2 = b
  Console.log(b2())
  string(string) pre = prefixer("x")
  for u32 i in 0..3 {
    Console.log(pre(i.toStr()))
  }
  string word = "outer"
  fun() show = () {
    fun() inner = () { Console.log("inner " + word) }
    inner()
  }
  show()
  Console.log(barker("max")())
}

Console.log(twice(21))
Console.log(apply(twice, 5))
Console.log(apply((i32 x) { x + base }, 5))
fun(string) greet = (string who) { Console.log("hello " + who) }
greet("rex")
(i32 a) { Console.log(a) }(42)
