  public set<i32> ids = []
}
Visited v = Visited()
v.ids.add(5)
v.ids.add(1)
v.ids.add(5)
Console.log(v.ids)                                 // [1, 5]

// not while it is walked (spec 6.4)
fun string grow(&set<i32> s) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s.add(n + 100)
  }
  return "grew"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(grow(&numbers))                         // locked
fun string again(&set<i32> s) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s.add(n)                                         // already there: the size does not change
  }
  return "same size"
}
Console.log(again(&numbers))                        // same size
// Converting a value to a member of a set (11.3.9): Box<Num>(x) picks the member from the argument
import Root

Generic ABC { u64, u32, u16 }
Generic CBA { u16, u32 }
Generic DEF { u64, u8 }
Generic Num { i64, r64 }
Generic Shapes { Circle, Shape }

trait Named {
  public fun string Name()
}

public class Shape : Named {
  replace fun string Name() { "shape" }
}

public class Circle : Shape {
}

public class Square : Shape {
}

public class Box<Item>(->Item value) {
  public Item value
  public fun ->string Show() { $"{value}" }
}

var a = Box<ABC>((u8)200)
var c = Box<CBA>((u8)200)
var d = Box<DEF>((u8)200)
var n = Box<Num>((u8)200)
var real = Box<Num>(5.5)
Console.log(a)
Console.log(c)
Console.log(d)
Console.log(n)
Console.log(real)
Box<u64> check = a
Box<i64> checkNum = n
var circle = Box<Shapes>(Circle())
var square = Box<Shapes>(Square())
Console.log(circle)
Console.log(square)
// step 6 of the sets plan (spec 6.8.6): union, intersection, difference, symmetricDifference give a new set of the kind of the left one; isSubsetOf,
// isSupersetOf, isDisjointFrom; set and hset mixed, the two sets unchanged
import Root

set<i32> a = [1, 2, 3, 4]
set<i32> b = [3, 4, 5]
Console.log(a.union(b))                            // [1, 2, 3, ...] (5 items)
Console.log(a.intersection(b))                     // [3, 4]
Console.log(a.difference(b))                       // [1, 2]
Console.log(b.difference(a))                       // [5]
Console.log(a.symmetricDifference(b))              // [1, 2, 5]
Console.log(a)                                     // [1, 2, 3, ...] (4 items): unchanged
Console.log(b)                                     // [3, 4, 5]: unchanged

// kept in variables; an hset on the left gives an hset
set<i32> both = a.intersection(b)
both.add(10)
Console.log(both)                                  // [3, 4, 10]
hset<i32> h = [4, 5, 6]
hset<i32> hu = h.union(a)                            // an hset with a set
Console.log(hu.len)                                // 6
set<i32> su = a.union(h)                             // a set with an hset: sorted
Console.log(su)                                    // [1, 2, 3, ...] (6 items)
Console.log(h.intersection(a).len)                 // 1
Console.log(h.difference(b).has(6))                // true
Console.log(h.symmetricDifference(b).len)          // 2: 3 and 6

// the tests
set<i32> small = [3, 4]
Console.log(small.isSubsetOf(a))                   // true
Console.log(a.isSubsetOf(small))                   // false
Console.log(a.isSupersetOf(small))                 // true
Console.log(small.isSubsetOf(small))               // true
Console.log(a.isDisjointFrom(b))                   // false
hset<i32> far = [100, 200]
Console.log(a.isDisjointFrom(far))                 // true
Console.log(far.isDisjointFrom(a))                 // true
set<i32> empty = []
Console.log(empty.isSubsetOf(a))                   // true
Console.log(a.union(empty).len)                    // 4
Console.log(a.intersection(empty).len)             // 0

// strings: the new set has its own copies
set<string> x = ["red", "green"]
hset<string> y = ["green", "blue"]
set<string> all = x.union(y)
Console.log(all)                                   // [blue, green, red]
x.clear()
Console.log(all)                                   // [blue, green, red]
Console.log(y.difference(all).len)                 // 0

// a new set made in a function
fun set<string> common(set<string> p, hset<string> q) {
  return p.intersection(q)
}
set<string> p = ["a", "b", "c"]
hset<string> q = ["c", "a", "z"]
Console.log(common(p, q))                          // [a, c]
// step 4 of the sets plan (spec 6.6, 6.8.6): add(collection), s += v, s += other, s -= v and s -= other on set and hset, the two kinds mixed
import Root

set<i32> sorted = [5, 1]
sorted += 3                                          // add(3)
sorted += 3                                          // already there
Console.log(sorted)                                // [1, 3, 5]
sorted -= 1                                          // remove(1)
sorted -= 99                                         // not there: nothing happens
Console.log(sorted)                                // [3, 5]

// add(collection) returns the number actually added; any collection with the same item type
arr<i32> a = [5, 6, 7]
Console.log(sorted.add(a))                         // 2: 5 was there
vec<i32> v = [8, 8, 9]
Console.log(sorted.add(v))                         // 2: 8 comes twice
list<i32> l = [10]
Console.log(sorted.add(l))                         // 1
hset<i32> hashed = [11, 3]
Console.log(sorted.add(hashed))                    // 1: an hset into a set
Console.log(sorted)                                // [3, 5, 6, ...] (8 items)
Console.log(sorted.add(sorted))                    // 0: the same set
Console.log(a)                                     // [5, 6, 7]: the source is unchanged

// += and -= with collections and literals
set<i32> small = [1, 2]
small += [3, 4]                                      // a literal: one add per item
small += hashed                                      // add(hashed)
Console.log(small)                                 // [1, 2, 3, ...] (5 items)
small -= [1, 2]                                      // one remove per item
small -= v                                           // removes every item of v that is there
small -= hashed
Console.log(small)                                 // [4]
hset<i32> other = [4, 20]
hset<i32> mixed = [1]
mixed += sorted                                      // a set into an hset
Console.log(mixed.len)                             // 9
mixed -= other
Console.log(mixed.has(4))                          // false
mixed -= mixed                                       // the same set: empty
Console.log(mixed.len)                             // 0

// strings
hset<string> words = ["a"]
vec<string> more = ["b", "c", "a"]
words += more
Console.log(words.len)                             // 3
set<string> sortedWords = []
sortedWords += words
Console.log(sortedWords)                           // [a, b, c]
sortedWords -= ["a", "c"]
Console.log(sortedWords)                           // [b]

// a field, a parameter
class Bag {
  public set<i32> items = []
}
Bag bag = Bag()
bag.items += 4
bag.items += [2, 6]
bag.items -= 6
Console.log(bag.items)                             // [2, 4]
fun fill(&hset<i32> s, vec<i32> from) {
  s += from
}
hset<i32> filled = []
fill(&filled, v)
Console.log(filled.len)                            // 2

// not while it is walked; the exception leaves a function that does not catch it (spec 6.4)
fun shrink(&set<i32> s) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s -= n
  }
}
fun string tryShrink(&set<i32> s) {
  shrink(&s)
  return "shrank"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(tryShrink(&sorted))                     // locked
fun string grow(&hset<i32> s, arr<i32> from) {
  for i32 n in s {
    s += from
  }
  return "grew"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "locked" }
}
Console.log(grow(&hashed, [100]))                   // locked
// set values as keys and their hash (spec 11.3.6.1, 6.8.4.1, 6.8.5.1): map and set keep the keys ordered by tag, then by value;
// hmap and hset hash the tag XOR the value; .hash of a set value is tag $ the hash of the value
import Root
Generic Nums { i32, u64, u8 }

Nums a = (i32)5
Nums b = 5ul
Nums c = (i32)5
Console.log(a.hash == c.hash)                          // true: same tag, same value
Console.log(a.hash == b.hash)                          // false: another tag
Console.log(a.hash == "i32".hash)                      // false: the value is part of it

map<Nums, string> names = [5b: "small five", 5ul: "big five"]
names[(i32)5] = "int five"
names[(i32)-3] = "int minus three"
Console.log(names.len)
Console.log(names[5ul])
Console.log(names[(i32)5])
Console.log(names.hasKey(5b))
for (Nums k, string v) in names {
  string kind = k is ? { i32 x: $"i32 {x}", u64 x: $"u64 {x}", u8 x: $"u8 {x}" }
  Console.log($"{kind}: {v}")
}
names.removeAt((i32)5)
Console.log(names.hasKey((i32)5))

hmap<Nums, i32> counts = []
counts[1b] = 10
counts[1ul] = 20
counts[(i32)1] = 30
counts[1b] += 1
Console.log($"{counts[1b]} {counts[1ul]} {counts[(i32)1]} {counts.len}")
counts.removeAt(1ul)
Console.log(counts.hasKey(1ul))

set<Nums> seen = []
seen.add(7b)
seen.add((i32)7)
seen.add(7b)
Console.log(seen.len)
hset<Nums> hs = []
hs.add(9ul)
Console.log(hs.has(9ul))
Console.log(hs.has((i32)9))
// set values with nullable members (spec 11.3.6.1, 11.3.8): T? of a native and the natives nullable by default (date, time, clock, lapse, addr)
import Root
Generic Maybe { i16?, u8 }
Generic When { date, i32 }

Maybe a = (i16?)null
Console.log(a)                                          // null: the null of i16?
Console.log(a == null)                                  // true
Maybe b = 7b
Console.log(b == null)                                  // false: u8 is not nullable
a = null                                                  // the only nullable member, i16?, takes it
Console.log(a is i16?)
a = (i16?)300
if a is i16? s { Console.log(s) }
Maybe same = (i16?)300
Console.log(a == same)
Maybe never
Console.log(never == null)                              // false: the generic null is not the null of a member

When w = date("2024-02-02")
Console.log(w)
date empty
When e = empty
Console.log(e == null)                                  // true: a null date
Console.log(w is ? { date d: "a date", i32 x: "a number" })
// set values (spec 11.3.6.1): a set as the type of a value is a closed union, the pair (tag, value); it is read with is and is ? { }
import Root
Generic Nums { i32, u64, u8 }
Generic Num { i32, r64 }

fun string describe(Nums k) {
  return "generic copy"                                   // a set as a parameter keeps its meaning: one copy per type
}

fun Num pick(bool real) {
  if real { return 2.5 }
  return 7
}

class Holder {
  public Nums value
}

Nums k = 5b                                               // u8
i32 count = 12
string text = k is ? { i32 x: "int", u64 x: "big", u8 x: "small" }
Console.log(text)
k = count                                                 // an i32
if k is i32 i { Console.log($"i32 {i}") }
if k is u8 b { Console.log("never") }
Console.log(k is i32)
Console.log(k is u8)
text = k is ? { u8: "small", default: "not small" }
Console.log(text)
Console.log(k)

Num n = 3                                                 // the default type of the literal, i32, is a member
Num m = 3.0                                               // r64
Console.log($"{n} {m} {n == m}")
Console.log(pick(true) == pick(true))
Console.log(pick(false))
Num zero = 0.0
Num minus = -0.0
Console.log(zero == minus)                              // reals compare as reals

arr<Num> mixed = [12, 12.0]
for Num item in mixed {
  r64 value = item is ? { i32 x: (r64)x * 2.0, r64 x: x / 2.0 }
  Console.log(value)
}
vec<Nums> v = [1b, 2b]
v.add(count)
Console.log(v.len)
Console.log(v.has(count))

Holder h = Holder()
Console.log(h.value)                                    // null: a new field holds the generic null
Console.log(h.value is i32)                             // false
Console.log(h.value == null)                            // false: no member of Nums is nullable
Console.log(h.value is ? { u8: "small", default: "unassigned or other" })
h.value = 9ul
Console.log(h.value is ? { u64 x: x + 1ul, default: 0ul })

public class Box<Item>(->Item value) {
  public Item value
}
Box<Num> boxed = Box<Num>((i32)5)                         // declared with the set: the copy holds set values
Console.log(boxed.value)
boxed = Box<Num>(2.5)
Console.log(boxed.value)
var picked = Box<Num>(1.5)                                // no declared target: the argument selects the member, Box<r64>
r64 half = picked.value / 2.0
Console.log(half)

Nums never
Console.log(never)
fun string strict(arr<Nums> items) {
  return items[0] is ? { i32 x: "int", u64 x: "big", u8 x: "small" }
  catch UnassignedTypeException { return "caught: never assigned" }
}
arr<Nums> empty = arr<Nums>[1]
Console.log(strict(empty))
// a field shares the string it is given (spec 4): k.label = a is the same string, a change through a is seen through the field, and when
// a ends the field keeps the text (level 3 warning). A borrowed string (a parameter), or an object of an inner scope, gets a copy.
import Root

class Kennel {
  public string label
  public string other
}

fun fill(&Kennel k, i32 n) {
  string t = "box " + n.toStr()
  k.label = t                      // shared: t ends with the function, k.label keeps the text
  t += "!"
}

fun copyParam(&Kennel k, string s) {
  k.other = s                      // s is borrowed: the field takes a copy
}

fun rebind(&Kennel k) {
  string a = "first " + 1.toStr()
  k.label = a
  Console.log(k.label)
  a = "second " + 2.toStr()        // a gets a new string; the field keeps the first
  Console.log($"{a} / {k.label}")
}

fun inner(&Kennel k) {
  string a = "outer " + 3.toStr()
  {
    Kennel local = Kennel()
    local.label = a                // local ends first: it takes a copy
    a += "?"
    Console.log(local.label)
  }
  Console.log(a)
}

Kennel k = Kennel()
string a = "top " + 0.toStr()
k.label = a
a += " changed"
Console.log(k.label)             // top 0 changed
fill(&k, 5)
Console.log(k.label)             // box 5!
string given = "given " + 9.toStr()
copyParam(&k, given)
given += "!"
Console.log(k.other)             // given 9
rebind(&k)
inner(&k)
k.label = "literal"
Console.log(k.label)
// Silent standard exceptions of the date and time constructors (spec 3.3, 15.4)
import Root

// no catch: the value falls back to null and nothing is raised
fun nothingCaught() {
  date a = date("not a date")
  Console.log(a == null)
  time none = time(-1)
  Console.log(none == null)
  time big = (time)90000000
  Console.log(big == null)
}

// a plain catch does not handle a silent exception
fun plainCatch() {
  date b = date("bad")
  Console.log(b == null)
  catch Exception ex {
    Console.log("wrong: catch ex must not see a silent exception")
  }
}

// a catch for its own type does (it covers the statements above it)
fun ownType() {
  date c = date("still bad")
  Console.log("not printed when raised")
  catch InvalidDateTimeFormatException ex {
    Console.log("caught: " + ex.message)
  }
}

fun otherType() {
  time late = time(90000000)
  Console.log("not printed either")
  catch OutOfRangeCastException ex {
    Console.log("caught: " + ex.message)
  }
}

// the other silent exception is not handled by a catch for a different one
fun wrongType() {
  date d = date("bad again")
  Console.log(d == null)
  catch OutOfRangeCastException {
    Console.log("wrong: different exception")
  }
}

fun catchAll() {
  time t = (time)(-3)
  Console.log("skipped")
  catch all {
    Console.log("catch all sees it")
  }
}

fun valid() {
  date fine = date("2024-02-02")
  Console.log(fine)
  catch InvalidDateTimeFormatException {
    Console.log("wrong: valid date")
  }
}

fun time safe(string text) {
  time t = time(text)
  catch InvalidDateTimeFormatException {
    return time("00:00")
  }
  return t
}

nothingCaught()
plainCatch()
ownType()
otherType()
wrongType()
catchAll()
valid()
Console.log(safe("08:15"))
Console.log(safe("garbage"))
namespace silentfiles
import Root.IO
import Root
alias Root.IO as fs

// the silent exceptions of the file library reach the function that calls the library function and catches them

string base = File.combinePaths(File.getTempDir(), "dflat-silent-test")
File.delete(base, fs.DeleteOptions.Recursive)
File.createDirectory(base)
File.createDirectory(File.combinePaths(base, "inner"))
File.createDirectory(File.combinePaths(base, "inner/deeper"))
File(File.combinePaths(base, "inner/file.txt")).writeAllText("x")

fun ->string tryDelete(string path) {
  bool done = File.delete(path)
  return $"deleted: {done}"
  catch DirectoryNotEmptyException e { return "DirectoryNotEmptyException (silent, id " + e.id + ")" }
}
Console.log(tryDelete(File.combinePaths(base, "inner")))     // DirectoryNotEmptyException (silent, id 28)
Console.log(tryDelete(File.combinePaths(base, "missing")))   // deleted: false

fun bool plainDelete(string path) {
  return File.delete(path)                                      // no catch: the silent exception is ignored, delete returns false
}
Console.log(plainDelete(File.combinePaths(base, "inner")))   // false

fun ->string tryCreate(string path) {
  File made = File.createDirectory(path)
  return "created " + made.name
  catch FileNameAlreadyExistsException e { return "FileNameAlreadyExistsException (silent, id " + e.id + ")" }
}
Console.log(tryCreate(File.combinePaths(base, "inner")))     // FileNameAlreadyExistsException (silent, id 29)
Console.log(tryCreate(File.combinePaths(base, "fresh")))     // created fresh

fun ->string viaObject(File directory) {
  return "deleted " + directory.delete()
  catch DirectoryNotEmptyException e { return "through the object, id " + e.id }
}
Console.log(viaObject(File(File.combinePaths(base, "inner"))))   // through the object, id 28

fun ->string copyTwice(File source, string target) {
  File first = source.copyTo(target)
  File second = source.copyTo(target)
  return "second copy: " + (second == null ? { "skipped", "made" })
  catch FileNameAlreadyExistsException e { return "FileNameAlreadyExistsException on the second copy" }
}
Console.log(copyTwice(File(File.combinePaths(base, "inner/file.txt")), File.combinePaths(base, "copy.txt")))
// FileNameAlreadyExistsException on the second copy

fun ->string copyIgnoring(File source, string target) {
  File first = source.copyTo(target, fs.OverwriteOptions.Overwrite)
  File second = source.copyTo(target)                           // the name exists: silent, ignored here, the copy is skipped
  return "second copy: " + (second == null ? { "skipped", "made" })
}
Console.log(copyIgnoring(File(File.combinePaths(base, "inner/file.txt")), File.combinePaths(base, "copy2.txt")))   // second copy: skipped

// streams: a seek before the start
fun ->string seekBack(&FileStream stream) {
  u64 at = stream.seek(-5, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.Begin)
  return $"position {at}"
  catch SeekBeforeBeginException e { return "SeekBeforeBeginException (silent, id " + e.id + ")" }
}
fun ->string seekIgnored(&FileStream stream) {
  u64 at = stream.seek(-5, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.Begin)
  return $"position {at}"
}
FileStream s = FileStream(File.combinePaths(base, "inner/file.txt"), fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
Console.log(seekBack(&s))                                      // SeekBeforeBeginException (silent, id 32)
Console.log(seekIgnored(&s))                                   // position 0
s is atEnd.

// the library errors that are not silent still go up on their own
fun ->string missing(string path) {
  return File(path).readAllText()
  catch FileNotFoundException e { return "FileNotFoundException id " + e.id }
}
Console.log(missing(File.combinePaths(base, "nothing.txt")))  // FileNotFoundException id 25

File.delete(base, fs.DeleteOptions.Recursive)
namespace silentmodel
import Root

// silent exceptions: they leave a function only for the caller that has a catch for them (EXCEPTIONS: THE MODEL, rules 4 to 6)

public class Warning(string text) : Exception, SilentException { }
public class Notice(string text) : Exception, SilentException { }
public class Trouble : Exception { }

fun i64 mul(i64 a, i64 b) {
  i64 product = a * b
  if product > 100 then throw Warning("big product")       // silent
  Console.log("  mul went on")                            // runs only when mul does not throw
  return product
}
fun i64 add3(i64 a, i64 b, i64 c) {                         // no catch: the warning of mul is ignored here
  return mul(a, b) + c
}
fun ->string observe(i64 a, i64 b, i64 c) {
  return "result " + add3(a, b, c)
  catch Warning w { return "observed in observe: " + w.message }
}
fun ->string observeMul(i64 a, i64 b) {
  return "mul " + mul(a, b)
  catch Warning w { return "observed in observeMul: " + w.message }
}
Console.log(observe(2, 3, 4))                             // mul went on / result 10
Console.log(observe(20, 30, 4))                           // result 4: mul returned 0 at its throw, add3 ignored the warning, observe never sees it
Console.log(observeMul(2, 3))                             // mul went on / mul 6
Console.log(observeMul(20, 30))                           // observed in observeMul: big product (mul stops at the throw)
Console.log(mul(20, 30))                                  // 0: mul returned at its throw, the warning is ignored here

// to send it up, catch and throw again
fun i64 checked(i64 a, i64 b) {
  return mul(a, b)
  catch Warning w { throw w }
}
fun ->string seeChecked(i64 a, i64 b) {
  return "checked " + checked(a, b)
  catch Warning w { return "observed up the chain: " + w.message }
}
Console.log(seeChecked(2, 3))                             // mul went on / checked 6
Console.log(seeChecked(20, 30))                           // observed up the chain: big product
Console.log(checked(20, 30))                              // nobody observes the rethrow: execution goes on after the call: 0

// catch SilentException and catch all observe silent exceptions; catch Exception does not (spec 15.1, 15.4, 15.8.2)
fun ->string sink(i64 a) {
  return "sink " + mul(a, a)
  catch Exception e { return "Exception sees: " + e.message }
}
fun ->string bySilent(i64 a) {
  return "bySilent " + mul(a, a)
  catch SilentException e { return "SilentException sees: " + e.message }
}
fun ->string byAll(i64 a) {
  return "byAll " + mul(a, a)
  catch all { return "all sees it" }
}
fun ->string wrongKind(i64 a) {
  return "wrongKind " + mul(a, a)
  catch Notice n { return "Notice: " + n.message }
}
Console.log(sink(20))                                     // sink 0: catch Exception does not observe a silent exception
Console.log(bySilent(20))                                 // SilentException sees: big product
Console.log(byAll(20))                                    // all sees it
Console.log(wrongKind(20))                                // wrongKind 0: a catch for another kind does not observe it

// a catch for a parent class below Exception observes the silent children of that class
public class Family : Exception { }
public class Cousin(string text) : Family, SilentException { }
fun i64 cousin(i64 a) {
  if a > 0 then throw Cousin("a cousin")
  return a
}
fun ->string byParent(i64 a) {
  return "byParent " + cousin(a)
  catch Family f { return "Family sees: " + f.message }
}
fun ->string byException(i64 a) {
  return "byException " + cousin(a)
  catch Exception e { return "Exception sees: " + e.message }
}
Console.log(byParent(5))                                  // Family sees: a cousin
Console.log(byParent(0))                                  // byParent 0
Console.log(byException(5))                               // byException 0: catch Exception does not observe it

// a silent exception thrown and caught in the same function
fun ->string sameFunction() {
  Console.log("  before")
  throw Notice("local")
  Console.log("  after the throw")
  return "finished"
  catch Notice n { return "local catch: " + n.message }
}
Console.log(sameFunction())                               // local catch: local

// virtual and trait calls
public class Sensor {
  unrestricted fun i64 read(i64 v) { v }
}
public class Noisy : Sensor {
  replace fun i64 read(i64 v) {
    if v > 5 then throw Notice("noisy")
    return v
  }
}
fun ->string reading(Sensor s, i64 v) {
  return "reading " + s.read(v)
  catch Notice n { return "noise: " + n.message }
}
fun ->string ignoring(Sensor s, i64 v) {
  return "ignoring " + s.read(v)
}
Console.log(reading(Sensor(), 9))                         // reading 9
Console.log(reading(Noisy(), 3))                          // reading 3
Console.log(reading(Noisy(), 9))                          // noise: noisy
Console.log(ignoring(Noisy(), 9))                         // ignoring 0: nobody observes it, read returned at its throw with the zero value

trait Probe {
  unrestricted fun i64 level(i64 v)
}
public class Deep : Probe {
  replace fun i64 level(i64 v) {
    if v < 0 then throw Warning("below zero")
    return v * 2
  }
}
fun ->string probing(Probe p, i64 v) {
  return "level " + p.level(v)
  catch Warning w { return "probe warning: " + w.message }
}
Console.log(probing(Deep(), 4))                           // level 8
Console.log(probing(Deep(), -4))                          // probe warning: below zero

// non-silent exceptions still go up on their own
fun i64 hard(i64 a) {
  if a == 0 then throw Trouble("zero")
  return a
}
fun i64 middleman(i64 a) { hard(a) + 1 }
fun ->string hardCatcher(i64 a) {
  return "hard " + middleman(a)
  catch Trouble t { return "Trouble: " + t.message }
}
Console.log(hardCatcher(3))                               // hard 4
Console.log(hardCatcher(0))                               // Trouble: zero

// catch SilentException e { throw e } passes any silent exception up one level, whatever its class
fun i64 forwardNotice(i64 a) {
  if a > 0 then throw Notice("forwarded notice")
  return a
}
fun i64 forwardWarning(i64 a) {
  if a > 0 then throw Warning("forwarded warning")
  return a
}
fun i64 forwarder(i64 which, i64 a) {
  if which == 0 then return forwardNotice(a)
  return forwardWarning(a)
  catch SilentException e { throw e }
}
fun ->string seeForwarded(i64 which, i64 a) {
  return "forwarded " + forwarder(which, a)
  catch Notice n { return "Notice seen: " + n.message }
  catch Warning w { return "Warning seen: " + w.message }
}
fun ->string ignoreForwarded(i64 which, i64 a) {
  return "ignored " + forwarder(which, a)
}
Console.log(seeForwarded(0, 5))                           // Notice seen: forwarded notice
Console.log(seeForwarded(1, 5))                           // Warning seen: forwarded warning
Console.log(seeForwarded(0, 0))                           // forwarded 0
Console.log(ignoreForwarded(0, 5))                        // ignored 0: nobody observes the re-thrown exception, the caller goes on with the value the function returned (0)
Console.log(ignoreForwarded(1, 5))                        // ignored 0
// Silent operations of the language (spec 15.4): overflow and a cast that does not fit are raised only where a catch of the same function handles them;
// without one the fallback applies in place (the result wraps, the cast saturates) and nothing is raised
import Root

fun ->string addCaught(i8 a, i8 b) {
  i8 sum = a + b
  return $"sum {sum}"
  catch OverflowException { return "overflow caught" }
}
fun i8 addPlain(i8 a, i8 b) { a + b }                       // no catch: wraps
fun ->string mulCaught(i32 a, i32 b) {
  i32 product = a * b
  return $"product {product}"
  catch OverflowException e { return "caught: " + e.message }
}
fun ->string subCaught(u8 a, u8 b) {
  u8 difference = a - b
  return $"difference {difference}"
  catch OverflowException { return "u8 underflow caught" }
}
fun ->string negateCaught(i8 a) {
  i8 negative = -a
  return $"negative {negative}"
  catch OverflowException { return "negation overflow caught" }
}
fun ->string stepCaught(i8 a) {
  i8 b = a
  b++
  return $"step {b}"
  catch OverflowException { return "increment overflow caught" }
}
fun ->string compoundCaught(i16 a) {
  i16 b = a
  b += 30000
  b += 30000
  return $"compound {b}"
  catch OverflowException { return "compound overflow caught" }
}
Console.log(addCaught(100, 20))
Console.log(addCaught(100, 30))
Console.log(addPlain(100, 30))
Console.log(mulCaught(1000, 1000))
Console.log(mulCaught(100000, 100000))
Console.log(subCaught(5, 3))
Console.log(subCaught(3, 5))
Console.log(negateCaught(5))
Console.log(negateCaught(-128))
Console.log(stepCaught(10))
Console.log(stepCaught(127))
Console.log(compoundCaught(1))

// which catches see it: catch Exception does not (the sum wraps), catch SilentException and catch all do
fun ->string byException(i8 a) {
  i8 sum = a + 100
  return $"Exception: wrapped to {sum}"
  catch Exception { return "Exception caught it" }
}
fun ->string bySilent(i8 a) {
  i8 sum = a + 100
  return $"Silent: {sum}"
  catch SilentException { return "SilentException caught it" }
}
fun ->string byAll(i8 a) {
  i8 sum = a + 100
  return $"All: {sum}"
  catch all { return "all caught it" }
}
Console.log(byException(100))
Console.log(bySilent(100))
Console.log(byAll(100))

// the overflow of a called function is not seen by the caller's catch: it stays in the function that does the operation
fun i8 wrapper(i8 a) { a + 100 }
fun ->string callerCatches(i8 a) {
  i8 sum = wrapper(a)
  return $"callee wrapped to {sum}"
  catch OverflowException { return "caller saw it" }
}
Console.log(callerCatches(100))

// casts that do not fit
fun ->string castCaught(i32 a) {
  i8 small = (i8)a
  return $"cast {small}"
  catch OutOfRangeCastException { return "cast out of range" }
}
fun i8 castPlain(i32 a) { (i8)a }
fun ->string castReal(r64 a) {
  u8 small = (u8)a
  return $"real cast {small}"
  catch OutOfRangeCastException { return "real cast out of range" }
}
Console.log(castCaught(100))
Console.log(castCaught(1000))
Console.log(castPlain(1000))
Console.log(castReal(12.7))
Console.log(castReal(300.5))
Console.log(castReal(-3.5))
// A silent throw leaves its function like any throw; the caller ignores it unless a catch around the call handles it (spec 15.4, 15.7.4)
import Root

public class Notice(string text) : Exception, SilentException { }
public class Trouble : Exception { }

fun note(i32 x) {
  if x > 0 then throw Notice("note")
  Console.log("  note finished")                 // not printed when it throws
}
fun callNote(i32 x) {                              // no catch: the notice is ignored here
  note(x)
  Console.log("  callNote goes on")
}
fun ->string seeNote(i32 x) {
  note(x)
  return "no notice"
  catch Notice n { return "saw " + n.message }
}
callNote(0)
callNote(1)
Console.log(seeNote(0))
Console.log(seeNote(1))

// the notice goes one level only: the middle function does not catch it, so the outer one never sees it
fun middle(i32 x) { note(x) }
fun ->string outer(i32 x) {
  middle(x)
  return "outer saw nothing"
  catch Notice n { return "outer saw " + n.message }
}
Console.log(outer(1))

// a function that fails for another reason still ignores the silent one it does not catch
fun i32 mixed(i32 x) {
  note(x)
  if x > 5 then throw Trouble("big")
  return x
}
fun ->string mixedCaller(i32 x) {
  return "mixed " + mixed(x)
  catch Trouble t { return "Trouble: " + t.message }
}
Console.log(mixedCaller(1))
Console.log(mixedCaller(9))

// a value-returning function gives its zero value to a caller that ignores the notice
fun i32 value(i32 x) {
  if x > 0 then throw Notice("value")
  return 42
}
fun i32 plusOne(i32 x) { value(x) + 1 }
Console.log(plusOne(0))
Console.log(plusOne(1))

// at the top level there is no caller above: the throw does nothing
throw Notice("top")
Console.log("top level went on")
namespace sorting
import Root

// sort, sortUnstable, copyFrom on arrays (spec 6.6)
arr<i32> numbers = [5, 3, 9, 1, 7, 3]
numbers.sort()
Console.log(numbers)                                     // [1, 3, 3, 5, 7, 9]
numbers.sort(SortDirection.DESC)
Console.log(numbers)                                     // [9, 7, 5, 3, 3, 1]
numbers.sort(SortDirection.ASC)
Console.log(numbers)                                     // [1, 3, 3, 5, 7, 9]

arr<string> names = ["pear", "Apple", "fig", "apple", "banana", ""]
names.sort()
Console.log(names)                                       // ["", Apple, apple, banana, fig, pear] by code point
names.sortUnstable(SortDirection.DESC)
Console.log(names)                                       // pear, fig, banana, apple, Apple, ""

arr<r64> reals = [2.5, -1.0, 0.0 / 0.0, 10.0, -7.25]
reals.sort()
Console.log(reals)                                       // [-7.25, -1, 2.5, 10, NaN]
arr<char> letters = ['d', 'a', 'c', 'b']
letters.sort()
Console.log(letters)                                     // [a, b, c, d]
arr<u8> bytes = [200, 3, 100, 255, 0]
bytes.sortUnstable()
Console.log(bytes)                                       // [0, 3, 100, 200, 255]
arr<bool> flags = [true, false, true, false]
flags.sort()
Console.log(flags)                                       // [false, false, true, true]
arr<date> dates = [date("2026-03-01"), date("2025-12-31"), date("2026-01-15")]
dates.sort()
Console.log(dates[0])                                    // 2025-12-31T00:00:00.000Z

// many items, both sorts agree with each other
arr<i64> big = arr<i64>[2000]
Random.seed(11ul)
for i32 k = 0..2000 { big[k] = Random.int(-500, 500) }
arr<i64> other = arr<i64>[2000]
other.copyFrom(0, big, 0, 2000)
big.sort()
other.sortUnstable()
bool same = true
bool ascending = true
for i32 k = 0..2000 {
  if big[k] != other[k] { same = false }
  if k > 0 && big[k - 1] > big[k] { ascending = false }
}
Console.log($"stable == unstable: {same}, ascending: {ascending}")   // true, true

// copyFrom
arr<i32> target = [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]
arr<i32> source = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
target.copyFrom(1, source, 0, 3)
Console.log(target)                                      // [0, 1, 2, 3, 0, 0]
target.copyFrom(3, source, 2..5)
Console.log(target)                                      // [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
target.copyFrom(0, source, 1..=2)
Console.log(target)                                      // [2, 3, 2, 3, 4, 5]
target.copyFrom(4, source, ..2)
Console.log(target)                                      // [2, 3, 2, 3, 1, 2]
target.copyFrom(2, source, 3..)
Console.log(target)                                      // [2, 3, 4, 5, 1, 2]
target.copyFrom(6, source, 0, 0)                           // nothing to copy at the end: allowed
Console.log(target.len)                                  // 6

fun ->string tryCopy(&arr<i32> to, arr<i32> from, i32 dst, i32 src, i32 count) {
  to.copyFrom(dst, from, src, count)
  return "copied"
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException ex { return "IndexOutOfRangeException " + ex.id }
  catch ModifyingProtectedSourceException ex { return "ModifyingProtectedSourceException " + ex.id }
}
fun copies(arr<i32> source) {
  arr<i32> dest = [2, 3, 4, 5, 1, 2]
  Console.log(tryCopy(&dest, source, 4, 0, 3))          // IndexOutOfRangeException 19 (does not fit in the destination)
  Console.log(tryCopy(&dest, source, 0, 3, 3))          // IndexOutOfRangeException 19 (does not fit in the source)
  Console.log(tryCopy(&dest, source, 0, -1, 1))         // IndexOutOfRangeException 19
  Console.log(tryCopy(&dest, dest, 0, 1, 2))            // ModifyingProtectedSourceException 20
  Console.log(tryCopy(&dest, source, 6, 0, 0))          // copied
}
copies(source)

// target.copyFrom(0, target, 1, 2)              copyFrom cannot use the same array as source and destination
// names.sort((a, b) { a.len < b.len })          Lambdas ... not supported yet
// arr<r32> small = [1.0]  small.copyFrom(0, numbers, 0, 1)   copyFrom needs an arr<r32> as the source
// The stack trace of a debug build (spec 15.2): innermost first, printed after the message when an exception nobody catches ends the program
namespace stacktrace

  public fun i32 divide(i32 a, i32 b) {
    return a / b
  }
}

  var c = Calc()
  return c.divide(10, x)
}
Console.log(run(2))
Console.log(run(0))
// ex.stackTrace (spec 15.2): the calls copied when the exception was raised, innermost first, as arr<DebugLine>
namespace stacktracecatch
import Root
fun i32 inner(i32 b) {
  return 10 / b
}
fun i32 outer(i32 b) {
  return inner(b)
}
fun show() {
  outer(0)
  catch Exception ex {
    Console.log($"line {ex.line}")
    var trace = ex.stackTrace
    Console.log(trace.len)
    for var item in trace {
      Console.log($"{item.file} ({item.lineNum}) {item.function}")
    }
  }
}

namespace streams
import Root.IO
import Root
alias Root.IO as fs

string base = File.combinePaths(File.getTempDir(), "dflat-streams-test")
File.delete(base, fs.DeleteOptions.Recursive)
File.createDirectory(base)
string path = File.combinePaths(base, "data.bin")

// write: create the file, bytes and single bytes
FileStream out = FileStream(path, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Exclusive, fs.StreamOpenMode.CreateOrTruncate)
Console.log($"out: canRead {out.canRead}, canWrite {out.canWrite}, canSeek {out.canSeek}, position {out.position}")
arr<u8> data = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]
u64 written = out.write(data)
out.writeByte(9)
u64 partial = out.write(data, 2, 3)          // 3, 4, 5
Console.log($"written {written}, partial {partial}, position {out.position}, length {out.length}")
out.flush()
out.close()
out.close()                                    // a second close does nothing
Console.log($"closed: canWrite {out.canWrite}")

// read: whole buffer, parts, end of stream, seek
FileStream input = File(path).open(fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
Console.log($"length {input.length}, canRead {input.canRead}, canWrite {input.canWrite}")
arr<u8> buffer = arr<u8>[6]
u64? got = input.read(buffer)
Console.log($"read {got}: {buffer[0]} {buffer[5]}")
got = input.read(buffer, 2, 3)
Console.log($"read part {got}: {buffer[2]} {buffer[3]} {buffer[4]}")
u64 newPosition = input.seek(-3, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.End)
Console.log($"seek from end: {newPosition}")
u8? one = input.readByte()
Console.log($"readByte {one}")
got = input.read(buffer)
Console.log($"read to the end {got}")
got = input.read(buffer)
u8? last = input.readByte()
Console.log($"at the end: {got == null}, readByte null: {last == null}")
u64 back = input.seek(1, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.Begin)
u8? second = input.readByte()
Console.log($"back to {back}, byte {second}, position {input.position}")
input is atEnd.
Console.log("input ended")

// read and write on one stream, append
FileStream both = FileStream(path, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Exclusive, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Exclusive, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
both.seek(0, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.Begin)
both.writeByte(100)
both.seek(0, fs.StreamSeekOrigin.Begin)
u8? first = both.readByte()
u8? next = both.readByte()
Console.log($"after write at 0: {first}, {next}")
both.close()
FileStream app = FileStream(path, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamOpenMode.AppendExisting)
app.writeByte(200)
app.close()
Console.log($"size after append: {File(path).size}")

// the modes a stream was opened with (read-only properties, spec 6.8.3)
FileStream modes = FileStream(path, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Exclusive, fs.StreamOpenMode.CreateOrOpen)
Console.log($"modes: read {modes.readMode}, write {modes.writeMode}, open {modes.openMode}")   // modes: read Shared, write Exclusive, open CreateOrOpen
Console.log(modes.canRead && modes.canWrite)                                                    // true
modes.close()
Console.log($"after close: {modes.readMode}, {modes.writeMode}")                                // the modes stay: Shared, Exclusive

// errors
fun ->string tryOpen(string p, fs.StreamOpenMode mode) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, mode)
  return "opened"
  catch FileNotFoundException ex { return "FileNotFoundException " + ex.id }
  catch FileAlreadyExistsException ex { return "FileAlreadyExistsException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(tryOpen(File.combinePaths(base, "nothing.bin"), fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting))
Console.log(tryOpen(path, fs.StreamOpenMode.CreateIfMissing))

fun ->string badModes(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.AppendExisting)
  return "opened"
  catch InvalidStreamModesException ex { return "InvalidStreamModesException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(badModes(path))

fun ->string directory(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
  return "opened"
  catch FileAccessToADirectoryException ex { return "FileAccessToADirectoryException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(directory(base))

fun ->string usage(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
  arr<u8> small = arr<u8>[2]
  s.write(small)
  return "wrote"
  catch StreamModeNotAllowedException ex { return "StreamModeNotAllowedException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(usage(path))

fun ->string range(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
  arr<u8> small = arr<u8>[2]
  s.read(small, 1, 5)
  return "read"
  catch InvalidBufferRangeException ex { return "InvalidBufferRangeException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(range(path))

fun ->string closed(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
  s.close()
  s.readByte()
  return "read"
  catch AccessToAClosedStreamException ex { return "AccessToAClosedStreamException " + ex.id }
}
Console.log(closed(path))

// the stream closes at the end of its scope: the file can be deleted afterwards
fun scoped(string p) {
  FileStream s = FileStream(p, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.Shared, fs.StreamReadWriteAccessMode.None, fs.StreamOpenMode.OpenExisting)
  Console.log("in scope, length " + s.length)
}
scoped(path)
Console.log($"deleted after scope: {File.delete(path)}")
File.delete(base, fs.DeleteOptions.Recursive)
namespace strformat
import Root

// string formats (spec 8.3); the expected text is in the comment of each line (| marks the ends)
string word = "Hello"
Console.log($"|{word:w10}|")                        // |Hello     |
Console.log($"|{word:w10a>}|")                      // |     Hello|
Console.log($"|{word:w10a^}|")                      // |  Hello   |
Console.log($"|{word:w10f*a>}|")                    // |*****Hello|
Console.log($"|{word:f.w9a^}|")                     // |..Hello..|
Console.log($"|{word:w3}|")                         // |Hello| (a width never cuts)

// case
string mixed = "hello woRLD. how ARE you"
Console.log(mixed.toStr("cU"))                       // HELLO WORLD. HOW ARE YOU
Console.log(mixed.toStr("cL"))                       // hello world. how are you
Console.log(mixed.toStr("cS"))                       // Hello world. how are you
Console.log(mixed.toStr("cT"))                       // Hello World. How Are You

// trim
string padded = "  \t spaced out  "
Console.log($"|{padded:s}|")                         // |spaced out|
Console.log($"|{padded:sl}|")                        // |spaced out  |
Console.log($"|{padded:sr}|")                        // |  \t spaced out|  (the tab is printed as it is)

// quoted and escaped
string tricky = "He said \"hi\"\n\\ end"
Console.log(tricky.toStr("q"))                       // "He said \"hi\"\n\\ end"
Console.log($"{word:q}")                             // "Hello"

// truncate: the marker counts in the width
string long = "Hello world"
Console.log($"|{long:w8t<'…'}|")                     // |Hello w…|
Console.log($"|{long:w8t>'…'}|")                     // |…o world|
Console.log($"|{long:w8t<'...'}|")                   // |Hello...|
Console.log($"|{long:w20t<'...'}|")                  // |Hello world         |  (short enough: no marker)
Console.log($"|{long:w3t<'....'}|")                  // |...|  (the marker alone, cut to the width)

// display columns
string wide = "日本語"
Console.log($"|{wide:wc8}|")                         // |日本語  |  (6 columns + 2)
Console.log($"|{wide:w8}|")                          // |日本語     |  (3 characters + 5)
Console.log($"|{wide:wc4t<'…'}|")                    // |日…|  (2 + 1 column of the marker, then 1 padding)

// several options, in any order
string raw = "  hello world  "
Console.log($"|{raw:w15cTsa^f_}|")                   // |__Hello World__|  (trim, then title, then center)
Console.log("abc".toStr("w6a>"))                      //    abc

// null is the empty string; a spec that is not valid at run time leaves the text as it is
string nothing = null
Console.log($"|{nothing:w4}|")                       // |    |
string badSpec = "zz"
Console.log(word.toStr(badSpec))                     // Hello

// errors it should give (one at a time):
// "x".toStr("w")            w needs a number (wc for display columns)
// "x".toStr("t<'..'")       t needs a width (w or wc)
// "x".toStr("a.")           a needs <, ^ or >
// "x".toStr("cX")           c needs U, L, S or T
// "x".toStr("f")            f needs a fill character

// Block strings (spec 4.4.4): above 4091 bytes a string is a chain of 4 KB blocks, changed where it is
import Root

// growing past 4091 bytes makes a block string; a borrow follows it
string text = "".padLeft(4000, 'a')
string view = text
for i32 round in 0..300 {
  text += "b"
}
Console.log($"{text.len} {view.len} {text.sizeof} {text == view} {text.endsWith("ab")}")

// reading by position across the blocks, from the start, the end and the middle
string chars = ""
arr<u64> positions = [0, 3999, 4000, 4071, 4072, 4073, 4299]
for u64 p in positions {
  chars += text[p].toStr()
}
Console.log(chars)

// editing in the middle: insert, remove, replaceAt; only the blocks touched change
text.insert(4050, "<MID>")
Console.log($"{text.len} {text.substr(4048, 9)} {view.contains("<MID>")}")
text.remove(4050, 5)
Console.log($"{text.len} {text.substr(4048, 4)}")
text.replaceAt(0, 3, "XYZ")
text.prepend(">>")
text.append("<<")
Console.log($"{text.len} {text.substr(0, 6)} {text.substr(4300, 10)}")

// a big insertion splits the block; a big removal frees blocks
string big = "".padLeft(9000, 'q')
text.insert(10, big)
Console.log($"{text.len} {text[10]} {text[9009]} {text[9010]}")
text.remove(5, 9000)
Console.log($"{text.len} {text.substr(0, 8)}")

// multi-byte characters never straddle two blocks
string wide = "".padLeft(3000, 'é')
Console.log($"{wide.len} {wide.sizeof} {wide[2047]} {wide[2999]}")
wide[1500] = 'x'
wide[2036] = '\u{1F600}'
Console.log($"{wide.len} {wide.sizeof} {wide[1500]} {wide[2036]} {wide[2037]}")

// iteration, comparison, search and printing
i32 count = 0
for char c in wide {
  count += 1
}
string other = wide!
Console.log($"{count} {other == wide} {wide.indexOf("x")} {wide.lastIndexOf('é')}")
Console.log(text.substr((u32)text.len - 12, 12))

// clear keeps a block string; shrink makes it small again
string temp = "".padLeft(5000, 'w')
string tempView = temp
temp.clear()
Console.log($"[{temp}] {tempView.len}")
temp += "small again"
temp.shrink()
Console.log($"{temp} {temp.len} {tempView}")
string longText = "".padLeft(4500, 'k')
longText.remove(100, 4400)
longText.shrink()
Console.log($"{longText.len} {longText.substr(95, 10)}")
// Changes in place (spec 4.1): a borrow sees them; a result used elsewhere is a new string
import Root

class Note {
  public string text
}

// += is always in place: b borrows a, so b sees the change; the string moves to bigger classes and b follows
string a = "start" + "!"
string b = a
for i32 round in 0..100 {
  a += "x"
}
Console.log($"{a.len} {b.len} {a == b}")
b += "-b"
Console.log(a.endsWith("-b"))

// a result not used, or given back to the same string: in place
string s = "  Hello " + "World  "
string view = s
s.trim()
Console.log($"[{view}]")
s = s.toUpper()
Console.log(view)
view = view.substr(0, 5).toLower()
Console.log(s)

// a result used elsewhere: a new string, the original is unchanged
string original = "keep me"
string other = original.toUpper()
Console.log($"{original} / {other}")

// an alias: string same = text, then text = same.trim() works in place
string text = " ali" + "ased "
string same = text
text = same.trim()
Console.log($"[{text}] [{same}]")

// fields and array items
Note n = Note()
n.text = "field"
n.text += " grows"
n.text.toUpper()
Console.log(n.text)
arr<string> items = ["one", "two"]
items[1] += "!"
items[0].toUpper()
Console.log(items)

// a literal is never written: the variable gets a string of its own
string lit = "literal"
string litView = lit
lit += "!"
Console.log($"{lit} {litView}")

// text[pos] = c, same size and different sizes
string chars = "hel" + "lo"
string charsView = chars
chars[0] = 'J'
chars[4] = 'é'
chars[1] = '\u{1F600}'
Console.log($"{chars} {charsView} {chars.len} {chars.sizeof}")
{
  chars[99] = 'x'
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException {
    Console.log("index out of range")
  }
}

// Strings in collections (spec 4, 6): an item is always its own copy; the collection frees its strings
import Root

// a copy: changing the source later does not change the item
string source = "so" + "urce"
vec<string> words = ["first"]
words.add(source)
list<string> chain = ["one"]
chain.add(source)
arr<string> slots = [source, "lit"]
source += "!"
Console.log($"{words} {chain} {slots} {source}")

// add, insert, items written again, removed
for i32 round in 0..50 {
  words.add("w" + round.toStr())
  chain.add("c" + round.toStr())
}
words.insert(0, "zero")
chain.insert(1, "between")
words[1] = words[2]
words[2] += "+"
chain[0] = "ONE"
chain[3] += "+"
words.removeAt(5)
words.remove("w10")
chain.removeAt(4)
chain.removeAll("c20")
words.truncate(10)
chain.truncate(10)
Console.log($"{words.len} {words}")
Console.log($"{chain.len} {chain}")

// fill copies the value into every item; clear empties them
words.fill("same" + "!")
chain.fill(3, "x")
slots.fill("filled")
Console.log($"{words[0]} {words[9]} {chain} {slots}")
slots.clear()
Console.log(slots)

// pop, ->c[i] and replace give the item to the receiver
vec<string> stack = ["a", "b" + "b", "c" + "c"]
string top = ""
bool popped = stack.pop(&top)
string moved = ->stack[0]
string old = stack.replace(1, "new")
Console.log($"{popped} {top} {moved} {old} {stack}")

// resize, fillAtCapacity, sort, swap

sized.resize(4, "r")
sized.sort()
sized.swap(0, 3)

// add(collection) copies, add(->other) moves; copyFrom copies; transformTo moves

vec<string> target = ["t"]
target.add(more)
more[0] += "*"
vec<string> given = ["g1", "g2"]
target.add(->given)
arr<string> plain = ["a1", "a2", "a3"]
target.copyFrom(1, plain, 0, 2)
plain[0] += "#"
list<string> asList = target.transformTo<list>()
Console.log($"{more} {asList} {plain}")

// string? (spec 7.1): null is not ""; variables, parameters and results
namespace stringopt
import Root

fun ->string? find(u32 k) {
  if k == 0 {
    return null
  }
  if k == 1 {
    return ""
  }
  string? found = "item " + k.toStr()
  return found
}

fun ->string describe(string? s) {
  if s == null {
    return "null"
  }
  return $"[{s}]"
}

string? a = null
string? b = ""
string? c = "abc"
Console.log($"{a} {b} {c}")
Console.log($"{a == null} {b == null} {b == ""} {c == "abc"}")
string s = a
string t = c
Console.log($"[{s}] {t}")
Console.log($"{a ?? "default"} [{b ?? "default"}] {find(0) ?? "none"}")
Console.log(find(1) == null)
Console.log(find(2))
Console.log($"{describe(a)} {describe(b)} {describe("x".toUpper())} {describe(find(3))}")
string base = "mine"
string? d = base
d = "other" + base
Console.log(d)
d = find(4)
Console.log(d)
d = null
Console.log(d)
d ??= "set"
d ??= "not set"
Console.log(d)
string? e = d ?? null
Console.log(e)
for u32 i in 0..3 {
  string? x = find(i)
  Console.log(x)
}
Console.log($"{c?.len} {a?.len} {c?.toUpper()}")
enforce c {
  Console.log($"enforced {c} {c.len}")
}
enforce a {
  Console.log("no")
} else {
  Console.log("a is null")
}
// string? (spec 7.1) in fields and in the items of arr, vec and list: each owns its string; the end of the object or collection frees it
namespace stringoptitems
import Root

public class Person(string name) {
  public string name
  public string? nick
  public string? note = "none"
  unrestricted fun ->string show() { $"{name} {nick ?? "-"} {note}" }
  public fun rename(string? n) { nick = n }
}

fun keep(&Person p, string text) {
  p.nick = text.toUpper()
}

fun fields() {
  Person a = Person("ann")
  Console.log(a.show())
  a.nick = "annie"
  a.rename(null)
  Console.log($"{a.nick == null} {a.show()}")
  a.note = ""
  Console.log($"[{a.note}] {a.note == null}")
  keep(&a, "x")
  Person b = a!
  b.nick = "copy"
  Console.log($"{a.show()} / {b.show()}")
  Console.log(a)
}

fun arrays() {
  string own = "own".toUpper()
  string? maybe = null
  arr<string?> a = ["x", null, "", own, maybe]
  a[0] = "y".toUpper()
  a[1] = own
  a[2] = null
  Console.log($"{a} {a[0] == "Y"} {a[2] == null}")
  for string? s in a {
    Console.print($"{s ?? "_"};")
  }
  Console.log("")
  arr<string?> b = a!
  b[0] = "z"
  Console.log($"{a[0]} {b[0]}")
  a.fill("f")
  a.clear()
  arr<string?> c = [null, "q"]
  b.copyFrom(0, c, 0, 2)
  Console.log($"{a} {b} {b.has(null)} {b.indexOf("q")}")
}

fun vecs() {
  string own = "own".toUpper()
  vec<string?> v = ["x", null]
  v.add(own)
  v.add(null)
  v.add("t".toUpper())
  v[1] = "now"
  v.insert(0, null)
  string? last
  v.pop(&last)
  v.removeAt(0)
  vec<string?> w = v.clone()
  w.add(null)
  Console.log($"{last} {v} {w.len} {w.filter((string? s) { s != null })}")
}

fun lists() {
  list<string?> l = ["x", null]
  l.add("own".toUpper())
  l.insert(1, "in".toUpper())
  l[0] = null
  l.removeAt(0)
  list<string?> m = l.clone()
  vec<string?> v = [null, "v"]
  m.add(v)
  l.fill("f")
  arr<string?> dest = [null, null]
  dest.copyFrom(0, m, 0, 2)
  Console.log($"{l} {m} {dest} {m.map<string>((string? s) { s ?? "-" })}")
}

arrays()
vecs()

﻿// Ownership of strings (spec 4, 12): b = a is the same string, a! copies, the owner frees at the end of the scope
import Root

class Person {
  public string name
  public string tag
}

fun ->string greet(string who) {
  return "Hello " + who
}

fun string keep(string who) {
  return who
}

fun ->string upperName(Person p) {
  p.name.toUpper()
}

fun string retag(string text) {
  text = text + "!"
  return text
}

fun ->string, u64 pair(string text) {
  string local = text + "?"
  return local, local.len
}

// an exception in the middle of a statement that made temporaries
fun i32 failing(string text) {
  if text.len > 3 then throw IndexOutOfRangeException()
  return (i32)text.len
}
i32 total = 0
for i32 round in 0..5 {
  total += failing("ab" + round.toStr()) + failing(("x" + round.toStr() + (round >= 3 ? { "yy", "" })).trim())
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException {
    total += 100
  }
}
Console.log(total)

// ?? on strings, generics, a return from inside a loop
string blank = ""
string named = blank ?? "anon" + "ymous"
fun Item same<Item>(Item value) { value }
string generic = same("gen" + "eric")
fun ->string firstLong(arr<string> words) {
  for string w in words {
    string upper = w.toUpper()
    if upper.len > 3 {
      return upper
    }
  }
  return "none"
}
Console.log($"{named} {generic} {firstLong(["ab", "abcd", "abcdef"])}")

namespace structbuffers
import Root

const i32 NAME_SIZE = 8

struct Matrix {
  arr<r32>[4] m = [1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0]

  public fun scale(r32 k) {
    for i32 i in 0..4 {
      m[i] = m[i] * k
    }
  }
  public unrestricted fun r32 trace() { m[0] + m[3] }
}

struct Device { u32 id, arr<byte>[NAME_SIZE] name }

struct Point { i32 x, i32 y }

struct Path { arr<Point>[3] points, i32 count }

struct Huge { arr<u64>[20000] data, i32 tag = 7 }

fun r32 total(Matrix x) {
  r32 sum = 0.0
  for r32 v in x.m {
    sum += v
  }
  return sum
}

fun i64 hugeSum(Huge h) {
  i64 sum = 0
  for u64 v in h.data {
    sum += (i64)v
  }
  return sum
}

var a = Matrix()
Console.log(a)
Console.log(a.m.len)
a.m[1] = 5.0
a.scale(2.0)
Console.log(a.m)
Console.log(a.trace())
Console.log(total(a))
Matrix b = a
b.m[0] = 100.0
Console.log($"{a.m[0]} {b.m[0]} {a == b}")
b = a
Console.log(a == b)
b.m = [9.0, 8.0, 7.0, 6.0]
Console.log(b.m)
a.m = b.m
Console.log(a.m)
var d = Device(id: 3, name: [68b, 102b, 108b, 97b, 116b, 0b, 0b, 0b])
Console.log(d.name[1])
var p = Path(count: 2)
p.points[1].x = 4
p.points[2] = Point(7, 8)
Console.log(p.points)
Huge h
h.data[19999] = 42
h.data[0] = 1
Console.log($"{h.tag} {h.data.len} {hugeSum(h)}")
var h2 = Huge(tag: 9)
Console.log(h2.tag)
for i32 i in 0..3 {
  Huge each
  each.data[0] = (u64)i
  Console.log(each.data[0])
}
// arr, vec and list of structs that hold strings or objects (spec 11.1.5.3, 6.6): the collection owns them as it owns tuples of
// objects; a copy of an item clones them, a removed or replaced item ends them, and they end with the collection
namespace structitems
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name = name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
struct Pet { Dog dog, string tag, i32 age }
fun run() {
  vec<Pet> v = [Pet(Dog("v1"), "a", 1), Pet(Dog("v2"), "b", 2)]
  list<Pet> l = [Pet(Dog("l1"), "c", 3)]
  arr<Pet> a = [Pet(Dog("a1"), "d", 4)]
  Pet p = Pet(Dog("p1"), "e", 5)
  v.add(p!)
  p.dog.name = "P1"
  Console.log($"{v[2].dog.name} {p.dog.name} {v.len}")
  v[0] = Pet(Dog("v3"), "f", 6)
  Pet t = ->v[1]
  Pet u = l.takeAt(0)
  Console.log($"took {t.dog.name}")
  v.removeAt(0)
  vec<Pet> c = v.clone()
  Console.log($"{c[1].dog.name} {c.len} {u.tag} {l.len} {a[0].age}")
  Console.log(v)
  Console.log("end of run")
}
