// structs that hold strings and objects as keys (spec 6.2, 6.2.2, 11.1.5.3): the collection keeps a clone (its strings and objects) and
// ends it with the entry; an object field is ordered by its hash and an hmap compares it with the equals of its class
import Root

class Tag(string name) : Keyable {
  public string name
  replace fun uns get_hash() { 7u }                 // every tag has the same hash
  replace fun bool equals(T other) {
    if T is Tag {
      return name == other.name
    }
    return false
  }
}

struct Named {
  string name,
  i32 n
}

struct Tagged {
  Tag tag,
  i32 n
}

map<Named, string> names = []
names[Named("b", 1)] = "b1"
names[Named("a", 2)] = "a2"
names[Named("a", 1)] = "a1"
names[Named("a", 1)] = "A1"                       // the same key: the value changes
Console.log(names.len)
for (Named k, string v) in names {
  Console.log($"  {k.name} {k.n} {v}")
}
Named look = Named("b", 1)
Console.log($"{names[look]} {names.hasKey(Named("c", 1))}")
names.removeAt(Named("a", 2))
Console.log(names.len)

hmap<Named, i32> counts = []
counts[Named("x", 1)] = 10
counts[Named("x", 2)] = 20
counts[Named("x", 1)] += 5
Console.log($"{counts.len} {counts[Named("x", 1)]} {counts.hasKey(Named("y", 1))}")
counts.removeAt(Named("x", 2))
Console.log(counts.len)

hmap<Tagged, i32> tags = []
tags[Tagged(Tag("a"), 1)] = 1
tags[Tagged(Tag("b"), 1)] = 2                     // same hash, equals says different: two keys
tags[Tagged(Tag("a"), 1)] += 10
Console.log($"{tags.len} {tags[Tagged(Tag("a"), 1)]} {tags[Tagged(Tag("b"), 1)]} {tags.hasKey(Tagged(Tag("c"), 1))}")

set<Tagged> ordered = []
ordered.add(Tagged(Tag("a"), 2))
ordered.add(Tagged(Tag("a"), 1))
Console.log(ordered.len)
for Tagged t in ordered {
  Console.log($"  {t.tag.name} {t.n}")
}
ordered.remove(Tagged(Tag("a"), 2))
Console.log(ordered.len)

string prefix = "long-prefix-"
names[Named(prefix + "z", 9)] = "z9"             // a computed string: the map keeps its own copy
Console.log($"{names[Named("long-prefix-z", 9)]} {names.len}")
namespace structmethods
import Root

struct Vec2 {
  r32 x,
  r32 y

  public unrestricted fun r32 lenSq() { x * x + y * y }
  public fun scale(r32 k) {
    x *= k
    this.y = this.y * k
  }
  public fun double() { scale(2.0) }
  public unrestricted fun Vec2 plus(Vec2 o) { Vec2(x + o.x, y + o.y) }
  public fun reset() { this = Vec2() }
  fun r32 secret() { 42.0 }
  public unrestricted fun r32 peek() { secret2() }
  unrestricted fun r32 secret2() { 7.0 }
}

struct Seg { Vec2 a, Vec2 b
  public fun stretch() { b.scale(3.0) }
}

class Ship {
  public Vec2 pos
}

fun r32 measure(Vec2 v) { v.lenSq() }

fun push(&Vec2 v) { v.double() }

var p = Vec2(3.0, 4.0)
Console.log(p.lenSq())
p.scale(2.0)
Console.log(p)
p.double()
Console.log(p)
Console.log(Vec2(1.0, 1.0).plus(p))
Console.log(measure(p))
push(&p)
Console.log(p)
p.reset()
Console.log(p)
var s = Seg(b: Vec2(1.0, 2.0))
s.stretch()
s.a.scale(5.0)
Console.log(s)
arr<Vec2>[2] items
items[1] = Vec2(1.0, 1.0)
items[1].scale(4.0)
Console.log(items)
var ship = Ship()
ship.pos = Vec2(2.0, 0.0)
ship.pos.double()
Console.log(ship.pos)
Console.log(p.peek())
Console.log(Vec2(2.0, 2.0).lenSq())
namespace structobjects
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

struct Pet {
  string label = "pet",
  Dog dog,
  i32 age

  public fun rename(string n) { label = n }
  public unrestricted fun ->string describe() { $"{label} {age}" }
  public static fun ->string kind() { "a pet" }
}

struct Owner { string who, Pet pet }

class Home {
  public Pet pet
}

fun Pet make(string n) { Pet(label: n, dog: Dog(n)) }

fun show(Pet p) { Console.log("show " + p.label) }

fun keep(->Pet p) { Console.log("keep " + p.label) }

fun Pet back(Pet p) { p }

Console.log("1 creation")
Pet a = Pet(label: "a", dog: Dog("rex"))
Console.log(a)
Console.log("2 copy clones")
Pet b = a
b.label = "b"
b.dog.name = "rex2"
Console.log($"{a.label} {a.dog.name} {b.label} {b.dog.name}")
Console.log("3 assign drops the old")
b = make("max")
Console.log(b.describe())
Console.log("4 field replaced")
b.dog = Dog("bo")
Console.log("5 lend and temporaries")
show(a)
show(make("tmp"))
Console.log(make("tmp2").label)
Console.log(make("tmp3").describe())
make("dropped")
Console.log("6 move")
keep(->b)
Console.log($"b after move: {b.label} {b.dog == null}")
keep(a!)
Console.log("7 nested and class field")
Owner o = Owner(who: "ann", pet: make("nest"))
o.pet.rename("nest2")
Console.log(o)
var h = Home()
h.pet = make("home")
Pet c = back(a)
Console.log(c.label)
Console.log("8 static")
Console.log(Pet.kind())
Console.log("9 end of program")
namespace structobjects2
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

struct Pet {
  string label,
  Dog dog,
  string? note

  public fun reset() { this = Pet(label: "reset", dog: Dog("fresh")) }
}

struct Owner { string who, Pet pet }

class Home {
  public Pet pet
}

fun Pet make(string n) { Pet(label: n, dog: Dog(n)) }

fun swapIn(&Pet p) { p = make("in") }

fun string labelOf(Pet p) { p.label }

Console.log("1 self assign")
Pet a = make("a")
a = a
Console.log(a.dog.name)
Console.log("2 & parameter")
swapIn(&a)
Console.log(a.label)
Console.log("3 this replaced")
a.reset()
Console.log(a.label)
Console.log("4 nested replaced")
Owner o = Owner(who: "o", pet: make("p1"))
o.pet = make("p2")
o.pet.dog = Dog("p3")
Console.log("5 string fields")
string name = "named"
a.label = name
name = "changed"
Console.log(a.label)
a.note = "note"
Console.log(a.note)
Console.log("6 class clone")
var h = Home()
h.pet = make("h1")
Home h2 = h!
h2.pet.dog.name = "h2"
Console.log($"{h.pet.dog.name} {h2.pet.dog.name}")
Console.log("7 loop")
for i32 i in 0..2 {
  Pet t = make($"loop{i}")
  Console.log(labelOf(t))
}
Console.log("8 copy")
Pet c = a!
c.dog.name = "copy"
Console.log($"{a.dog.name} {c.dog.name}")
Console.log("9 end")
namespace structs
import Root

// spec 11.1.5, step 1: declaration, creation, start values, fields, locals, parameters, results

struct Vec3 {
  r32 x,
  r32 y,
  r32 z = -1.0
}

struct Box { Vec3 min, Vec3 max, i32 id = 7, bool on }

struct Big { i32 a, i32 b, i32 c, i32 d }

struct Stamp { date when, i32? count, char mark = 'k' }

fun Vec3 add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) {
  return Vec3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z)
}

fun grow(&Vec3 v) {
  v.x = v.x * 2.0
  v.z++
}

fun r32 sum(Vec3 v) { v.x + v.y + v.z }

fun i32 bump(&i32 counter) {
  counter++
  return counter
}

class Holder {
  public Vec3 p
}

// creation by name: the other fields take their start value or zero
var s = Vec3(x: 2.0)
Console.log($"{s.x} {s.y} {s.z}")
Vec3 zero = Vec3()
Console.log(zero)
// creation by position: all the fields, in order
Vec3 t = Vec3(1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
Console.log(t)

// parameters and results by value
var u = add(s, t)
Console.log(u)
Console.log(sum(u))

// = copies
Vec3 c = u
c.x = 100.0
Console.log($"{u.x} {c.x}")

// & parameter changes the caller's struct
grow(&c)
Console.log($"{c.x} {c.z}")

// structs inside structs are stored inline
var b = Box()
Console.log($"{b.id} {b.on} {b.min.z} {b.max.x}")
b.max.y += 5.0
b.min = Vec3(z: 9.0)
Console.log(b)

// a struct field of a class
var h = Holder()
Console.log(h.p.z)
h.p.x = 4.0
Console.log(h)

// arrays and vecs of structs: the items take the start values
arr<Vec3>[3] points
points[1].y = 6.0
points[2] = Vec3(7.0, 8.0, 9.0)
Console.log(points)
var v = vec<Vec3>()
v.add(Vec3(x: 1.0))
v[0].z += 10.0
Console.log(v)
var literal = [Vec3(y: 1.0), Vec3(y: 2.0)]
Console.log(literal[1].y)

// printing: the first 3 fields, then ...
Console.log(Big(1, 2, 3, 4).toStr())
Console.log(Stamp(count: 3))

// == field by field
Console.log($"{Vec3(1.0, 2.0, 3.0) == t} {Vec3(x: 1.0) == t} {b == b}")

// the values are computed in the order they are written
i32 calls = 0
var ordered = Big(d: bump(&calls), a: bump(&calls))
Console.log($"{ordered.a} {ordered.d}")
namespace structtransmute
import Root

struct Pair { i16 low, i16 high }
struct Whole {
  u32 value
  public fun clear() { value = 0 }
}
struct<compact> Rgba { u8 r, u8 g, u8 b, u8 a }
struct Holder { Pair p, i32 tag }

fun u32 asWhole(Pair p) { ([Whole]p).value }

fun Pair make() { Pair(1, 1) }

Pair pair = Pair(1, 2)
Console.log(([Whole]pair).value)
([Whole]pair).value = 5
Console.log(pair)
Whole copy = [Whole]pair
copy.value = 7
Console.log($"{pair.low} {copy.value}")
Console.log(asWhole(pair))
([Whole]pair).clear()
Console.log(pair)
u32 bits = (u32)16909060
Console.log([Rgba]bits)
([Rgba]bits).a = 255
Console.log(bits)
Console.log([u32]Rgba(1, 0, 0, 0))
var h = Holder(tag: 3)
([Whole]h.p).value = 65537
Console.log(h)
Console.log(([Whole]make()).value)
arr<Pair>[2] pairs
([Whole]pairs[1]).value = 131074
Console.log(pairs)
namespace structtuples
import Root

struct Vec2 { r32 x, r32 y }
struct Flip { r32 b, r32 a }
struct Size { i32 w, i32 h }
struct Named { string name, i32 age }
struct Grid { arr<u8>[3] cells, i32 tag }

class Sprite {
  public pos { r32 x, r32 y }
}

fun i32, i32 bounds() { return Size(3, 4) }

fun (string n, i32 a) person() { Named(name: "ann", age: 30) }

fun Vec2 make() { Vec2(5.0, 6.0) }

var s = Sprite()
s.pos = Vec2(12.5, 5.0)
Console.log($"{s.pos.x} {s.pos.y}")
var f = Flip(1.0, 2.0)
s.pos = f
Console.log($"{s.pos.x} {s.pos.y}")
s.pos = make()
Console.log($"{s.pos.x} {s.pos.y}")
(r32 x, r32 y) t = Vec2(7.0, 8.0)
Console.log(t)
r32 a, r32 b = f
Console.log($"{a} {b}")
i32 w, i32 h = bounds()
Console.log($"{w} {h}")
var p = person()
Console.log(p)
Named n = Named(name: "bob", age: 4)
string who, i32 age = n
n.name = "changed"
Console.log($"{who} {age} {n.name}")
Console.log(Vec2(1.0, 2.0).hash == Vec2(1.0, 2.0).hash)
Console.log(Vec2(1.0, 2.0).hash == Vec2(2.0, 1.0).hash)
Console.log(n.hash == Named(name: "changed", age: 4).hash)
var g = Grid(cells: [1b, 2b, 3b])
var g2 = g
g2.cells[2] = 9b
Console.log(g.hash == g2.hash)
namespace switches
import Root

enum Color { Red, Green, Blue }

fun ->string classify(i32 value) {
  string result = "?"
  switch value {
    1: result = "one", 2: result = "two"
    3, 4, 5: {
      result = "three to five"
    }
    default: result = "other"
  }
  return result
}

for i32 i = 0..7 {
  Console.log($"classify({i}) = {classify(i)}")
}

// jumps between cases, by value and to default
fun ->string walk(i32 start) {
  string path = ""
  switch start {
    1: {
      path += "1 "
      jump 3
    }
    2: {
      path += "2 "
      jump default
    }
    3: {
      path += "3 "
    }
    default: path += "default "
  }
  return path
}
Console.log("walk(1) = " + walk(1))
Console.log("walk(2) = " + walk(2))

// enum subject: entries without the enum name, jump by entry name
Color paint = Color.Green
switch paint {
  Red: Console.log("red")
  Green: {
    Console.log("green, then...")
    jump Blue
  }
  Blue: Console.log("blue")
}

// strings, labels out of a switch, break of the enclosing loop
string word = "beta"
switch word {
  "alpha": Console.log("first")
  "beta": jump #after#
  default: Console.log("unknown")
}
Console.log("not printed")
#after#
Console.log("jumped to #after#")

i32 seen = 0
for i32 n = 0..10 {
  switch n {
    4: break
    default: seen++
  }
}
Console.log($"loop stopped at 4, seen = {seen}")

// the subject is evaluated once
i32 calls = 0
fun i32 next(i32 value) { value + 1 }
switch next(calls) {
  1: Console.log("next(0) is 1")
}

// switch expressions
for i32 i = 0..4 {
  bool odd = i ? { 1, 3: true, default: false }
  string name = i ? { 0: "zero", 1: "one", default: "many" }
  Console.log($"{i}: odd = {odd}, name = {name}")
}
bool flag = true
string yesNo = flag ? { "yes", "no" }
Console.log($"flag ? yes/no = {yesNo}")
i32 count = 0
Console.log($"count ? = {count ? { "some", "none" }}")
string hue = paint ? { Red: "warm", Green: "natural", Blue: "cold" }
Console.log($"hue = {hue}")
i64 wide = flag ? { 1, 2l }
Console.log($"wide = {wide}")
r64 mixed = flag ? { 1, 2.5 }
Console.log($"mixed = {mixed}")
// A new object that nothing keeps ends when its statement ends (point 85): an argument to a borrowing parameter, the receiver of a call or
// of a field read. One kept by a variable, a field or a ->T parameter is not a temporary
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
  public fun ->string bark() { name + " barks" }
  public fun Dog same() { this }
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}

fun ->string show(Dog d) { "show " + d.name }
fun ->string both(Dog a, Dog b) { a.name + " and " + b.name }
fun ->Dog make(string name) { Dog(name) }
fun keep(->Dog d) { Console.log("  kept " + d.name) }

Console.log("argument")
Console.log(show(Dog("a")))
Console.log("two arguments")
Console.log(both(Dog("b"), make("c")))
Console.log("receiver of a call")
Console.log(make("d").bark())
Console.log("receiver of a field")
Console.log(Dog("e").name)
Console.log("borrowed result used in the same statement")
Console.log(Dog("f").same().bark())
Console.log("in a loop")
for i32 i = 0..2 {
  Console.log(show(Dog($"loop {i}")))
}
Console.log("kept, not a temporary")
Dog g = Dog("g")
keep(Dog("h"))
Console.log("done")
// Trait constructors with parameters (spec 11.1.1, 11.2, step 3): a constructor of the class calls them in its chain,
// : Trait(args); overloads; the property initializers of the trait run before its constructor body; order parent, traits, class
import Root

trait Named {
  string name
  public string trail
  Named(string name) {
    this.name = name
    trail += "Named(s)"
  }
  Named(string first, string last) {
    name = first + " " + last
    trail += "Named(s,s)"
  }
  public fun ->string title() { "~" + name + "~" }
}

trait Scored {
  i32 score
  Scored() {
    score = 1
  }
  Scored(i32 start) {
    score = start
  }
  public fun i32 points() { score }
}

// a trait with a constructor without parameters only: it runs by itself
trait Stamped {
  i32 stamp
  Stamped() { stamp = 77 }
  public fun i32 when() { stamp }
}

public class Player : Named, Scored, Stamped {
  public i32 level = 1

  public Player(string name) : Named(name) {
    trail += ">Player(s)"
  }
  public Player(string first, string last, i32 points) : Named(first, last), Scored(points) {
    trail += ">Player(s,s,i)"
  }
  public Player() : Player("anon") {
    level = 0
  }
  public fun ->string show() { $"{title()} {points()} {when()} {level} {trail}" }
}

// a parent with a constructor, a trait with parameters in the child
public class Base {
  public string from = "?"
  public Base(string from) { this.from = from }
}
public class Hero : Base, Named {
  public Hero(string name) : Base("base"), Named(name) { }
  public fun ->string show() { $"{title()} {from} {trail}" }
}

// the trait constructor fails: the exception reaches the caller
trait Positive {
  i32 v
  Positive(i32 v) {
    if v < 0 then throw Exception("negative")
    this.v = v
  }
  public fun i32 value() { v }
}
public class Amount : Positive {
  public Amount(i32 v) : Positive(v) { }
}

Console.log(Player("Ann").show())
Console.log(Player("Bob", "Lee", 9).show())
Console.log(Player().show())
Console.log(Hero("Zed").show())
fun ->string tryAmount(i32 v) {
  Amount a = Amount(v)
  return "made " + a.value().toStr()
  catch Exception ex {
    return "failed: " + ex.message
  }
}
Console.log(tryAmount(4))
Console.log(tryAmount(-4))
// replacing a trait property (spec 11.2): a property without accessor blocks is a simple field in every class; a class can replace it
// (field is the storage of the trait); once replaced, the code of the trait and the reads through the trait type call the accessors of
// the class of the object; a property no class replaces stays a direct memory access
import Root
trait Shape {
  public i32 sides
  public i32 other
  Shape() { sides = 3 }
  unrestricted fun i32 doubled() { sides * 2 }
}
class Square : Shape {
  replace public i32 sides { R: { field + 1 } }
}
class Tri : Shape { }
fun i32 sidesOf(Shape s) { s.sides }

trait Sized {
  public readonly u32 count
}
class Bag : Sized {
  public fun fill() { count = 7 }
}
fun u32 countOf(Sized s) { s.count }

Square q = Square()
Tri t = Tri()
Console.log($"{q.sides} {t.sides}")              // 4 3: the R: of Square, the plain field of Tri
Console.log($"{sidesOf(q)} {sidesOf(t)}")        // 4 3: through the trait type
Console.log($"{q.doubled()} {t.doubled()}")      // 8 6: the code of the trait reads the replacement
q.sides = 10                                       // the default W: of the replacement
t.sides = 5
Console.log($"{q.sides} {t.sides}")              // 11 5
q.other = 2
Console.log(q.other)
Bag b = Bag()
b.fill()
Console.log($"{b.count} {countOf(b)}")           // 7 7
// Classes, phase 3: traits (properties, constructor, default methods, parent trait, dispatch)
import Root

trait Named {
  public string name = "unnamed"
  unrestricted fun string Label()                    // must be implemented; only reads (spec 1.6.2)
  unrestricted fun ->string Hello() { "Hi " + Label() + " (" + name + ")" }
}

trait Shape : Named {
  public i32 sides
  Shape() { sides = 3 }
  unrestricted fun r64 Area()                       // only reads (spec 1.6.2)
  unrestricted fun ->string Info() { Hello() + " area " + Area() + " sides " + sides }
}

public class Square : Shape {
  public r64 side = 2.0
  replace fun string Label() { "square" }
  replace fun r64 Area() { side * side }
  public fun SetName(string n) { name = n }
}

public class Circle : Shape {
  public r64 radius = 1.0
  replace fun string Label() { "circle" }
  replace fun r64 Area() { 3.0 * radius * radius }
  replace fun string Hello() { "Hello from the circle" }
}

public class Base {
  public fun string Label() { "base label" }
}

public class Derived : Base, Named {
  public i32 extra = 5
}

public class Loud : Square {
  replace fun string Label() { "LOUD" }
}

// values of a trait type: dynamic dispatch
fun ->string describe(Shape s) { s.Info() }
fun string greet(Named n) { n.Hello() }

Square sq = Square()
Circle ci = Circle()
Console.log(sq.Label())
Console.log(sq.Hello())
Console.log(sq.Info())
Console.log(ci.Hello())
Console.log(ci.Info())
Console.log(sq.sides)
sq.SetName("my square")
Console.log(sq.name)
Console.log(describe(sq))
Console.log(describe(ci))
Console.log(greet(sq))
Console.log(greet(ci))

// a class that gets the function from its parent class
Derived d = Derived()
Console.log(d.Hello())
Console.log(greet(d))
Console.log(d.name)
Console.log(d.extra)

// a subclass overriding a function that answers a trait
Loud loud = Loud()
loud.side = 3.0
Console.log(describe(loud))
Console.log(greet(loud))

// properties through a trait-typed value
&Shape s = ci                    // a read/write borrow: its properties are written
Console.log(s.sides)
s.sides = 8
Console.log(ci.sides)
s.name = "renamed"
Console.log(ci.name)
Console.log(s.Hello())

// a mixed array of any shape
Loud loud2 = Loud()
loud2.side = 3.0
arr<Shape> shapes = [Square(), Circle(), ->loud2]   // an array owns its items: new objects, or moved in with ->
r64 total = 0.0
for Shape item in shapes { total += item.Area() }
Console.log(total)
for var item in shapes { Console.print(item.Label() + ";") }
Console.log("")

// a trait value assigned to the parent trait
Named n = s
Console.log(n.Label())
n = d
Console.log(n.Label())
Named none = null
Console.log(none == null)

// is: with a class, and with a trait
Console.log(s is Circle)
Console.log(s is Square)
Console.log(sq is Shape)
Console.log(d is Shape)
Console.log(d is Named)
Console.log(n is Base)
arr<Named> mixed = [Square(), Derived(), Circle()]   // new objects: the variables keep theirs
for Named item in mixed {
  if item is Shape sh {
    Console.log("shape " + sh.Area())
  } else if item is Base b {
    Console.log("base " + b.Label())
  }
}

// null access through a trait
i32 caught = 0
none.Hello()
catch NullPointerException {
  caught += 1
}
Console.log(caught)
Console.log(sq)
// Traits: the same member in two traits, qualified names, trait constructors and construction order
import Root

trait TraitA {
  public string tag = "A"
  public fun string Who() { "A's who" }
  public fun string OnlyA() { "only A" }
}
trait TraitB {
  public string tag = "B"
  public fun string Who() { "B's who" }
}
trait Counted {
  public i32 made
  Counted() { made = 100 }
}

public class Both : TraitA, TraitB {
  replace fun ->string Who() { "both: " + TraitA.Who() + " / " + TraitB.Who() + " / " + TraitA.tag + TraitB.tag }
}

public class Plain : TraitA, TraitB {
  public i32 x
}

public class Order : Counted {
  public i32 value = made + 1
}

Both b = Both()
Console.log(b.Who())
Console.log(b.TraitA.Who())
Console.log(b.TraitB.Who())
Console.log(b.TraitA.tag)
Console.log(b.TraitB.tag)
b.TraitB.tag = "changed"
Console.log(b.TraitB.tag)
Console.log(b.TraitA.tag)
Console.log(b.OnlyA())

Plain p = Plain()
Console.log(p.TraitA.Who())
Console.log(p.TraitB.Who())
&TraitA viewA = p
Console.log(viewA.Who())
Console.log(viewA.tag)

// the trait constructor runs before the body of the class
Order o = Order()
Console.log(o.value)
Console.log(o.made)
// Private state in traits, sub-traits, asparent in traits, executeBefore / executeAfter and the counter of Collection (spec 11.2, 6.4)
import Root

// a private member of a trait is seen only by the code of the trait
trait Counter {
  i32 hits
  fun bump() { hits += 1 }
  public fun i32 count() {
    bump()
    return hits
  }
}

// the same private name in another trait: the compiler keeps them apart
trait Tagged {
  string hits = "tag"
  public fun string tag() { hits }
}

// a sub-trait reads the private members of its parent and calls its private functions
trait LoudCounter : Counter {
  public fun i32 twice() {
    bump()
    bump()
    return hits
  }
}

// the constructor of a trait sets its private fields
trait Started {
  i32 start
  Started() { start = 40 }
  public fun i32 begin() { start + 2 }
}

public class Thing : LoudCounter, Tagged, Started {
  public i32 hits = 7    // a field of the class: the private fields of the traits are not seen here
}

Thing t = Thing()
Console.log($"{t.count()} {t.twice()} {t.count()} {t.tag()} {t.begin()} {t.hits}")

// asparent in a trait: the children of the object call it through their parent reference
trait Visited {
  public i32 visits
  asparent fun ping() { visits += 1 }
}

public class Home : Visited { }

public class Guest(Home home as parent) {
  public fun visit() { home.ping() }
}

Home home = Home()
Guest guest = Guest(home)
guest.visit()
guest.visit()
Console.log($"visits {home.visits}")

// executeBefore and executeAfter: they run around the implementation of the class, on every call
trait Logged {
  public fun i32 work(i32 n)
    executeBefore {
      Console.log($"before {n}")
    }
    executeAfter {
      Console.log($"after {n} -> {work.result}")
    }
}

public class NegativeWork : Exception { }

public class Worker : Logged {
  replace fun i32 work(i32 n) {
    if n < 0 {
      throw NegativeWork("negative")
    }
    return n * 2
  }
}

// a call of the parent implementation does not run the blocks again
public class Lazy : Worker {
  replace fun i32 work(i32 n) { parent.work(n) + 1 }
}

fun ->string attempt(&Worker job, i32 n) {
  return $"result {job.work(n)}"
  catch NegativeWork { return "failed: no executeAfter" }
}

Worker worker = Worker()
Console.log(worker.work(3))
Console.log(attempt(&worker, 4))
Console.log(attempt(&worker, -1))
Lazy lazy = Lazy()
Console.log(lazy.work(5))

// a custom collection gets the counter of open iterators from Collection: getIterator() adds one (executeAfter of IndexCollection),
// releaseIterator() takes one back (executeAfter of Collection), whatever the class writes
public class Bag : IndexCollection<i32> {
  public arr<i32> items = [10, 20, 30]

  replace fun uns get_len() { items.len }
  replace fun bool has(i32 value) { items.has(value) }
  replace fun clear() { items.clear() }

  replace fun ->IndexIterator<i32> getIterator() {
    BagIterator iterator = BagIterator(this)
    return ->iterator
  }

  replace fun releaseIterator() {
    Console.log("released")
  }

  public fun string releaseAgain() {
    releaseIterator()
    return "released by hand"
    catch InvalidIteratorReleaseException { return "nothing to release" }
  }
}

public class BagIterator(Bag collection as parent) : IndexIterator<i32> {
  uns cursor = 0

  replace fun (i32 value, bool isValid) next() {
    i32 none = 0
    if cursor >= collection.len {
      return (none, false)
    }
    i32 item = collection.items[cursor]
    cursor += 1
    return (item, true)
  }
} atEnd {
  if collection != null {
    collection.releaseIterator()
  }
}

Bag bag = Bag()
Console.log(bag.releaseAgain())
fun walk(Bag source) {
  var iterator = source.getIterator()
  var got = iterator.next()
  Console.log($"first {got.value}")
}
walk(bag)
Console.log(bag.releaseAgain())
for i32 x in bag {
  Console.print($"{x};")
}
Console.log("")
Console.log(bag.releaseAgain())

// a break ends the iterator of the loop too
for i32 x in bag {
  if x == 20 {
    break
  }
}
Console.log(bag.releaseAgain())
// tryClone() (spec 12.6): the clone of the class of the object at run time, or ObjectNotClonableException (not silent) when that class
// cannot be cloned; the result is then null. It works on trait values, where clone() and a! are a compile error
import Root

public trait Shape { }
public class Square(i32 side) : Shape {
  public i32 side
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  end square {side}")
}
public class Dog {
  public string name = "dog"
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
public notclonable class Guard : Dog { }
public class Named(string name) {
  public string name
  public fun ->Named clone() { Named(name + " (own clone)") }
}

fun ->string copyName(Dog d) {
  Dog c = d.tryClone()
  return "copy of " + c.name
  catch ObjectNotClonableException { return "not clonable" }
}
fun i32 copySide(Shape s) {
  Shape c = s.tryClone()
  if c is Square sq {
    return sq.side
  }
  return -1
}
fun run() {
  Dog rex = Dog()
  rex.name = "rex"
  Console.log(copyName(rex))
  Guard g = Guard()
  g.name = "guard"
  Console.log(copyName(g))
  Square sq = Square(4)
  Console.log($"side {copySide(sq)}")
  Named n = Named("n")
  Named m = n.tryClone()
  Console.log(m.name)
  Dog none = null
  Dog other = none.tryClone()
  Console.log($"null gives null {other == null}")
}
run()
Console.log("done")
namespace tryparse
import Root

// tryParse (spec 9)
i32 count
bool ok = i32.tryParse("42", &count)
Console.log($"i32 '42': {ok}, {count}")                             // true, 42
ok = i32.tryParse("abc", &count)
Console.log($"i32 'abc': {ok}, unchanged {count}")                   // false, 42
ok = i32.tryParse("  -17 apples", &count)
Console.log($"i32 '  -17 apples': {ok}, {count}")                    // true, -17
ok = i32.tryParse("99999999999", &count)
Console.log($"i32 out of range: {ok}, unchanged {count}")            // false, -17
u8 small
ok = u8.tryParse("-5", &small)
Console.log($"u8 '-5': {ok}, {small}")                                // false, 0
ok = u8.tryParse("200", &small)
Console.log($"u8 '200': {ok}, {small}")                               // true, 200
r64 amount
ok = r64.tryParse("12.3", &amount)
Console.log($"r64 '12.3': {ok}, {amount}")                            // true, 12.3
ok = r64.tryParse("1.5e3", &amount)
Console.log($"r64 '1.5e3': {ok}, {amount}")                           // true, 1500
ok = r64.tryParse("", &amount)
Console.log($"r64 '': {ok}, {amount}")                                // false, 1500
i32 code
ok = i32.tryParse("0x2A", &code, "0x{bh}")
Console.log($"with a format: {ok}, {code}")                           // true, 42
ok = i32.tryParse("0x2A", &code, format: "{S}")
Console.log($"format that does not match: {ok}, {code}")              // false, 42

// dates and times
date when
ok = date.tryParse("2026-10-17 14:30", &when)
Console.log($"date: {ok}, {when}")                                    // true, 2026-10-17T14:30:00.000Z
ok = date.tryParse("not a date", &when)
Console.log($"bad date: {ok}, unchanged {when}")                       // false, same date
time clockTime
ok = time.tryParse("13:45", &clockTime)
Console.log($"time: {ok}, {clockTime}")                                // true
ok = time.tryParse("25:00", &clockTime)
Console.log($"bad time: {ok}")                                         // false

// the holes of an interpolated string run left to right
fun i32 counter(string label) {
  Console.print(label)
  return 1
}
string order = $"{counter("a")}{counter("b")}{counter("c")}"
Console.log("")
Console.log(order)                                                     // abc then 111

// errors it should give (one at a time):
// i64.tryParse("7", &code)          i64.tryParse needs a i64 variable, not i32
// i32.tryParse("7", code)             the result of i32.tryParse is passed as &variable
// Tuple items by index (spec 3.2): the index is a literal, a const or an enum value; reads and assignments;
// indexes apply left to right
import Root

const i32 SECOND = 1
enum Slot { First, Second, Third }

fun (i32 sum, i32 count) stats(vec<i32> nums) {
  i32 s = 0
  for n in nums {
    s += n
  }
  return (s, (i32)nums.len)
}

var st = stats([1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13])
i32 total = st[0]
i32 n = st[1]
Console.log($"{total} {n}")

(i32, string, r64) t = (7, "seven", 7.5)
const i32 LAST = 2
Console.log(t[0])
Console.log(t[SECOND])
Console.log(t[LAST])
Console.log(t[Slot.Second])

t[0] = 70
t[SECOND] = "seventy"
t[2] += 0.25
Console.log(t)

arr<(i32, string)> pairs = [(1, "one"), (2, "two"), (3, "three")]
Console.log(pairs[2][1])
((i32, i32), string) nested = ((4, 5), "n")
Console.log(nested[0][1])
// collections of tuples that hold objects or arrays (spec 3.2, 6.6): the collection owns the parts of its tuples; a copy of an item
// clones them, a replaced or removed item ends them, and they end with the collection
namespace tupleitems
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name = name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
fun literals() {
  list<(Dog, i32)> l = [(Dog("l1"), 1), (Dog("l2"), 2)]
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("v1"), 1)]
  arr<(Dog, i32)> a = [(Dog("a1"), 1), (Dog("a2"), 2)]
  Console.log($"{l[0].0.name} {v[0].0.name} {a[1].0.name} {l.len}")
  Console.log("end of literals")
}
literals()
fun writes() {
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("w1"), 1)]
  v[0] = (Dog("w2"), 2)
  (Dog, i32) t = (Dog("w3"), 3)
  v[0] = t
  t.0.name = "W3"
  Console.log($"{v[0].0.name} {t.0.name}")
  Console.log("end of writes")
}
writes()
fun arrays() {
  vec<(arr<i32>, i32)> pairs = [([1, 2], 1)]
  arr<i32> x = [7, 8, 9]
  pairs[0] = (x, 2)
  x[0] = 70
  Console.log($"{pairs[0].0} {x}")
}
arrays()
fun removals() {
  list<(Dog, i32)> l = [(Dog("r1"), 1), (Dog("r2"), 2), (Dog("r3"), 3)]
  (Dog, i32) t = l.takeAt(1)
  Console.log($"took {t.0.name} {l.len}")
  l.removeAt(0)
  Console.log(l.len)
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("p1"), 1), (Dog("p2"), 2)]
  bool got = v.pop(&t)
  Console.log($"popped {t.0.name}")
  v.clear()
  Console.log("end of removals")
}
removals()
fun whole() {
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("c1"), 3), (Dog("c2"), 1)]
  vec<(Dog, i32)> c = v.clone()
  c[0].0.name = "C1"
  Console.log($"{v[0].0.name} {c[0].0.name}")
  v.sort(((Dog, i32) x, (Dog, i32) y) { x.1 - y.1 })
  Console.log(v[0].0.name)
  vec<(Dog, i32)> big = v.filter(((Dog, i32) x) { x.1 > 2 })
  Console.log(big.len)
  for var item in v {
    Console.log(item.0.name)
  }
  Console.log(v)
  Console.log("end of whole")
}
whole()
fun parts() {
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("f1"), 1), (Dog("f2"), 2)]
  Console.log($"{v[0].0.name} {v[0].1}")
  (Dog, i32) t = ->v[1]
  Console.log($"moved {t.0.name}")
  list<(Dog, i32)> l = [(Dog("g1"), 1)]
  (Dog, i32) old = l.replace(0, (Dog("g2"), 2))
  Console.log($"replaced {old.0.name} {l[0].0.name}")
  Console.log("end of parts")
}
parts()
// Tuples with objects of Keyable classes as keys of map, hmap, set, hset (spec 6.2, 6.8.4.1, 6.8.5.1): the collection keeps a clone of the
// key, the new objects of a key given to it end with the statement, a borrowed one stays with its owner; an hmap compares with equals.
// A tuple literal lent to a call: its new parts end with the statement (spec 3.2). Map values that are tuples with objects or arrays

import Root
class Dog(string name) : Keyable {
  public string name
  replace fun uns get_hash() { 7u }                 // every dog has the same hash: equals decides in the hmap
  replace fun bool equals(T other) {
    if T is Dog {
      return name == other.name
    }
    return false
  }
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  {name} ends")
}
class Cat(string name) : Keyable {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  cat {name} ends")
}
fun show((Dog, i32) t) { Console.log($"show {t.0.name} {t.1}") }
fun keys() {
  map<(Dog, i32), string> m = []
  m[(Dog("A"), 1)] = "a1"
  m[(Dog("B"), 2)] = "b2"
  Dog a = Dog("A")
  m[(a, 3)] = "a3"
  Console.log($"{m.len} {m[(Dog("A"), 1)]} {m.hasKey((a, 3))} {m.hasKey((a, 9))}")
  for var (k, v) in m {
    Console.log($"{k.0.name} {k.1} {v}")
  }
  m.removeAt((Dog("B"), 2))
  Console.log($"after remove {m.len} {a.name}")
  hmap<(string, Dog), i32> h = []
  h[("x", Dog("H"))] = 1
  h[("x", Dog("H"))] = 2
  Console.log($"{h.len} {h[("x", Dog("H"))]}")
  hset<(Cat, i32)> s = []
  Cat c = Cat("C")
  s.add((c, 1))
  Console.log($"{s.len} {s.has((c, 1))}")
  set<(i32, Dog)> ordered = [(1, Dog("S1")), (0, Dog("S0"))]
  Console.log($"{ordered}")
  Console.log("end of keys")
}
fun lent() {
  Dog rex = Dog("Rex")
  show((Dog("New"), 1))
  show((rex, 2))
  Console.log($"end of lent {rex.name}")
}
fun values() {
  map<i32, (Dog, i32)> m = [1: (Dog("V1"), 1)]
  m[2] = (Dog("V2"), 2)
  m[1] = (Dog("V3"), 3)
  (Dog, i32) taken = ->m[2]
  Console.log($"{m} {taken.0.name}")
  hmap<string, (arr<i32>, i32)> h = []
  h["a"] = ([1, 2], 2)
  Console.log($"{h["a"].0[1]}")
  Console.log("end of values")
}
keys()
lent()
values()
Console.log("done")
// Objects in tuples (spec 3.2, as in structs 11.1.5.3): the tuple owns them, a copy clones them, a new tuple is taken as it is, they end with the tuple
namespace tupleobjects
import Root
class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"{name} ends")
}
class Box {
  public (Dog d, i32 n) pair = (Dog("InBox"), 1)
}
fun ->(Dog d, i32 n) make() { (Dog("Made"), 2) }
fun f() {
  (Dog d, i32 n) t = (Dog("A"), 1)
  Dog rex = Dog("Rex")
  (Dog d, i32 n) copy = (rex, 5)
  Console.log($"{t.d.name} {copy.d.name} {rex.name}")
  t = (Dog("B"), 2)
  Console.log("replaced")
  var b = Box()
  var c = b!
  Console.log($"{c.pair.d.name}")
  var m = make()
  Console.log($"{m.d.name}")
  make()
  Console.log("end of f")
}
f()
// One part of a tuple item written in place (spec 3.2): arr, vec, list items and map, hmap values; a replaced object or array ends.
// Nested tuple items by number: t.0.1 is item 1 of item 0, never the real 0.1 (spec 3.2)
import Root
class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  {name} ends")
}
struct P { i32 x, i32 y }
fun items() {
  arr<(i32 n, string s)> a = [(1, "a"), (2, "b")]
  a[0].s = "z"
  a[1].n *= 4
  list<(i32, string)> l = [(1, "a")]
  l[0].1 = "q"
  l[0].0++
  map<string, (i32, string)> m = ["k": (1, "a")]
  m["k"].0 = 9
  m["k"].1 = "w"
  hmap<i32, (i32, i32)> h = [1: (1, 1)]
  h[1].1 += 5
  Console.log($"{a} {l} {m} {h}")
}
fun owned() {
  vec<(Dog, i32)> v = [(Dog("A"), 1)]
  v[0].0 = Dog("B")
  v[0].1 = 7
  Dog c = Dog("C")
  v[0].0 = c!
  map<i32, (Dog, i32)> m = [1: (Dog("M"), 1)]
  m[1].0 = Dog("N")
  m[1].0.name = "NN"
  vec<(arr<i32>, i32)> arrays = [([1, 2], 1)]
  arrays[0].0 = [5, 6, 7]
  arrays[0].0[1] = 9
  Console.log($"{v} {m} {arrays}")
  Console.log("end of owned")
}
fun nested() {
  ((i32, i32), i32) t = ((1, 2), 3)
  t.0.1 = 7
  t.0.0 += 10
  vec<((i32, i32), i32)> n = [((1, 2), 3)]
  n[0].0.1 = 8
  (((i32, i32), i32), i32) d = (((1, 2), 3), 4)
  d.0.0.1 = 9
  vec<(P, i32)> s = [(P(1, 2), 1)]
  s[0].0.x = 5
  Console.log($"{t} {t.0.1} {n[0].0.1} {d.0.0.1} {s} {1.5 + 0.25}")
}
items()
owned()
nested()
namespace tuples
import Root

fun i32, string getUser() { return 1, "ada" }

fun i32, string forward() { return getUser() }

fun (i32 id, string name) getPair() { return (2, "bob") }

fun i64, r64, bool stats(i32 count) {
  return count * 2, (r64)count / 2.0, count > 3
}

// multiple return values
i32 id, string name = getUser()
Console.log($"getUser: {id} {name}")
i32 otherId, _ = forward()
Console.log($"forward id: {otherId}")
i64 doubled, r64 halved, bool big = stats(5)
Console.log($"stats(5): {doubled} {halved} {big}")
id, name = getUser()
_, name, i32 level = 7, "carl", 3
Console.log($"name = {name}, level = {level}")

// swap: the right side is evaluated first
i32 x = 1
i32 y = 2
x, y = y, x
Console.log($"after swap: x = {x}, y = {y}")

// tuples
(i32 id, string name) pair = getPair()
Console.log($"pair = {pair}, pair.id = {pair.id}, pair.name = {pair.name}, pair.1 = {pair.1}")
pair.id = 20
pair.name += "!"
Console.log($"changed pair = {pair}")
(i32, string) plain = pair
Console.log($"plain = {plain}, equal: {plain == pair}")
(i64, r64) widened = (3, 1.5)
Console.log($"widened = {widened}")
(i32 count, (bool flag, char letter) inner) nested = (1, (true, 'z'))
Console.log($"nested = {nested}, nested.inner.letter = {nested.inner.letter}")
(i32 a, i32 b) zero
Console.log($"zero = {zero}")
bool literalEqual = (1, "a") == (1, "a")
bool different = (1, 2) != (1, 3)
Console.log($"literal equal: {literalEqual}, different: {different}")

// nullable tuple
(i32, i32)? maybePair
Console.log($"maybePair = {maybePair}")
maybePair = (4, 5)
Console.log($"maybePair = {maybePair}, first = {maybePair?.0}")
// tuples that hold structs with strings or objects (spec 3.2, 11.1.5.3): the tuple owns the struct as it owns an object; a copy of
// the tuple clones it, a new one is taken as it is, and it ends with the tuple
namespace tuplestructs
import Root
public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name = name
} atEnd {
  Console.log("  end " + name)
}
struct Pet { Dog dog, string tag, i32 age }
fun ->(Pet, i32) make(string name) { (Pet(Dog(name), "made", 1), 7) }
fun Pet, i32 two(string name) {
  return Pet(Dog(name), "two", 2), 9
}
fun locals() {
  (Pet, i32) a = (Pet(Dog("a1"), "x", 1), 1)
  Pet p = Pet(Dog("p1"), "y", 2)
  (Pet, i32) b = (p, 2)
  p.dog.name = "P1"
  Console.log($"{a.0.dog.name} {b.0.dog.name} {p.dog.name} {b.0.tag}")
  a = b
  Console.log($"{a.0.dog.name} {a.1}")
  (Pet, i32) c = make("m1")
  Console.log($"{c.0.dog.name} {c.1}")
  Pet q, i32 n = two("t1")
  Console.log($"{q.dog.name} {n}")
  Console.log("end of locals")
}
locals()
fun items() {
  vec<(Pet, i32)> v = [(Pet(Dog("v1"), "a", 1), 1)]
  (Pet, i32) t = (Pet(Dog("v2"), "b", 2), 2)
  v.add(t)
  t.0.dog.name = "V2"
  v[0] = (Pet(Dog("v3"), "c", 3), 3)
  map<string, (Pet, i32)> m = ["k": (Pet(Dog("k1"), "d", 4), 4)]
  m["k"] = (Pet(Dog("k2"), "e", 5), 5)
  Console.log($"{v[1].0.dog.name} {t.0.dog.name} {v[0].0.tag} {m["k"].0.dog.name}")
  Console.log("end of items")
}
items()
fun unkept() {
  make("u1")
  two("u2")
  Console.log(make("u3").0.dog.name)
  Pet q, i32 n = two("u4")
  q, n = two("u5")
  Console.log($"{q.dog.name} {n}")
  Console.log("end of unkept")
}
unkept()
fun fromvalues() {
  Pet q = Pet(Dog("w1"), "a", 1)
  Pet p = Pet(Dog("w2"), "b", 2)
  i32 n = 0
  p, n = q, 3
  q.dog.name = "W1"
  Console.log($"{p.dog.name} {q.dog.name} {n}")
  p, n = Pet(Dog("w3"), "c", 3), 4
  Console.log($"{p.dog.name} {n}")
  Pet r, i32 k = q, 5
  r.dog.name = "R1"
  Console.log($"{r.dog.name} {q.dog.name} {k}")
  p, q = q, p
  Console.log($"{p.dog.name} {q.dog.name}")
  Console.log("end of fromvalues")
}
fromvalues()
// The type switch of generics (11.3.7): value is ? { type: expression, ... }
import Root

Generic Convertible { Numeric, char, string }

trait Named {
  public fun string Name()
}

public class Dog(string label) : Named {
  replace fun string Name() { label }
}

fun r64 toReal(Convertible item) {
  r64 result = item is ? {
    Numeric: (r64)item,
    char: (r64)(u32)item,
    string: (r64)item.len
  }
  return result
}

fun string kind<Item>(Item value) {
  value is ? {
    i32, i64: "integer",
    Reals: "real",
    Named: "named",
    default: "other"
  }
}

Console.log(toReal(3))
Console.log(toReal(2.5))
Console.log(toReal('A'))
Console.log(toReal("four"))
Console.log(kind(1))
Console.log(kind<i64>(1))
Console.log(kind(1.5))
Console.log(kind(Dog("rex")))
Console.log(kind("text"))
// unrestricted is checked (spec 1.6.2): reading, unrestricted calls on this and on what it owns, read-only collection members and locals are allowed

class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
  public i32 hunger = 5
  unrestricted fun bark() { }
  public fun eat(i32 n) { hunger -= n }
}

  Dog boss = Dog("Rex")
  vec<Dog> dogs = []
  i32 visits
  string label = "k"
  unrestricted fun bool f() { boss.bark(); i32 local = visits; local++; dogs.has(boss) || local == 1 }
}

// &T results of values (spec 13.1.1): the address of the place; &i32 s = f() writes it, i32 x = f() copies, f() = v / += / ++ on the call
namespace valuelends
import Root
class Board {
  public i32 score
  public string title = "board"
  public vec<i32> cells = [0, 0, 0, 0]
  public fun &i32 scoreRef() { score }
  public fun &i32 cell(uns i) { cells[i] }
  public fun &string titleRef() { title }
}
fun f() {
  Board board = Board()
  &i32 s = board.scoreRef()
  s += 10
  Console.log($"score {board.score}")
  i32 copy = board.scoreRef()
  board.scoreRef()++
  board.scoreRef() += 5
  board.cell(3) = 7
  Console.log($"copy {copy}, score {board.score}, cell 3 is {board.cells[3]}")
  &string t = board.titleRef()
  t += "!"
  Console.log(board.title)
  &i32 n = board.score
  n = 99
  Console.log(board.score)
}
struct Vec2 { r64 x, r64 y }
class Ship {
  public Vec2 pos
  public fun &Vec2 posRef() { pos }
}
fun structs() {
  Ship s = Ship()
  &Vec2 p = s.posRef()
  p.x = 3.0
  s.posRef().y += 2.0
  Vec2 copy = s.posRef()
  Console.log($"{s.pos} {copy}")
}
fun counted() {
  &@Board shared = @Board()
  enforce shared {
    &string t = shared.titleRef()
    t += "!"
  }
  enforce shared { Console.log(shared.title) }
}

// Value parameters of generics (11.3.3): constants that are part of the type
import Root

public class Grid<u64 Width, u64 Height, Cell>() {
  public arr<Cell> cells = arr<Cell>[Width * Height]

  unrestricted fun u64 Count() { Width * Height }
  public fun Set(u64 x, u64 y, Cell value) { cells[y * Width + x] = value }
  public fun Cell Get(u64 x, u64 y) { cells[y * Width + x] }
}

public class Flag<bool On>() {
  public fun string Show() {
    if On {
      return "on"
    }
    return "off"
  }
}

public class Wrapper<u64 N>() {
  public fun u64 Twice() { Grid<N, 2, i32>().Count() }
}

public class Offset<i32 By>() {
  public fun i32 Apply(i32 x) { x + By }
}

fun u64 sizeOf<u64 N>(i32 ignored) { N << 2 }

fun u64 gridCount<u64 N>(Grid<N, 3, i32> grid) { grid.Count() + N }

Grid<4, 3, i32> g = Grid<4, 3, i32>()
g.Set(1, 2, 42)
Console.log(g.Get(1, 2))
Console.log(g.Count())
Grid<2 + 2, 3, i32> same = Grid<4, 3, i32>()
Console.log(same.Count())
Grid<2, 2, string> names = Grid<2, 2, string>()
names.Set(0, 1, "x")
Console.log(names.Get(0, 1))
Console.log(Flag<true>().Show())
Console.log(Flag<false>().Show())
Console.log(Wrapper<5>().Twice())
Console.log(sizeOf<3>(0))
Console.log(gridCount(g))
Console.log(Offset<-5>().Apply(7))
Console.log(g)

fun ->IndexIterator<i32> orphan() {
  vec<i32> local = [7, 8]
  var iterator = local.getIterator()
  return ->iterator
}

fun ->string tryNext(&IndexIterator<i32> iterator) {
  var got = iterator.next()
  return $"{got.value}"
  catch UsageOfDestroyedObjectException { return "the vec is gone" }
}

// the iterator by hand: it keeps the vec from changing its size until it ends (spec 6.4), here at the end of the function
fun byHand(vec<i32> source) {
  var iterator = source.getIterator()
  var first = iterator.next()
  var second = iterator.next()
  Console.log($"{first.value} {first.isValid} {second.value} {second.isValid}")
  var more = iterator.next()
  more = iterator.next()
  more = iterator.next()
  more = iterator.next()
  Console.log(more.isValid)
}
byHand(numbers)

// an index outside the vector
fun i32 outside(vec<i32> source, u64 index) {
  return source[index]
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException { return -1 }
}
Console.log(outside(numbers, 1))
Console.log(outside(numbers, 40))
fun bool badRemove(&vec<i32> source) {
  source.removeAt(10)
  return true
  catch IndexOutOfRangeException { return false }
}
Console.log(badRemove(&numbers))
// vec growth policy (spec 6.8.2) and the sized declaration vec<T>[n] name (spec 6.3)
import Root

// 16 items on the first add, then the capacity doubles
vec<i32> numbers = []
Console.log($"start {numbers.len} {numbers.capacity}")
uns last = numbers.capacity
for i32 i in 0..100 {
  numbers.add(i)
  if numbers.capacity != last {
    Console.log($"len {numbers.len} capacity {numbers.capacity}")
    last = numbers.capacity
  }
}

// past 4 MB of block the capacity grows by half: 1M i32 = 4 MB
vec<i32> big = vec<i32>[1048576]
big.fillAtCapacity(1)
Console.log($"big {big.len} {big.capacity}")
big.add(2)
Console.log($"big {big.len} {big.capacity}")

// below the limit it doubles
vec<i64> half = vec<i64>[1000]
half.fillAtCapacity(1)
half.add(2)
Console.log($"half {half.len} {half.capacity}")

// add(collection) follows the same rule
vec<i32> joined = []
arr<i32> three = [1, 2, 3]
joined.add(three)
Console.log($"joined {joined.len} {joined.capacity}")
arr<i32> forty = arr<i32>[40]
joined.add(forty)
Console.log($"joined {joined.len} {joined.capacity}")

// the capacity on the declaration: an empty vec
vec<string>[50] parts
Console.log($"parts {parts.len} {parts.capacity}")
vec<i32>[3] few = []
few.add(1)
few.add(2)
few.add(3)
few.add(4)
Console.log($"few {few} {few.len} {few.capacity}")
uns n = 7
vec<u8>[n * 2] computed
Console.log($"computed {computed.len} {computed.capacity}")
// A vector owns its items (spec 12): adding moves in, removing moves out or ends the item, a plain read borrows.
// A double drop would end the program with "Double free": this test must run clean.
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
}

fun fill(&vec<Dog> kennel, u64 count) {
  u64 i = 0
  while i < count {
    kennel.add(Dog("dog"))
    i += 1
  }
}

vec<Dog> kennel = []
Dog rex = Dog("rex")
kennel.add(->rex)                  // rex is now owned by the vector
kennel.add(Dog("fido"))
kennel.insert(1, Dog("luna"))
Console.log(kennel.len)
Console.log(kennel[1].name)

kennel[1] = Dog("max")             // the old item (luna) ends
Console.log(kennel[1].name)

Dog taken = ->kennel[0]            // moves rex out: the slot is empty
Console.log(taken.name)
Console.log(kennel[0] == null)

Dog last
bool had = kennel.pop(&last)     // fido moves into last
Console.log($"{had} {last.name}")

kennel.removeAt(0)                 // the empty slot goes away
Console.log(kennel.len)
kennel.clear()                     // the items become null: the size is kept
Console.log(kennel.len)
kennel.truncate(0)

fill(&kennel, 1000)
Console.log(kennel.len)
kennel.truncate(10)
Console.log(kennel.len)
kennel.clear()

// vectors of vectors, and a vector kept in a field
vec<vec<i32>> grid = []
vec<i32> row = [1, 2, 3]
grid.add(->row)
grid.add([4, 5])
Console.log(grid[1][0])
Console.log(grid.len)

}

// Vectors aligned with the spec 0.0.13: Collection<Item>, a change during an iteration, fill(null), swap, ->
import Root

public class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
}

// a vec is an IndexCollection<T>, which extends Collection<T>: it can be given to a function of those types
fun u64 sizeOf(Collection<i32> items) { items.len }
fun bool contains(Collection<string> items, string text) { items.has(text) }
vec<i32> numbers = [3, 1, 2]
vec<string> words = ["a", "b"]
Console.log(sizeOf(numbers))
Console.log(contains(words, "b"))
Console.log(contains(words, "z"))
&IndexCollection<i32> indexed = numbers   // a read/write borrow: clear changes it
Console.log(indexed.len)
indexed.clear()
Console.log(numbers)             // clear keeps the size (spec 6.6)

// changing the size or the order of a vec while it is iterated raises ModifyingIteratedCollectionException (a Panic in a release build);
// replacing an item is allowed (spec 6.4)
fun string addWhileLooping(&vec<i32> source) {
  for i32 n in source {
    source.add(n)
  }
  return "no exception"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "changed while iterated" }
}
fun string replaceWhileLooping(&vec<i32> source) {
  for i32 n in source {
    source[0] = 9
  }
  return "an item can be replaced while iterated"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "never" }
}
fun ->string sortWhileLooping(&vec<i32> source) {
  for i32 n in source {
    source.reverse()
  }
  return "no exception"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException e { return "reordered: " + e.message }
}
fun ->string readOnly(vec<i32> source) {
  i32 total = 0
  for i32 n in source {
    total += n
  }
  return $"sum {total}"
  catch ModifyingIteratedCollectionException { return "never" }
}
vec<i32> values = [1, 2, 3]
Console.log(readOnly(values))
Console.log(addWhileLooping(&values))
Console.log(replaceWhileLooping(&values))
Console.log(sortWhileLooping(&values))
Console.log(readOnly(values))

// swap exchanges two positions; replace puts a new item and gives back the old one
vec<string> letters = ["x", "y", "z"]
letters.swap(0, 2)
Console.log(letters)
string was = letters.replace(1, "Y")
Console.log($"{was} {letters}")

// objects: ->, fill(null) and resize with null end the objects the items held
vec<Dog> kennel = [Dog("rex"), Dog("fido")]
Dog extra = Dog("luna")
kennel.add(->extra)
kennel[0] = Dog("max")
Dog spare = Dog("spare")
kennel[1] = ->spare
Console.log($"{kennel[0].name} {kennel[1].name} {kennel[2].name}")
kennel.fill(null)
Console.log($"{kennel.len} {kennel[0] == null} {kennel[2] == null}")
kennel.resize(5, null)
Console.log(kennel.len)
kennel.clear()
// Weak references (spec 12.7): only on counted objects, made with %x; fields, header parameters, parameters, variables; enforce gives a
// temporary usage; the holder does not keep the object alive
namespace weak
import Root

class Context {
  public i32 id = 7
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"context {id} ends")
}

class Handle(%Context context) {
  public %Context other
  public fun i32 read() {
    enforce context { return context.id }
    return -1
  }
  public fun link(%Context c) { other = c }
  public fun bool hasOther() {
    enforce other { return true }
    return false
  }
}

fun string probe(%Context c) {
  enforce c { return "alive" }
  return "gone"
}

@Context ctx = Context()
var h = Handle(%ctx)
Console.log(h.read())
Console.log(h.hasOther())
h.link(%ctx)
Console.log(h.hasOther())
Console.log($"weak references {ctx.weakrefsCount}")
%Context w
Console.log(probe(%ctx))
w = %ctx
enforce w {
  Console.log($"{w.id}, users inside enforce {ctx.usersCount}")
} else {
  Console.log("none")
}
Console.log($"users after enforce {ctx.usersCount}")
%Context copy = w                           // a weak reference copied into another
Console.log($"weak references {ctx.weakrefsCount}")
enforce copy { Console.log("copy ok") } else { Console.log("copy none") }
ctx is done.                                // the last user: the object ends, the weak references see it gone
Console.log(h.read())
Console.log(h.hasOther())
enforce w { Console.log(w.id) } else { Console.log("w gone") }
Console.log(probe(%copy))
copy is done.
Console.log(h)

// a weak field does not end with its holder
Context plain8 = Context()
plain8.id = 8                                // set before it is counted: a @T is read-only (spec 12.7)
@Context ctx2 = @plain8
var h2 = Handle(%ctx2)
h2 is atEnd.
Console.log($"{ctx2.id}, weak references {ctx2.weakrefsCount}")
Console.log(probe(%ctx2))

// the temporary usage of enforce keeps the object alive while the block runs, and is released on every way out
fun i32 early(%Context c) {
  enforce c {
    if c.id == 8 { return 1 }
    return 2
  }
  return 0
}
Console.log(early(%ctx2))
Console.log($"users after early {ctx2.usersCount}")
// a weak header parameter redeclared as public readonly keeps the value given to the constructor (spec 12.7); an addr prints as hex (spec 2.3)
namespace weakheader
import Root

class C {
  public i32 id = 9
}

class H(%C c) {
  public readonly %C c
  public fun i32 read() {
    enforce c { return c.id }
    return -1
  }
}

@C ctx = C()
var h = H(%ctx)
Console.log(h.read())
addr a = null
Console.log(a)
// for &T x in c (spec 6.4): a read/write borrow of each item of arr, vec, list and of each value of a map, written in place; an object
// or a tuple assigned through it replaces the item (the old one ends). & on a part of for (a, b) in c: the part is written in the item
import Root
class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  {name} ends")
}
struct P { i32 x, i32 y }
fun f() {
  vec<i32> v = [1, 2]
  for &i32 x in v { x += 10 }
  arr<r64> a = [1.5, 2.5]
  for &r64 y in a { y *= 2.0 }
  list<i32> l = [7, 8]
  for &i32 z in l { z = 0 }
  vec<Dog> d = [Dog("a"), Dog("b")]
  for &Dog g in d { g.name = g.name + "!" }
  for &Dog g in d { g = Dog("new") }
  vec<P> ps = [P(1, 2)]
  for &P p in ps { p.x = 9 }
  vec<(Dog, i32)> t = [(Dog("t"), 1)]
  for &(Dog, i32) e in t { e.1 = 5 }
  for &(Dog, i32) e in t { e = (Dog("t2"), 6) }
  map<string, i32> ages = ["a": 1, "b": 2]
  for (string k, &i32 n) in ages { n += 100 }
  hmap<i32, Dog> hd = [1: Dog("h")]
  for (var k, &Dog hv) in hd { hv.name = "hh" }
  Console.log($"{v} {a} {l} {d} {ps} {t} {ages} {hd}")
  Console.log("end of f")
}

fun g() {
  vec<(i32, string)> v = [(1, "a"), (2, "b")]
  for (&i32 n, string s) in v { n *= 3 }
  Console.log($"{v}")
  vec<P> ps = [P(1, 2), P(3, 4)]
  for &i32 x, i32 y in ps { x = y }
  Console.log($"{ps}")
  list<(Dog, i32)> dogs = [(Dog("a"), 1)]
  for (&Dog d, &i32 n) in dogs { d.name = "z"; n = 42 }
  Console.log($"{dogs}")
  for (&Dog d, i32 n) in dogs { d = Dog("w") }
  arr<(r64, bool)> a = [(1.0, false)]
  for (r64 q, &bool b) in a { b = q > 0.5 }
  Console.log($"{dogs} {a}")
  Console.log("end of f")
}
map<string, i32> totals = ["a": 1]
for &i32 x in totals.values { x += 5 }
Console.log($"{totals}")
f()
g()
Console.log("done")
// for &T x on string, string?, function and struct-with-objects items (spec 6.4): written in place, the old value ends; & on the string
// and object fields of a struct item
import Root
class Dog(string name) {
  public string name
} atEnd {
  Console.log($"  {name} ends")
}
struct Pet { string name, Dog dog }
fun f() {
  vec<string> v = ["a", "b"]
  string kept = "keep"
  for &string s in v { s = s + "!" }
  for &string s in v { s.append("?") }
  list<string> l = ["x"]
  for &string s in l { s = kept }
  kept = "changed"
  arr<string?> o = ["p", null]
  for &string? s in o {
    if s == null { s = "was null" } else { s = null }
  }
  vec<fun i32(i32)> fs = [(i32 x) { x + 1 }]
  for &fun i32(i32) g in fs { g = (i32 x) { x * 10 } }
  vec<Pet> pets = [Pet("p1", Dog("d1"))]
  for &Pet p in pets { p.name = "p2" }
  for &Pet p in pets { p = Pet("p3", Dog("d3")) }
  for &string n, &Dog d in pets { n = n + "+"; d = Dog("d4") }
  Console.log($"{v} {l} {o} {fs[0](3)} {pets}")
  Console.log("end of f")
}
f()
Console.log("done")
